  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hrímfaxi, \"surai berembun beku\", adalah kuda Nótt (Malam) dalam mitologi Nordik; buih dari kekang mulutnya jatuh ke bumi menjadi embun.",
    "en": "Hrímfaxi, 'frost mane', is the horse of Nótt (Night) in Norse mythology; froth from its bit falls to earth as dew.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Hrímfaxi adalah kuda Nótt, sang Malam, sedangkan Skinfaxi kuda Dagr, sang Siang. Namanya berarti \"surai berembun beku\", dan buih yang jatuh dari kekang mulutnya diyakini menjadi embun di bumi. Dalam Vafþrúðnismál, Odin menyebut Hrímfaxi sebagai kuda yang membawa malam di atas para dewa dan menjatuhkan tetes buih setiap pagi, yang menjadi embun di lembah-lembah.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Hrímfaxi is the horse of Nótt, the Night, while Skinfaxi is that of Dagr, the Day. Its name means 'frost mane', and the froth falling from its bit is believed to become dew on earth. In the Vafþrúðnismál Odin names Hrímfaxi as the horse that draws night over the gods and lets fall drops of foam each morning, which become dew in the valleys.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hrimfaxi-c01",
        "hrimfaxi-c03",
        "hrimfaxi-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam tradisi Denmark, Hrymfaxe atau Rimfaxe adalah kuda yang ditambatkan pada kereta bulan dan menariknya melintasi langit pada malam hari; namanya dijelaskan sebagai kuda bersurai berselimut embun beku yang menarik kereta Malam. Penyair Oehlenschläger menyebut kuda-kuda langit ini dalam puisinya Guldhornene, dan lambang kotamadya Faxe menampilkan gambar bergaya Hrymfaxe atau Skinfaxe sebagai permainan kata nama kota itu.",
      "en": "In Danish tradition Hrymfaxe or Rimfaxe is the horse harnessed to the moon's chariot, drawing it across the sky by night; the name is explained as the rime-maned horse that pulls Night's chariot.       "source_id": "hrimfaxi-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, Skinfaxi (Old Norse: [ˈskinˌfɑkse] ) and Hrímfaxi [ˈhriːmˌfɑkse] are the horses of Dagr (day) and Nótt (night). The names Skinfaxi and Hrímfaxi mean \"shining mane\" and \"frost mane\", respectively. ... \"Froth\" is believed to fall from Hrímfaxi's bit to the earth and become dew.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Skinfaxi dan Hrímfaxi adalah kuda Dagr (siang) dan Nótt (malam); namanya berarti \"surai bersinar\" dan \"surai berembun beku\", dan buih dari kekang Hrímfaxi diyakini jatuh ke bumi menjadi embun.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology Skinfaxi and Hrímfaxi are the horses of Dagr (day) and Nótt (night); the names mean 'shining mane' and 'frost mane', and froth from Hrímfaxi's bit is believed to fall to earth as dew."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hrimfaxi-c02",
      "source_id": "hrimfaxi-s2",
      "quote": "Hrymfaxe eller Rimfaxe er i nordisk mytologi hesten, der er forspændt månevognen. Den trækker månen over himlen om natten. Skummet fra hestens bidsel falder som dug.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik versi Denmark, Hrymfaxe atau Rimfaxe adalah kuda yang ditambatkan pada kereta bulan, menarik bulan melintasi langit di malam hari, dan buih dari kekangnya jatuh sebagai embun.",
        "en": "In the Danish account Hrymfaxe or Rimfaxe is the horse harnessed to the moon's chariot, drawing the moon across the night sky, its bit's foam falling as dew."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hrimfaxi-c03",
      "source_id": "hrimfaxi-s3",
      "quote": "Rimfakse (Norrønt:Hrímfaxi, rim i manen) er i norrøn mytologi den hesten Natt rir på over himmelen hver natt. Morgenduggen kom dryppende fra Rimfakses bissel. Rimfakse nevnes i Den eldre Edda, der Odin sier følgende i Vavtrudnesmål:",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rimfakse (Nordik Kuno Hrímfaxi, \"embun beku di surai\") adalah kuda yang ditunggangi Natt melintasi langit setiap malam; embun pagi menetes dari kekangnya, dan ia disebut Odin dalam Vafþrúðnismál di Edda Puisi.",
        "en": "Rimfakse (Old Norse Hrímfaxi, 'rime in the mane') is the horse Night rides across the sky every night; morning dew drips from its bit, and Odin names it in the Vafþrúðnismál of the Elder Edda."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hrimfaxi-c04",
      "source_id": "hrimfaxi-s2",
      "quote": "Oehlenschlägers digt Guldhornene omtaler himmelhestene: ... Faxes kommunevåben forestiller en stilliseret tegning af Hrymfaxe hhv. Skinfaxe og repræsenterer et ordspil på byens/kommunens navn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puisi Oehlenschläger Guldhornene menyebut kuda-kuda langit, dan lambang kotamadya Faxe menampilkan gambar bergaya Hrymfaxe atau Skinfaxe sebagai permainan kata nama kota itu.",
        "en": "Oehlenschläger's poem Guldhornene mentions the sky horses, and the arms of Faxe Municipality show a stylised Hrymfaxe or Skinfaxe as a pun on the town's name."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hrimfaxi-c05",
      "source_id": "hrimfaxi-s3",
      "quote": "Rimfakse heiter hesten som dreg natt over gjæve gudar; skumdropar han slepper kvar morgon; av det kjem dogg i dalar.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bait Vafþrúðnismál: \"Rimfakse nama kuda yang menarik malam di atas para dewa yang mulia; tetes buih ia jatuhkan setiap pagi; dari situ datang embun di lembah-lembah.\"",
        "en": "The Vafþrúðnismál stanza: 'Rimfakse is the horse that draws night over the noble gods; drops of foam he lets fall each morning; from them comes dew in the dales.'"
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hrimfaxi-c06",
      "source_id": "hrimfaxi-s2",
      "quote": "Hrymfaxe betyder hesten med rimdækket manke som trækker Nats vogn henover himlen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hrymfaxe berarti kuda bersurai berselimut embun beku yang menarik kereta Malam melintasi langit.",
        "en": "Hrymfaxe means the horse with the rime-covered mane that draws Night's chariot across the sky."
      }
    }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "K'ajk' adalah roh dalam mitologi Armenia dan Georgia, golongan dev yang tinggal di gunung dan gua; namanya berarti \"pemberani\".",
    "en": "The K'ajk' are spirits of Armenian and Georgian mythology, a group of devs living in mountains and caves; their name means 'brave'.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "k-ajk-c01",
      "k-ajk-c02",
      "k-ajk-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "K'aǰk' atau K'ajk' (bahasa Armenia untuk \"pemberani\") adalah roh netral dari golongan dev dalam mitologi Armenia. Mereka tinggal di gunung, gua, atau ngarai dan menjalankan hukuman yang ditetapkan bagi manusia. Yang terkenal antara lain K'ajk' dari Gunung Masis (Ararat), yang merantai Artavazd di sebuah gua setelah ia dikutuk ayahnya yang telah wafat, Raja Artašes.",
      "en": "K'aǰk' or K'ajk' (Armenian for 'brave') are neutral spirits of the dev group in Armenian mythology. They live in mountains, caves or gorges and carry out the punishments decreed for humans. Well known are the K'ajk' of Mount Masis (Ararat), who chained Artavazd in a cave after he had been cursed by his dead father, King Artašes.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "k-ajk-c01",
        "k-ajk-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mereka juga disebut roh angin, badai, dan perang dalam mitologi Armenia dan Georgia. Namanya berasal dari kata Armenia k'aǰ, \"pemberani\", sebuah eufemisme yang mengungkapkan kekuatan yang dikaitkan dengan roh-roh ini. Menurut sebuah legenda Armenia, Nuh dikaruniai sepasang anak lelaki dan perempuan selama banjir besar; ketika Tuhan bertanya apakah ia punya anak lain selain Sem, Ham, dan Yafet, Nuh menyangkal, dan kedua anak itu menjadi tak terlihat, menurunkan bangsa kaji dan peri. Dalam cerita rakyat Armenia, kata ini juga sering dipakai untuk makhluk halus jahat pada umumnya, seperti vishap dan chark.",
      "en": "They are also described as spirits of wind, storms and war in Armenian and Georgian mythology. The name comes from Armenian k'aǰ, 'brave', a euphemism expressing the power ascribed to these spirits. An Armenian legend tells that during the Flood Noah had a boy and a girl; when God asked whether he had other children besides Shem, Ham and Japheth, Noah said no, and the two children became invisible, founding the race of the kaji and peri. In Armenian folklore the word also often stands for evil spirits in general, such as the vishap and chark.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "k-ajk-c03",
        "k-ajk-c04",
        "k-ajk-c05",
        "k-ajk-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "K'aǰk' atau K'ajk' (bahasa Armenia untuk \"pemberani\") adalah roh netral dari golongan dev dalam mitologi Armenia.",
        "en": "K'aǰk' or K'ajk' (Armenian for 'brave') are neutral spirits of the dev group in Armenian mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-ajk-c02",
      "source_id": "k-ajk-s1",
      "quote": "Die K’ajk’ leben in Bergen, Höhlen oder Schluchten und führen die für Menschen festgesetzten Strafen aus. Bekannt wurden unter anderem die K’ajk’ vom Berg „Masis“ (armenisch für Ararat), die den Artavazd in einer Höhle in Ketten legten, nachdem dieser von seinem toten Vater, dem armenischen König Artašes, verflucht worden war.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "K'ajk' tinggal di gunung, gua, atau ngarai dan menjalankan hukuman yang ditetapkan bagi manusia; yang terkenal antara lain K'ajk' dari Gunung Masis (Ararat), yang merantai Artavazd di gua setelah ia dikutuk ayahnya yang telah wafat, Raja Artašes.",
        "en": "The K'ajk' live in mountains, caves or gorges and carry out punishments decreed for people; famous are those of Mount Masis (Ararat), who chained Artavazd in a cave after his dead father, King Artašes, cursed him."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-ajk-c03",
      "source_id": "k-ajk-s2",
      "quote": "Кадж, каджк, или каджи (арм. Քաջք, груз. ქაჯი) — дух ветра, бурь и войны в армянской и грузинской мифологиях.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadzh, kadzhk, atau kaji (Armenia: Քաջք, Georgia: ქაჯი) adalah roh angin, badai, dan perang dalam mitologi Armenia dan Georgia.",
        "en": "Kadzh, kadzhk or kaji (Armenian Քաջք, Georgian ქაჯი) is a spirit of wind, storms and war (дух ветра, бурь и войны) in Armenian and Georgian mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-ajk-c04",
      "source_id": "k-ajk-s2",
      "quote": "Наименование происходит от армянского слова k’aǰ - «храбрый», то есть название духов является эвфемизмом, выражающим могущество, приписываемое этим духам.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Namanya berasal dari kata Armenia k'aǰ, \"pemberani\", sehingga nama roh-roh ini merupakan eufemisme yang mengungkapkan kekuatan yang dikaitkan dengan mereka.",
        "en": "The name comes from Armenian k'aǰ, 'brave' (храбрый), so it is a euphemism expressing the power ascribed to these spirits."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-ajk-c05",
      "source_id": "k-ajk-s2",
      "quote": "Согласно легенде, во время потопа у Ноя родились дети: мальчик и девочка. После того, как ковчег причалил к Арарату, Бог спросил Ноя, имеются ли у него ещё дети, кроме Сима, Хама и Яфета, на что Ной ответил отрицательно. Тогда два ребёнка стали невидимыми. От них и пошёл род каджей и пери.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, selama banjir besar Nuh dikaruniai seorang anak lelaki dan perempuan; setelah bahtera berlabuh di Ararat, Tuhan bertanya apakah ia punya anak lain selain Sem, Ham, dan Yafet, Nuh menyangkal, lalu kedua anak itu menjadi tak terlihat dan menurunkan bangsa kaji dan peri.",
        "en": "By legend, during the Flood Noah had a boy and a girl; after the ark landed on Ararat God asked if he had other children besides Shem, Ham and Japheth, Noah said no, and the two became invisible, founding the race of kaji and peri."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "k-ajk-c06",
      "source_id": "k-ajk-s2",
      "quote": "В армянском фольклоре, слово кадж зачастую обозначает нечисть, будь то вишап, чарк и другие сверхъестественные существа.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Armenia, kata kadzh sering dipakai untuk makhluk halus jahat pada umumnya, entah vishap, chark, atau makhluk gaib lain.",
        "en": "In Armenian folklore the word kadzh often denotes unclean spirits in general (нечисть), whether vishap, chark or other supernatural beings."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Sifat K'ajk'",
        "en": "Nature of the K'ajk'"
      },

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lutzelfrau adalah penyihir dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang memberi apel, kacang, dan prem kering kepada anak-anak pada Hari Santa Lusia, 13 Desember, tetapi juga punya sisi gelap yang mengancam anak nakal.",
    "en": "Lutzelfrau is a witch of German folklore who gives children apples, nuts and dried plums on Saint Lucy's Day, 13 December, but also has a dark side that threatens naughty children.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lutzelfrau-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lutzelfrau, disebut Lutscherl di Austria timur dan Luz, Luzi, atau Luzel di Hutan Bayern, adalah penyihir dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang memberi hadiah, terutama apel, kacang, dan prem kering, kepada anak-anak pada Hari Santa Lusia (13 Desember). Namun ia juga punya sisi \"Lusia gelap\" yang konon dapat membelah perut atau menenggelamkan anak-anak nakal.",
      "en": "Known as Lutscherl in eastern Austria and as Luz, Luzi or Luzel in the Bavarian Forest, the Lutzelfrau is a witch of German folklore. On the feast of Saint Lucy (13 December) she brings children presents, above all apples, nuts and dried plums, yet she also has a 'dark Lucia' side that reputedly disembowels or drowns naughty children.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lutzelfrau-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya jelas dikaitkan dengan Santa Lusia dari Sirakusa; Lucia berarti \"yang bercahaya\". Di Burgenland ia juga disebut Luzia atau Lutzl, dan Lutzelfrau atau Pudlfrau datang pada hari Santa Lusia sambil menggulirkan manisan lewat celah pintu. Tradisi serupa ada di Ceko (Lucie), Slowakia (Lucka), Slovenia, dan Kroasia.",
      "en": "Her name is clearly tied to Saint Lucy of Syracuse; Lucia means 'luminous'. In Burgenland she is also called Luzia or Lutzl, and the Lutzelfrau or Pudlfrau visits on St Lucy's day rolling sweets through the doorway. Similar customs exist in Czechia (Lucie), Slovakia (Lucka), Slovenia and Croatia.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lutzelfrau-c02",
        "lutzelfrau-c03",
        "lutzelfrau-c04",
        "lutzelfrau-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut sumber pertengahan abad ke-19, Lusia di Bohemia dan Tirol tampil sebagai kambing yang berselubung seprai dengan tanduk mencuat, mirip tradisi di Skandinavia. Ia juga konon terbang di malam hari dengan sapunya.",
      "en": "A source from the mid-1800s says that in Bohemia and Tyrol Lucia took the shape of a goat covered by a bedsheet, its horns sticking out, much as in Scandinavia. She is also said to fly by night on her broom.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lutzelfrau-c06",
        "lutzelfrau-c07"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "lutzelfrau-c01",
      "source_id": "lutzelfrau-s1",
      "quote": "or Lutscherl in Eastern Austria, Luz , Luzi , or Luzel in the Bavarian forest, is a witch in German folklore who gives gifts — particularly apples, nuts and dried plums — to children on Saint Lucy's Day (December 13), but also has a \"dark Lucia\" side ... This dark, or malevolent Lutzelfrau is reputed to may disembowel (or drown) naughty children",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lutzelfrau, atau Lutscherl di Austria timur dan Luz, Luzi, atau Luzel di Hutan Bayern, adalah penyihir cerita rakyat Jerman yang memberi apel, kacang, dan prem kering kepada anak-anak pada Hari Santa Lusia (13 Desember), tetapi juga punya sisi \"Lusia gelap\" yang konon membelah perut atau menenggelamkan anak nakal.",
        "en": "Lutzelfrau, or Lutscherl in eastern Austria and Luz, Luzi or Luzel in the Bavarian Forest, is a witch of German folklore giving apples, nuts and dried plums to children on Saint Lucy's Day (13 December), but with a 'dark Lucia' side said to disembowel or drown naughty children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutzelfrau-c02",
      "source_id": "lutzelfrau-s1",
      "quote": "While Lutzefrau serves as one standardized generic name for the tradition in the Alpine areas of Austria and Bavaria, Germany, in parts of Austria, she is more simply called Lucia . Around Burgenland, she is known as Luzia , Lutzl , or Fersenlutzl .",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lutzelfrau adalah nama umum tradisi ini di wilayah Alpen Austria dan Bayern, Jerman; di sebagian Austria ia disebut Lucia, dan di sekitar Burgenland Luzia, Lutzl, atau Fersenlutzl.",
        "en": "Lutzelfrau is the standard generic name of the tradition in the Alpine areas of Austria and Bavaria, Germany; in parts of Austria she is simply Lucia, and around Burgenland Luzia, Lutzl or Fersenlutzl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutzelfrau-c03",
      "source_id": "lutzelfrau-s1",
      "quote": "Lutzelfrau is of course associated with Sankt Lucia (Saint Lucy of Syracuse) and Lucia is a name signifying \"luminous\" (German: leuchtende ).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lutzelfrau tentu dikaitkan dengan Santa Lusia dari Sirakusa, dan Lucia adalah nama yang berarti \"yang bercahaya\".",
        "en": "Lutzelfrau is of course associated with Saint Lucy of Syracuse, and Lucia is a name meaning 'luminous'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutzelfrau-c04",
      "source_id": "lutzelfrau-s1",
      "quote": "In Czechia, she is known as Lucie or Louce , etc. In Slovakia, she is called Lucka . Lutzelfrau customs are also common in Slovenia and Croatia, where a \"dark Luz\" was contrasted to the Christian saint. In Slovene, she is called Lucija .",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Ceko ia dikenal sebagai Lucie atau Louce, di Slowakia Lucka; adat Lutzelfrau juga umum di Slovenia dan Kroasia, tempat \"Luz gelap\" dikontraskan dengan santa Kristen, dan dalam bahasa Slovenia ia disebut Lucija.",
        "en": "In Czechia she is Lucie or Louce, in Slovakia Lucka; Lutzelfrau customs are also common in Slovenia and Croatia, where a 'dark Luz' was contrasted with the Christian saint, and in Slovene she is Lucija."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutzelfrau-c05",
      "source_id": "lutzelfrau-s1",
      "quote": "In Burgenland, Lutzelfrau or Pudlfrau visits the children on St. Lucia's day and \"tosses (pudelt ) sweets through the doorway.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Burgenland, Lutzelfrau atau Pudlfrau mengunjungi anak-anak pada hari Santa Lusia dan \"menggulirkan\" manisan lewat ambang pintu.",
        "en": "In Burgenland the Lutzelfrau or Pudlfrau visits children on St Lucy's day and 'tosses' sweets through the doorway."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutzelfrau-c06",
      "source_id": "lutzelfrau-s2",
      "quote": "Lutzelfrau est une sorcière du folklore allemand qui volerait la nuit sur son balai, elle offre des cadeaux - en particulier des pommes, des noix et des prunes séchées - aux enfants le jour de la fête de Sainte-Lucie ( 13 décembre ).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lutzelfrau adalah penyihir cerita rakyat Jerman yang konon terbang di malam hari dengan sapunya dan memberi apel, kacang, dan prem kering kepada anak-anak pada hari raya Santa Lusia (13 Desember).",
        "en": "Lutzelfrau is a witch of German folklore said to fly at night on her broom, who gives apples, nuts and dried plums to children on Saint Lucy's day (13 December)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lutzelfrau-c07",
      "source_id": "lutzelfrau-s1",
      "quote": "According to a mid-19th century source, the Lucia of Bohemia and Tyrol appeared as a goat, draped in a bedsheet with horns protruding out of the sheet. This is paralleled in Scandinavia.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sumber pertengahan abad ke-19, Lusia di Bohemia dan Tirol tampil sebagai kambing yang berselubung seprai dengan tanduk mencuat, dan hal serupa ada di Skandinavia.",
        "en": "According to a mid-19th-century source the Lucia of Bohemia and Tyrol appeared as a goat draped in a bedsheet with horns poking out, paralleled in Scandinavia."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gagak malam adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Eropa, terutama berupa burung mirip gagak yang muncul di malam hari dan dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak-anak agar segera tidur.",
    "en": "Night ravens are creatures of European folklore, mostly raven-like birds that appear at night, used to frighten children into going to bed.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "night-raven-c01",
      "night-raven-c07"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gagak malam (Denmark dan Norwegia: nattravn; Swedia: nattramn, nattkorp; Jerman: Nachtkrapp, Nachtrabe) adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Eropa yang umumnya digambarkan seperti gagak yang muncul di malam hari. Ceritanya berbeda menurut daerah, dan gagak malam bisa juga makhluk lain, misalnya mayat hidup, yang berubah wujud menjadi gagak. Ia kadang dipakai sebagai momok dalam kisah peringatan agar anak-anak mau tidur.",
      "en": "Known in German as Nachtkrapp or Nachtrabe, in Swedish as nattramn or nattkorp and in Danish and Norwegian as nattravn, night ravens are mostly pictured in European folk tradition as raven-like beings of the dark. Beliefs vary from place to place, and a night raven may also be some other being, for example an undead creature in raven shape. They sometimes serve as bugbears in cautionary tales to send children to bed.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "night-raven-c01",
        "night-raven-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, terutama Jerman selatan dan Austria, gagak malam keluar dari persembunyiannya di malam hari untuk berburu; bila dilihat anak kecil, ia menculik mereka ke sarangnya dan memangsanya, sedangkan legenda lain menyebut ia hanya memasukkan anak ke karungnya. Ada pula versi baik yang disebut Guter Nachtkrapp, \"gagak malam yang baik\".",
      "en": "German tradition, chiefly in southern Germany and Austria, usually has the night raven emerging after dark to hunt; any small child who sees it is carried to its nest and eaten, though some legends say it only stuffs children into its bag. There is also a kindly version called the Guter Nachtkrapp, the 'good night raven'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "night-raven-c02",
        "night-raven-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Krabb atau Krapp dalam dialek Jerman Hulu berarti gagak atau burung sejenis, dari bahasa Jerman Hulu Kuno (h)raban; burung pemakan bangkai ini mudah dikaitkan dengan berbagai pengaruh buruk, misalnya menculik anak di malam hari. Di Thüringen tengah, anak-anak diperingatkan tentang kawanan gagak malam yang menangkap anak yang belum pulang setelah gelap.",
      "en": "The name Krabb or Krapp in Upper German dialects means raven or related corvid, from Old High German (h)raban; these carrion birds were easily credited with baleful deeds such as stealing children by night. In central Thuringia children are warned of night ravens that come in flocks and carry off children not home after dark.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "night-raven-c05",
        "night-raven-c06"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "Danish and Norwegian: nattravn ; Swedish: nattramn , nattkorp ; German: Nachtkrapp , Nachtrabe ) are creatures of European folklore, predominantly described as raven like creatures appearing in the night.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gagak malam (Denmark/Norwegia: nattravn; Swedia: nattramn, nattkorp; Jerman: Nachtkrapp, Nachtrabe) adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Eropa yang umumnya digambarkan mirip gagak dan muncul di malam hari; ceritanya berbeda menurut daerah, gagak malam bisa juga makhluk lain seperti mayat hidup yang berubah menjadi gagak, dan kadang dipakai sebagai momok dalam kisah peringatan.",
        "en": "Night ravens (Danish/Norwegian nattravn; Swedish nattramn, nattkorp; German Nachtkrapp, Nachtrabe) are creatures of European folklore, mostly raven-like beings appearing at night; the lore differs regionally, they may be other beings such as undead shapeshifted into ravens, and they are sometimes bugbears in cautionary tales."
      }

    {
      "id": "night-raven-c02",
      "source_id": "night-raven-s1",
      "quote": "In German folklore (German: Nachtkrapp , Nachtrabe ), mainly of Southern Germany and Austria, the most common legends claim that the night raven leaves its hiding place at night to hunt.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, terutama Jerman selatan dan Austria, gagak malam keluar dari persembunyiannya di malam hari untuk berburu; bila dilihat anak kecil, ia menculik mereka ke sarangnya dan memangsanya, sedangkan legenda lain menyebut ia hanya memasukkan anak ke karungnya dan membawanya pergi.",
        "en": "In German folklore, mainly southern Germany and Austria, the night raven leaves its hiding place at night to hunt; if seen by small children it carries them to its nest and devours them, while other legends say it merely bags them and takes them away."

      "source_id": "night-raven-s1",

    {
      "id": "night-raven-c04",
      "source_id": "night-raven-s2",
      "quote": "Nachtkrabb oder Nacht(t)rapp ist im süddeutschen und österreichischen Raum der Name einer Kinderschreckfigur, die eine ähnliche Funktion wie der Sandmann erfüllt.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nachtkrabb atau Nacht(t)rapp di Jerman selatan dan Austria adalah nama momok penakut anak dengan fungsi serupa Sandmann; ia konon menyambar anak yang masih di luar setelah gelap, dan padanannya di Franken disebut Nachtgiger.",
        "en": "Nachtkrabb or Nacht(t)rapp is, in southern Germany and Austria, the name of a child-frightening figure with a function like the Sandman's; it supposedly snatches children still outdoors after dark, and its Franconian counterpart is the Nachtgiger."

    {
      "id": "night-raven-c05",
      "source_id": "night-raven-s2",

      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Krabb (juga Krapp, Krabbe, Rappe; dari Jerman Hulu Kuno [h]raban) dalam dialek Jerman Hulu berarti gagak atau burung sejenis; burung pemakan bangkai ini mudah dikaitkan dengan berbagai pengaruh buruk, misalnya menculik anak di malam hari.",
        "en": "Krabb (also Krapp, Krabbe, Rappe; from Old High German [h]raban) in Upper German dialects means raven or other corvids; these carrion birds were readily credited with baleful deeds, such as the raven stealing children at night."

      "source_id": "night-raven-s2",

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Nis Puk adalah sejenis kobold atau roh rumah dari wilayah berbahasa Denmark, Jerman Rendah, dan Frisia Utara di Schleswig, yang menjaga rumah dan ternak asalkan diberi bubur pada hari Natal.",
    "en": "Nis Puk is a kind of kobold or house spirit from the Danish-, Low German- and North Frisian-speaking lands of Schleswig, who guards house and livestock if given porridge at Christmas.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "nis-puk-c01",

    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Nis Puk (juga Niß Puk) adalah makhluk legenda sejenis kobold dari wilayah berbahasa Denmark, Jerman Rendah, dan Frisia Utara di Jerman utara dan Denmark selatan, termasuk Schleswig. Di Denmark ia juga dikenal sebagai nis pug, varian dari nisse. Tergantung tempatnya, ia bisa tampil sebagai roh rumah tangga atau berperan seperti Drak atau kobold, roh dari neraka yang membuat pemiliknya kaya dengan membawakan barang curian.",
      "en": "A legendary being of the kobold type, Nis Puk (also Niß Puk) belongs to the borderland of southern Denmark and northern Germany, including Schleswig, where Danish, Low German and North Frisian are spoken. In Denmark it is also known as nis pug, a variant of the nisse. Depending on place, it may appear as a household spirit or play the role of a Drak or kobold, an infernal spirit enriching its owner with stolen goods.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nis-puk-c01",
        "nis-puk-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya menggabungkan Nisse dan Puk, dua kata yang bermakna sama; Niß konon berasal dari nama Nikolaus, sedangkan Puk dari bahasa Denmark Kuno puge, dari Nordik Kuno púki, \"kobold\". Menurut sage, Nis Puk tinggal di loteng atau lumbung, tak terlihat oleh orang dewasa dan menjaga rumah, halaman, serta ternak bila penghuni memperlakukan anak dan hewan mereka dengan baik.",
      "en": "His name joins Nisse and Puk, two words meaning the same; Niß is said to come from the name Nikolaus, and Puk from Old Danish puge, from Old Norse púki, 'kobold'. By the legends Nis Puk lives in the attic or barn, invisible to adults and looks after house, yard and animals if the household treats its own children and animals well.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "nis-puk-c03",
        "nis-puk-c04",
        "nis-puk-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Setahun sekali, saat Natal, Nis Puk harus diberi semangkuk bubur dengan mentega; bila tidak, ia pindah ke desa lain dan rumah itu terancam kerusakan dan malapetaka. Penulis Boy Lornsen mengangkatnya dalam beberapa buku anak, dan di depan sekolah Neukirchen berdiri patung kecil Nis Puk.",
      "en": "Once a year, at Christmas, Nis Puk must be given a bowl of porridge with butter; otherwise he moves to another village and the house is exposed to decay and misfortune.         "nis-puk-c06"
      ]

      "quote": "The Nis Puk (sometimes also Niß Puk ( German: [ˈnɪs pʊk] )) is a legendary creature, a kind of Kobold, from Danish-, Low German- and North Frisian-speaking areas of Northern Germany and Southern Denmark, among them Schleswig, today divided into the German Southern Schleswig and Danish Northern Schleswig.       "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nis Puk (juga Niß Puk) adalah makhluk legenda sejenis kobold dari wilayah berbahasa Denmark, Jerman Rendah, dan Frisia Utara di Jerman utara dan Denmark selatan, termasuk Schleswig; di Denmark ia juga disebut nis pug, varian nisse.",
        "en": "Nis Puk (also Niß Puk) is a legendary kobold-like creature from Danish-, Low German- and North Frisian-speaking northern Germany and southern Denmark, including Schleswig; in Denmark it is also nis pug, a variant of the nisse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nis-puk-c02",
      "source_id": "nis-puk-s1",
      "quote": "Depending on the place, it can either appear as a domestic spirit or take on the role of a being generally called Drak or Kobold in Danish and German mythology, an infernal spirit making its owner wealthy by bringing them stolen goods.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tergantung tempatnya, ia bisa tampil sebagai roh rumah tangga atau berperan sebagai makhluk yang disebut Drak atau kobold dalam mitologi Denmark dan Jerman, roh dari neraka yang membuat pemiliknya kaya dengan membawakan barang curian.",
        "en": "Depending on place, it may appear as a domestic spirit or take the role of what Danish and German mythology call a Drak or kobold, an infernal spirit enriching its owner with stolen goods."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nis-puk-c03",
      "source_id": "nis-puk-s1",
      "quote": "The form Nis Puk is described as a \"tautological juxtaposition\" of two words meaning the same thing. The name Niß said to be derived from the proper name Nikolaus, i.e., Nicholas ... The name Puk is attested in the Old Danish form puge , descended from Old Norse puki púke , meaning kobold",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk Nis Puk disebut \"penjajaran tautologis\" dua kata bermakna sama: Niß konon dari nama Nikolaus, dan Puk tercatat dalam bahasa Denmark Kuno puge, dari Nordik Kuno púki, \"kobold\".",
        "en": "The form Nis Puk is called a 'tautological juxtaposition' of two words meaning the same: Niß said to come from Nikolaus, and Puk attested as Old Danish puge, from Old Norse púki, 'kobold'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nis-puk-c04",
      "source_id": "nis-puk-s2",
      "quote": "Der Nis Puk soll auf Haus, Hof und Tiere achtgeben ... Der Nis Puk soll auf dem Dachboden oder in einer Scheune leben. Angeblich können ihn Erwachsene nicht mehr sehen. Einmal im Jahr, zu Weihnachten, muss dem Nis Puk eine Schüssel Grütze (mit Butter) gebracht werden. Geschieht das nicht, wandert der Nis Puk in ein anderes Dorf.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nis Puk menjaga rumah, halaman, dan ternak; ia tinggal di loteng atau lumbung, tak terlihat oleh orang dewasa, dan setahun sekali saat Natal harus diberi semangkuk bubur bermentega, bila tidak ia pindah ke desa lain.",
        "en": "Nis Puk looks after house, yard and animals; he lives in the attic or barn, unseen by adults, and once a year at Christmas must get a bowl of porridge with butter, or he moves to another village."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nis-puk-c05",
      "source_id": "nis-puk-s2",
      "quote": "Nis Puk (im Sylter Friesisch Nes Pük, im Dänischen zum Teil auch Nis Pug und Nis Puge) ist eine Figur aus dem Bereich der Volksmärchen. ... Vor der Schule in Neukirchen befindet sich eine kleine Statue von Nis Puk.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nis Puk (Frisia Sylt: Nes Pük; Denmark: Nis Pug, Nis Puge) adalah tokoh dongeng rakyat, dan di depan sekolah Neukirchen berdiri patung kecil Nis Puk.",
        "en": "Nis Puk (Sylt Frisian Nes Pük; Danish Nis Pug, Nis Puge) is a folk-tale figure, and a small statue of him stands in front of the school in Neukirchen."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "nis-puk-c06",
      "source_id": "nis-puk-s3",
      "quote": "Figuren forbinder den dansk-skånsk-norske nisse med den engelske puk ... Han tager sig af huset og dyrene, hvis beboerne er villige til at behandle deres egne børn og dyr godt. ... Huset ville så være udsat for forfald og ulykker. ... Et ordsprog fra øen Sild siger på sildfrisisk Hi glüüret üs en pük (≈Han stirrer som en Nis Puk). Figuren fandt indgang i flere af Boy Lornsens børnebøger.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Figur ini memadukan nisse Denmark-Skåne-Norwegia dengan puk Inggris; ia menjaga rumah dan hewan bila penghuni bersedia memperlakukan anak dan hewan mereka dengan baik; tanpa bubur rumah itu terancam kerusakan dan malapetaka; pepatah Sylt berbunyi \"Hi glüüret üs en pük\" (ia menatap seperti Nis Puk), dan ia muncul dalam buku-buku anak Boy Lornsen.",
        "en": "The figure combines the Danish-Scanian-Norwegian nisse with the English puk; he looks after house and animals if the occupants treat their own children and animals well; without porridge the house faces decay and misfortune; a Sylt saying runs 'Hi glüüret üs en pük' (he stares like a Nis Puk), and he features in Boy Lornsen's children's books."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

      "field": "sources",

  ]

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Rå adalah roh penjaga tempat atau bentang alam tertentu dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia dan Sami, yang bisa membawa untung atau sial bagi manusia.",
    "en": "A rå is a spirit that keeps a particular place or landform in Scandinavian and Sami folklore, able to bring good or bad luck to people.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ra-c01",
      "ra-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, rå (jamak rår), kependekan dari rådare (\"penguasa\"), dari kata råda (\"memerintah, menasihati\") dan Nordik Kuno ráða, adalah roh penjaga suatu tempat atau bentang alam. Rå dikenal dalam budaya Nordik maupun Sami, tempat ia disebut radie. Manusia perlu menjaga hubungan baik dengannya, karena ia menguasai kekuatan alam dan hewan di bawah asuhannya serta dapat membawa untung atau sial bagi siapa pun yang mengganggu wilayahnya.",
      "en": "In Scandinavian folklore a rå (plural rår), short for rådare ('ruler'), from råda ('to rule, to advise') and Old Norse ráða, is the keeper of a particular place or landform. It is known in Nordic and Sami culture alike, the Sami calling it radie. People had to keep on good terms with it, since it ruled the natural forces and animals in its care and could bring good or bad luck to anyone meddling with its domain.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ra-c01",
        "ra-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Rår dibedakan menurut wilayah alam yang dijaganya, seperti skogsrå atau hulder untuk hutan, sjörå untuk air tawar, havsrå untuk air asin, dan bergsrå untuk gunung. Menurut kepercayaan lama, setiap benda, hewan, dan tumbuhan punya rå pelindungnya sendiri, dan rå juga bisa menjaga tempat dan benda milik manusia, seperti skeppsrået (rå kapal) dan gruvrået (rå tambang). Rår dapat tampil berwujud hewan atau manusia; skogsrå dan bergsrå biasanya digambarkan perempuan.",
      "en": "Rår are distinguished by the sphere of nature they keep, such as the skogsrå or hulder of the forest, the sjörå of fresh water, the havsrå of salt water and the bergsrå of mountains. In old belief every object, animal and plant had its own protecting rå, and a rå might also oversee human places and things, such as the skeppsrået (ship's rå) and gruvrået (mine's rå). Rår may appear as animals or as men or women; the skogsrå and bergsrå are usually described as female.",

      "quote": "short for rådare (\"ruler\") from råda (to rule, to advise) from old Norse ráða, is a spirit who is the keeper or warden of a particular location or landform. The rå is known both in Nordic culture and in the Sami culture, where it is called radie.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Rå, kependekan dari rådare (\"penguasa\"), dari råda (\"memerintah, menasihati\") dan Nordik Kuno ráða, adalah roh penjaga suatu tempat atau bentang alam, dikenal dalam budaya Nordik maupun Sami, yang menyebutnya radie.",
        "en": "Rå, short for rådare ('ruler'), from råda ('to rule, to advise') and Old Norse ráða, is a spirit keeping a particular place or landform, known in both Nordic and Sami culture, where it is called radie."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ra-c02",
      "source_id": "ra-s1",
      "quote": "It was important for humans to cultivate good relationships with them, since they had power over the natural forces and animals under their care, and could cause both good and bad luck for humans who interfered with the places and creatures under their watch.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Manusia penting menjaga hubungan baik dengan mereka, karena mereka menguasai kekuatan alam dan hewan di bawah asuhannya dan dapat membawa untung maupun sial bagi orang yang mengganggu tempat dan makhluk yang dijaganya.",
        "en": "It was important for people to keep good relations with them, as they ruled the natural forces and animals in their care and could bring good or bad luck to anyone interfering with what they watched over."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ra-c03",
      "source_id": "ra-s1",
      "quote": "The different species of rår are sometimes distinguished according to the different spheres of nature with which each was associated, such as skogsrå or hulder (forest), sjörå (freshwater) or havsrå (saltwater), and bergsrå (mountains).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jenis-jenis rår kadang dibedakan menurut wilayah alam yang dikaitkan dengannya, seperti skogsrå atau hulder (hutan), sjörå (air tawar), havsrå (air asin), dan bergsrå (gunung).",

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Roggenmuhme adalah roh gandum perempuan dan momok penakut anak dalam sage Jerman, yang berkeliaran di ladang gandum hitam dan dapat menyuburkan atau mengeringkan ladang.",
    "en": "The Roggenmuhme is a female grain demon and children's bugbear of German legend who roams the rye fields and can either make them fertile or wither them.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "roggenmuhme-c01",
      "roggenmuhme-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Roggenmuhme adalah roh gandum perempuan sekaligus momok penakut anak dalam sage Jerman yang tinggal di ladang. Ia berjalan mondar-mandir di ladang, makan biji-bijian, dan mencabut bulir yang belum matang; bila marah kepada petani, ia mengeringkan ladangnya. Namun pada umumnya langkahnya melintasi ladang membawa kesuburan, dan saat panen ia melarikan diri ke berkas terakhir.",
      "en": "The Roggenmuhme is a female grain demon and children's bugbear of German legend who dwells in the fields. She walks up and down the field, eats the grain and pulls out unripe ears; when angry with a farmer she dries out his field. Generally, though, her passage through the field brings fertility, and at harvest she flees into the last sheaf.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roggenmuhme-c01",
        "roggenmuhme-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia diberi bagian panen yang dibiarkan berdiri atau dilempar ke ladang, adat yang diharapkan membuatnya berkenan dan mendatangkan tahun yang subur. Tempat tinggalnya dibayangkan di bawah tanah, di \"kerajaan akar\" atau gua. Seperti Frau Holle dan Perchta, ia menghukum pelayan malas yang tidak menyelesaikan pintalannya pada dua belas malam, sehingga ia juga disebut Rockenmör; napasnya membawa penyakit dan kematian.",
      "en": "She receives a share of the harvest, left standing or thrown into the field, a custom meant to win her favour and bring a fruitful next year. Her home is imagined underground, in a 'root realm' or cave. Like Frau Holle and Perchta she punishes lazy maids who leave their distaffs unspun in the Twelve Nights, hence the name Rockenmör; her breath brings sickness and death.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roggenmuhme-c02",
        "roggenmuhme-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia punya banyak nama, seperti Roggenmutter, Roggenfrau, Roggenhexe, Kornmutter, dan Kornfrau. Dalam mitologi Slavia, Jermanik, dan Baltik, sosok ini dikenal sebagai \"perempuan gandum hitam\" yang terkait ladang, roti, dan gandum hitam, disebut sebagai roh atau dewi.",
      "en": "She has many names, such as Roggenmutter, Roggenfrau, Roggenhexe, Kornmutter and Kornfrau. In Slavic, Germanic and Baltic mythology this figure is known as the 'rye woman' linked with field, bread and rye, described as a spirit or goddess.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "roggenmuhme-c04",
        "roggenmuhme-c05",
        "roggenmuhme-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

    {
      "id": "roggenmuhme-s2",
      "url": "https://ru.wikipedia.org/wiki/%D0%A0%D0%B6%D0%B0%D0%BD%D0%B0%D1%8F_%D0%B1%D0%B0%D0%B1%D0%B0",
      "title": "Ржаная баба",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (ru)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "ru",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "roggenmuhme-c01",
      "source_id": "roggenmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "Die Roggenmuhme ist ein weiblicher Korndämon und Kinderschreck der deutschen Sage, der sich im Feld und Acker aufhält.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roggenmuhme adalah roh gandum perempuan dan momok penakut anak dalam sage Jerman yang tinggal di ladang.",
        "en": "The Roggenmuhme is a female grain demon and children's bugbear of German legend who keeps to field and acre."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenmuhme-c02",
      "source_id": "roggenmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "Die Roggenmuhme geht im Feld auf und ab, ernährt sich vom Korn und reißt die unreifen Ähren aus. Wenn sie dem Bauer zürnt, so dorrt sie sein Feld aus und straft ihn auf diese Weise. Allgemein sorgt das Durchschreiten des Feldes durch die Roggenmuhme allerdings für Fruchtbarkeit. Bei der Ernte flieht sie in die letzte Garbe. Die Roggenmuhme erhält auch einen Anteil an der Ernte",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roggenmuhme berjalan mondar-mandir di ladang, makan biji-bijian, dan mencabut bulir yang belum matang; bila marah kepada petani ia mengeringkan ladangnya, tetapi umumnya langkahnya membawa kesuburan; saat panen ia melarikan diri ke berkas terakhir, dan ia juga diberi bagian panen.",
        "en": "The Roggenmuhme walks the field, feeds on grain and pulls out unripe ears; angry with a farmer she withers his field, but generally her passage brings fertility; at harvest she flees into the last sheaf and also receives a share of the crop."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenmuhme-c03",
      "source_id": "roggenmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "Die Wohnung der Roggenmuhme wird unterirdisch gedacht, im „Wurzelreich“ oder in einer Höhle. Die Roggenmuhme ähnelt Frau Holle und Perchta darin, dass sie die faulen Mägde straft, welche in den Zwölfnächten ihre Spinnrocken nicht abgesponnen haben. Daher heißt sie auch Rockenmör. Der Hauch der Roggenmuhme bringt Krankheit und Tod.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tempat tinggal Roggenmuhme dibayangkan di bawah tanah, di \"kerajaan akar\" atau gua; seperti Frau Holle dan Perchta ia menghukum pelayan malas yang tidak menyelesaikan pintalan pada dua belas malam, sehingga disebut juga Rockenmör; napasnya membawa penyakit dan kematian.",
        "en": "Her dwelling is imagined underground, in a 'root realm' or cave; like Frau Holle and Perchta she punishes lazy maids who leave their distaffs unspun in the Twelve Nights, hence also Rockenmör; her breath brings sickness and death."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenmuhme-c04",
      "source_id": "roggenmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "Folgende Namen auf Roggen- sind für die Roggenmuhme außerdem überliefert: Roggenmutter, Roggenfrau, Roggenweib, Roggenweibchen, Roggenwief, Roggenalte, Roggenhexe",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama berunsur Roggen- yang tercatat untuk Roggenmuhme antara lain Roggenmutter, Roggenfrau, Roggenweib, Roggenalte, dan Roggenhexe.",
        "en": "Names in Roggen- recorded for the Roggenmuhme include Roggenmutter, Roggenfrau, Roggenweib, Roggenalte and Roggenhexe."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenmuhme-c05",
      "source_id": "roggenmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "Namen mit dem Element Korn- sind beispielsweise: Kornmutter, Kornmoder, Kornmuhme, Kornmöhm, Kornmähne, Kornfrau, Kornweib",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama berunsur Korn- misalnya Kornmutter, Kornmoder, Kornmuhme, Kornfrau, dan Kornweib.",
        "en": "Names with Korn- include Kornmutter, Kornmoder, Kornmuhme, Kornfrau and Kornweib."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "roggenmuhme-c06",
      "source_id": "roggenmuhme-s2",
      "quote": "Ржаная баба (лит. Rugių ̃ bóba, серб. Баба Руга, Баба Рога, нем. Roggenmuhme) – персонаж славянской, германской и балтской мифологии, связанный с полем, хлебом и рожью. Упоминается как дух или божество.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perempuan gandum hitam (Lituania: Rugių bóba; Serbia: Baba Ruga; Jerman: Roggenmuhme) adalah tokoh mitologi Slavia, Jermanik, dan Baltik yang terkait dengan ladang, roti, dan gandum hitam, disebut sebagai roh atau dewi.",
        "en": "The 'rye woman' (Lithuanian Rugių bóba, Serbian Baba Ruga, German Roggenmuhme) is a figure of Slavic, Germanic and Baltic mythology linked with field, bread and rye (поле, хлеб, рожь), named as a spirit or deity."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Skinfaxi, \"surai bersinar\", adalah kuda Dagr (Siang) dalam mitologi Nordik yang menarik kereta Siang melintasi langit, dan suainya menerangi langit serta bumi.",
    "en": "Skinfaxi, 'shining mane', is the horse of Dagr (Day) in Norse mythology that draws Day's chariot across the sky, its mane lighting up sky and earth.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "skinfaxi-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Skinfaxi adalah kuda Dagr, sang Siang, sedangkan Hrímfaxi kuda Nótt, sang Malam. Namanya berarti \"surai bersinar\". Setiap hari Skinfaxi menarik kereta Dagr melintasi langit, dan suainya menerangi langit serta bumi di bawahnya.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Skinfaxi is the horse of Dagr, the Day, while Hrímfaxi is that of Nótt, the Night. Its name means 'shining mane'. Every day Skinfaxi pulls Dagr's chariot across the sky, and its mane lights up the sky and the earth below.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "skinfaxi-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut satu uraian, Skinfaxe ditambatkan pada kereta Dag dan dikendarai melintasi langit setiap hari; surai dan ekornya bersinar seperti emas dan menerangi dunia. Dalam Gylfaginning di Den yngre Edda disebutkan bahwa kuda milik Dag bernama Skinfakse, dan dari suainya cahaya memancar ke seluruh udara dan bumi. Penyair Oehlenschläger menyebutnya dalam puisi Guldhornene.",
      "en": "In one account Skinfaxe is harnessed to Dag's chariot and driven across the sky each day; its mane and tail shine like gold and light the world. The Gylfaginning of the Younger Edda says the horse Dag has is called Skinfakse, and light shines over all the air and earth from its mane. The poet Oehlenschläger mentions it in his poem Guldhornene.",

      "quote": "In Norse mythology, Skinfaxi (Old Norse: [ˈskinˌfɑkse] ) and Hrímfaxi [ˈhriːmˌfɑkse] are the horses of Dagr (day) and Nótt (night).       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Skinfaxi dan Hrímfaxi adalah kuda Dagr (siang) dan Nótt (malam); namanya berarti \"surai bersinar\" dan \"surai berembun beku\", dan Skinfaxi menarik kereta Dagr melintasi langit setiap hari sementara suainya menerangi langit dan bumi.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology Skinfaxi and Hrímfaxi are the horses of Dagr (day) and Nótt (night); the names mean 'shining mane' and 'frost mane', and Skinfaxi pulls Dagr's chariot across the sky every day, its mane lighting sky and earth."
      }

    {
      "id": "skinfaxi-c02",
      "source_id": "skinfaxi-s2",
      "quote": "Skinfaxe (Oldnordisk: Skinfaxi, 'skinnende manke') er hesten, der i nordisk mytologi er forspændt Dags vogn. Han bliver på denne kørt hen over himlen hver dag. Hestens manke og hale skinner som guld og oplyser verden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Skinfaxe (Nordik Kuno Skinfaxi, \"surai bersinar\") adalah kuda yang ditambatkan pada kereta Dag dalam mitologi Nordik dan dikendarai melintasi langit setiap hari; surai dan ekornya bersinar seperti emas dan menerangi dunia.",
        "en": "Skinfaxe (Old Norse Skinfaxi, 'shining mane') is the horse harnessed to Dag's chariot in Norse mythology, driven across the sky each day; its mane and tail shine like gold and light up the world."

    },
    {
      "id": "skinfaxi-c03",
      "source_id": "skinfaxi-s3",
      "quote": "Skinfakse (Norrønt: Skinfaxi, skinnende man) er i norrøn mytologi den hesten Dag rir på over himmelen hver dag. Skinfakse nevnes i Gylvaginning i Den yngre Edda: Den hesten Dag har heiter Skinfakse og det lyser over all lufta og jorda frå fakset hans",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Skinfakse (Nordik Kuno Skinfaxi, \"surai bersinar\") adalah kuda yang ditunggangi Dag melintasi langit setiap hari; dalam Gylfaginning di Den yngre Edda disebut: \"Kuda milik Dag bernama Skinfakse, dan cahaya memancar ke seluruh udara dan bumi dari suainya.\"",
        "en": "Skinfakse (Old Norse Skinfaxi, 'shining mane') is the horse Day rides across the sky daily; the Gylfaginning of the Younger Edda says: 'The horse Dag has is called Skinfakse, and light shines over all the air and earth from his mane.'"

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Slattenpatten, \"payudara kendur\", adalah makhluk perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Denmark, sejenis perempuan elf yang berpayudara sangat panjang dan dikejar Kong Volmer setiap malam.",
    "en": "Slattenpatten, 'flaccid-breasts', is a female being of Danish folklore, a kind of elf-woman with extremely long breasts who is chased every night by Kong Volmer.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "slattenpatte-c01",
      "slattenpatte-c02",
      "slattenpatte-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Slattenpatten atau slattenlangpat (harfiah \"payudara kendur\" atau \"payudara panjang yang melorot\") adalah makhluk perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Denmark, terutama dikaitkan dengan Suså. Ia juga disebut ellekvinde atau ellekone (perempuan elf), tetapi berbeda dari ellepige yang biasanya digambarkan sangat cantik, ia dicirikan oleh payudara yang amat panjang.",
      "en": "Danish folk tradition, especially around Suså, tells of Slattenpatten or slattenlangpat, a female being whose name means roughly 'flabby breasts' or 'long sagging breast'. She is also called ellekvinde or ellekone (elf-woman), but unlike the ellepige, usually described as very beautiful, she is marked by extraordinarily long breasts.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "slattenpatte-c01",
        "slattenpatte-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Payudaranya menjuntai sampai ke pinggang, tetapi dapat dilempar ke atas bahu agar anak yang digendong di punggung bisa menyusu; saat dikejar, ia melempar payudaranya ke bahu agar tidak menghalangi larinya. Di Sjælland selatan ada sejumlah legenda tentang ia yang diburu \"Kong Volmer\" (sebenarnya Valdemar Atterdag) atau \"Wojens Jæger\" dengan anjing-anjingnya; menurut legenda, Kong Volmer menembaknya setiap malam, tetapi ia kembali keesokan paginya.",
      "en": "Her breasts hang down to the waist, but can be thrown over the shoulder so a child carried on her back can feed; when chased she flings them over her shoulders so they do not hinder her running. In southern Zealand several legends tell of her being hunted by 'Kong Volmer' (really Valdemar Atterdag) or 'Wojens Jæger' with his hounds; by legend Kong Volmer shot her every night, yet she came back the next morning.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "slattenpatte-c02",
        "slattenpatte-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Slattenpatten mencuri dari manusia di malam hari, tetapi tak bisa mencuri roti yang digoresi tanda salib. Orang yang dikejarnya bisa lolos dengan berlari menyeberangi alur bajak, karena ia hanya dapat berlari searah alur yang dibajak dengan bajak baja, atau dengan melompati air mengalir. Menurut legenda lain, payudaranya panjang agar ia dapat menyusui anak-anak ikannya di dalam air, sehingga ia juga dikaitkan dengan air dan dianggap dewi air, khususnya di Sjælland menurut sebagian arkeolog.",
      "en": "Slattenpatten steals from people at night, but cannot take bread scored with a cross. Someone she pursues can escape by running across the plough furrows, since she can only run along furrows ploughed with a steel plough, or by jumping over running water. Another legend says her breasts were long so she could suckle her fish-children under water, so she is also linked with water and seen as a water goddess, particularly in Zealand according to some archaeologists.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "slattenpatte-c05",
        "slattenpatte-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tanngnjóstr, \"penggeretak gigi\", adalah salah satu dari dua kambing jantan yang menarik kereta dewa Thor dalam mitologi Nordik; bersama Tanngrisnir ia dapat disembelih lalu dihidupkan kembali.",
    "en": "Tanngnjóstr, 'teeth-grinder', is one of the two goats that pull the god Thor's chariot in Norse mythology; with Tanngrisnir it can be slaughtered and brought back to life.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tanngnjost-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tanngnjóstr adalah salah satu kambing jantan penarik kereta dewa Thor dalam mitologi Nordik, berpasangan dengan Tanngrisnir. Keduanya tercatat dalam Edda Puisi, yang dihimpun pada abad ke-13 dari tradisi lebih tua, dan dalam Edda Prosa karya Snorri Sturluson dari abad yang sama. Namanya dalam bahasa Nordik Kuno berarti \"penggeretak gigi\"; Rudolf Simek menilai nama-nama ini masih muda saat dicatat dan mungkin rekaan Snorri.",
      "en": "Paired with Tanngrisnir, Tanngnjóstr is one of the goats drawing the chariot of the god Thor in Norse mythology. Both appear in the Poetic Edda, a 13th-century compilation of older traditional material, and in Snorri Sturluson's Prose Edda, also of the 13th century. Its Old Norse name means 'teeth-grinder'; Rudolf Simek judges the names young when recorded and possibly Snorri's invention.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tanngnjost-c01",
        "tanngnjost-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Edda Prosa, Thor dapat memasak kedua kambingnya untuk santapan, lalu menghidupkannya kembali keesokan harinya dengan palu Mjölnir. Suatu kali Thor bermalam di rumah petani dan berbagi daging kambing; anak petani, Þjálfi, mematahkan tulang paha untuk mengisap sumsumnya, sehingga salah satu kambing pincang setelah dibangkitkan, dan Thor menjadikan Þjálfi serta saudarinya Röskva pelayannya.",
      "en": "According to the Prose Edda, Thor can cook his two goats for a meal and revive them the next day with his hammer Mjölnir. Once Thor stayed with a farming family and shared the goat meat; the farmer's son Þjálfi split a ham-bone for its marrow, so one goat was lame after being raised, and Thor took Þjálfi and his sister Röskva as his servants.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tanngnjost-c03",
        "tanngnjost-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Tanngnióstr, sebagaimana tertulis dalam Gylfaginning, dijelaskan dari tann, \"gigi\", dan gnjóstr dari kata kerja gnísta, \"menggesek\", dan ditafsirkan sebagai tiruan bunyi petir yang menyambar, sejalan dengan gemuruh kereta Thor sebagai guntur. Para sarjana mengaitkan kambing yang terus pulih ini dengan babi Sæhrímnir yang disantap setiap malam.",
      "en": "The name Tanngnióstr, as written in the Gylfaginning, is derived from tann, 'tooth', and gnjóstr from the verb gnísta, 'to rub together', and read as imitating the sound of striking lightning, in line with the rumble of Thor's chariot as thunder. Scholars link these ever-renewed goats to the beast Sæhrímnir, eaten every night.",

        "tanngnjost-c04",
        "tanngnjost-c06",
        "tanngnjost-c07"
      ]

      "id": "tanngnjost-s2",
      "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanngnjostr",
      "title": "Tanngnjostr",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (de)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "de",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "tanngnjost-c01",
      "source_id": "tanngnjost-s1",
      "quote": "and Tanngnjóstr ([ˈtɑnːˌɡnjoːstz̠] ) are the goats who pull the chariot of the god Thor in Norse mythology. They are attested in the Poetic Edda, compiled in the 13th century from earlier traditional sources, and the Prose Edda, written by Snorri Sturluson in the 13th century.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tanngrisnir dan Tanngnjóstr adalah kambing jantan penarik kereta dewa Thor dalam mitologi Nordik, tercatat dalam Edda Puisi yang dihimpun abad ke-13 dari tradisi lebih tua dan dalam Edda Prosa yang ditulis Snorri Sturluson pada abad ke-13.",
        "en": "Tanngrisnir and Tanngnjóstr are the goats that pull the god Thor's chariot in Norse mythology, attested in the Poetic Edda, compiled in the 13th century from older tradition, and in the Prose Edda written by Snorri Sturluson in the 13th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tanngnjost-c02",
      "source_id": "tanngnjost-s1",
      "quote": "The Old Norse name Tanngrisnir translates to \"teeth-barer, snarler\" and Tanngnjóstr to \"teeth-grinder, teeth-gnasher\". Scholar Rudolf Simek comments that the names were young when recorded, and may have been inventions of Snorri. Tanngnjóstr is sometimes modernly anglicized as Tanngiost.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Nordik Kuno Tanngrisnir berarti \"penyeringai gigi, penggeram\" dan Tanngnjóstr \"penggeretak gigi\"; Rudolf Simek menilai nama-nama ini masih muda saat dicatat dan mungkin rekaan Snorri; Tanngnjóstr kadang ditulis Tanngiost dalam bahasa Inggris modern.",
        "en": "Old Norse Tanngrisnir means 'teeth-barer, snarler' and Tanngnjóstr 'teeth-grinder, teeth-gnasher'; Rudolf Simek says the names were young when recorded and may be Snorri's inventions; Tanngnjóstr is sometimes anglicised Tanngiost."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tanngnjost-c03",
      "source_id": "tanngnjost-s1",
      "quote": "The Prose Edda relates that when Thor cooks the goats, their flesh provides sustenance for the god, and, after Thor resurrects them with his hammer, Mjölnir, they are brought back to life the next day.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Edda Prosa, daging kambing yang dimasak Thor menjadi santapan sang dewa, dan setelah dibangkitkan dengan palu Mjölnir mereka hidup kembali keesokan harinya.",
        "en": "The Prose Edda says that when Thor cooks the goats their flesh feeds the god, and after he revives them with his hammer Mjölnir they live again the next day."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tanngnjost-c04",
      "source_id": "tanngnjost-s2",
      "quote": "Die Namen bedeuten übersetzt: „Mit den Zähnen knirschend, Zahnknirscher“, aus dem altnordischen „Tann“ für Zahn und „gnjóstr“ vom Verb „gnísta“ für aneinanderreiben. ... Die Namen werden dahin gedeutet, dass sie lautmalend das Geräusch des in Zacken niederfahrenden Blitzes nachahmen, analog zum lautmalenden Geräusch durch den fahrenden Wagen Thors als der Donner",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama-nama ini berarti \"menggeretakkan gigi, penggeretak gigi\", dari Nordik Kuno tann (\"gigi\") dan gnjóstr dari kata kerja gnísta (\"menggesek\"), dan ditafsirkan sebagai tiruan bunyi petir yang menyambar bergerigi, sejalan dengan bunyi kereta Thor sebagai guntur.",
        "en": "The names mean 'grinding the teeth, tooth-gnasher', from Old Norse tann ('tooth') and gnjóstr from the verb gnísta ('to rub together'), read as imitating the sound of jagged lightning, as Thor's moving chariot sounds as thunder."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tanngnjost-c05",
      "source_id": "tanngnjost-s1",
      "quote": "one of their children, Þjálfi, broke one of the bones to suck out the marrow, resulting in the lameness of one of the goats upon resurrection. As a result, Thor maintains Þjálfi and his sister Röskva as his servants.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Salah satu anak petani, Þjálfi, mematahkan tulang untuk mengisap sumsumnya sehingga satu kambing pincang setelah dibangkitkan, dan karena itu Thor menjadikan Þjálfi dan saudarinya Röskva pelayannya.",
        "en": "One of the farmer's children, Þjálfi, broke a bone to suck the marrow, leaving one goat lame on revival, so Thor keeps Þjálfi and his sister Röskva as servants."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tanngnjost-c06",
      "source_id": "tanngnjost-s1",
      "quote": "Scholars have linked the ever-replenishing goats to the nightly-consumed beast Sæhrímnir in Norse mythology and Scandinavian folk beliefs involving herring bones and witchcraft.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para sarjana mengaitkan kambing yang terus pulih ini dengan Sæhrímnir yang disantap setiap malam dalam mitologi Nordik dan dengan kepercayaan rakyat Skandinavia tentang tulang ikan haring dan sihir.",
        "en": "Scholars link the ever-replenishing goats to Sæhrímnir, eaten nightly in Norse mythology, and to Scandinavian folk beliefs about herring bones and witchcraft."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tanngnjost-c07",
      "source_id": "tanngnjost-s2",
      "quote": "„Þórr á hafra ii er svá heita: Tanngnióstr ok Tanngrisnir ...“ „Þórr besitzt zwei Böcke Tanngnióstr und Tanngrisnir ...“",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gylfaginning bab 21: \"Thor memiliki dua kambing jantan, Tanngnióstr dan Tanngrisnir.\"",
        "en": "Gylfaginning chapter 21: 'Thor owns two goats, Tanngnióstr and Tanngrisnir.'"
      }
    }
  ],

    "en": "Tanngrisnir, 'teeth-barer', is a goat of Thor in Norse mythology who with Tanngnjóstr pulls the god's chariot and can be eaten and then revived.",

    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Tanngrisnir dan pasangannya Tanngnjóstr adalah kambing jantan yang menarik kereta dewa Thor, dan dikenal juga sebagai kambing-kambing Thor. Dalam bahasa Nordik Kuno, namanya diterjemahkan \"penyeringai gigi\" atau \"penggeram\", dan dalam satu uraian juga \"penggeretak gigi\" atau \"bergigi renggang\".       "en": "In Norse mythology Tanngrisnir and its partner Tanngnjóstr are the goats that draw the chariot of the god Thor, also known as Thor's goats.     {

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Naga dalam puisi Inggris Kuno Beowulf adalah lawan ketiga dan terakhir sang pahlawan: penimbun harta penyembur api yang mengamuk karena sebuah piala dicuri dari sarangnya.",
    "en": "The dragon of the Old English poem Beowulf is the hero's third and last foe: a fire-breathing treasure-keeper that rampages after a cup is stolen from its lair.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "the-dragon-beowulf-c01",
      "the-dragon-beowulf-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Babak akhir puisi Anglo-Saxon Beowulf memuat pertarungan Beowulf dengan seekor naga, monster ketiga yang dihadapinya. Setelah memerintah kaum Geat dengan bijak selama lima puluh tahun, seorang budak membangunkan dan membuat naga itu murka dengan mencuri piala berhias permata dari sarangnya. Naga itu lalu membakar rumah-rumah dan tanah kaum Geat, termasuk rumah Beowulf, sehingga sang raja memutuskan melawannya sendiri.",
      "en": "The final act of the Anglo-Saxon poem Beowulf has Beowulf fighting a dragon, the third monster he faces. After he has ruled the Geats wisely for fifty years, a slave wakes and enrages the dragon by stealing a jewelled cup from its lair. The dragon then burns the Geats' homes and lands, Beowulf's included, and the king resolves to fight it himself.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-dragon-beowulf-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para pengiringnya lari ketakutan, kecuali Wiglaf yang tetap bertarung di sisinya. Ketika naga itu melukai Beowulf secara fatal, Wiglaf menyerangnya dengan pedang, dan Beowulf membunuhnya dengan belatinya. Naga itu konon telah tidur di atas harta selama tiga ratus tahun; ia digambarkan bisa terbang dan merayap, menyembur api, dan gigitannya berbisa.",
      "en": "His thanes flee in terror, except Wiglaf, who fights at his side. When the dragon wounds Beowulf mortally, Wiglaf strikes it with his sword and Beowulf kills it with his dagger. The dragon is said to have slept on the treasure for three hundred years; it is described as able to fly and crawl, breathing fire, with a venomous bite.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-dragon-beowulf-c01",
        "the-dragon-beowulf-c05",
        "the-dragon-beowulf-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Beowulf adalah karya sastra Inggris pertama yang menampilkan pembunuh naga, dan penyairnya yang pertama memadukan berbagai motif Jermanik menjadi naga penyembur api yang khas. Pertarungan ini melambangkan perlawanan Beowulf terhadap kejahatan dan kehancuran, sedangkan naga itu bertindak sebagai \"raja emas\" tiruan yang membalas pencurian satu piala dengan menyerang seluruh kerajaan. Naga Beowulf diadaptasi J. R. R. Tolkien untuk Dunia Tengah dalam The Hobbit (1937).",
      "en": "Beowulf is the first work of English literature to present a dragonslayer, and its poet was the first to combine Germanic motifs into a distinctive fire-breathing dragon. The fight symbolises Beowulf's stand against evil and destruction, while the dragon acts as a mock 'goldking', avenging the theft of one cup by attacking the whole kingdom. J. R. R. Tolkien later reworked this dragon for his Middle-earth novel The Hobbit (1937).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "the-dragon-beowulf-c02",
        "the-dragon-beowulf-c03",
        "the-dragon-beowulf-c04"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beowulf memerintah dengan bijak selama lima puluh tahun sampai seorang budak membangunkan dan membuat murka seekor naga dengan mencuri piala berpermata dari sarangnya; naga itu membakar rumah dan tanah kaum Geat, Beowulf memutuskan melawannya sendiri, dan ketika naga melukainya secara fatal, Wiglaf menyerang dengan pedang dan Beowulf membunuhnya dengan belati.",
        "en": "Beowulf rules wisely for fifty years until a slave wakes and angers a dragon by stealing a jewelled cup from its lair; the dragon burns the Geats' homes and lands, Beowulf resolves to fight it himself, and when it wounds him fatally, Wiglaf strikes with his sword and Beowulf kills it with his dagger."
      }

    {
      "id": "the-dragon-beowulf-c02",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-beowulf-s1",
      "quote": "Beowulf is the first piece of English literature to present a dragonslayer.       "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Beowulf adalah karya sastra Inggris pertama yang menampilkan pembunuh naga; walau naganya memuat banyak motif umum tradisi Jermanik, penyair Beowulf yang pertama memadukannya menjadi naga penyembur api yang khas, dan naga ini diadaptasi untuk Dunia Tengah dalam The Hobbit (1937) karya J. R. R. Tolkien.",
        "en": "Beowulf is the first English work to present a dragonslayer; though its dragon shows many common Germanic motifs, the poet was the first to combine them into a distinctive fire-breathing dragon, which was adapted for Middle-earth in J. R. R. Tolkien's The Hobbit (1937)."
      }

    {

      "source_id": "the-dragon-beowulf-s1",
      "quote": "The legend of the dragonslayer already existed in Norse sagas such as the tale of Sigurd and Fáfnir, and the Beowulf poet incorporates motifs and themes common to dragonlore in the poem.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda pembunuh naga sudah ada dalam saga Nordik seperti kisah Sigurd dan Fáfnir, dan penyair Beowulf memasukkan motif serta tema umum kisah naga ke dalam puisinya.",
        "en": "The dragonslayer legend already existed in Norse sagas such as the tale of Sigurd and Fáfnir, and the Beowulf poet incorporates common dragonlore motifs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "the-dragon-beowulf-c04",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-beowulf-s1",
      "quote": "The fight with the dragon symbolizes Beowulf's stand against evil and destruction, and, as the hero, he knows that failure will bring destruction to his people after many years of peace.       "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertarungan dengan naga melambangkan perlawanan Beowulf terhadap kejahatan dan kehancuran; naga itu sendiri bertindak sebagai \"raja emas\" tiruan yang menganggap serangan terhadap kerajaan Beowulf sebagai balasan pantas atas pencurian satu piala.",
        "en": "The fight symbolises Beowulf's stand against evil and destruction; the dragon acts as a mock 'goldking', treating an attack on Beowulf's kingdom as fit retribution for the theft of a single cup."
      }

    {
      "id": "the-dragon-beowulf-c05",
      "source_id": "the-dragon-beowulf-s2",
      "quote": "Un dragon (draca ou wyrm en vieil anglais) est le troisième et dernier adversaire du héros Beowulf dans le poème épique anglo-saxon Beowulf. Il en possède toutes les caractéristiques : c'est un reptile maléfique, capable de voler et de ramper, qui crache du feu et dont la morsure est empoisonnée.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Naga (draca atau wyrm dalam bahasa Inggris Kuno) adalah lawan ketiga dan terakhir Beowulf dalam epos Anglo-Saxon itu, salah satu contoh awal naga Eropa dalam sastra: reptil jahat yang bisa terbang dan merayap, menyembur api, dan gigitannya berbisa.",
        "en": "The dragon (Old English draca or wyrm) is the third and last foe of Beowulf in the Anglo-Saxon epic, one of the earliest European literary dragons: an evil reptile able to fly and crawl, breathing fire, with a poisonous bite."

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pencurian itu membuat murka naga yang telah tidur di atas harta itu selama tiga ratus tahun, dan ia keluar dari sarangnya untuk menebar kehancuran di kerajaan Geat sebagai balasan.",
        "en": "The theft enraged the dragon, which had slept on the treasure for three hundred years, and it left its lair to wreak destruction on the Geats' kingdom in revenge."

    "id": "Trow atau drow adalah peri atau roh jahil dan jahat dalam cerita rakyat kepulauan Orkney dan Shetland, makhluk malam yang tinggal di gundukan tanah dan gemar musik.",
    "en": "The trow or drow is a malign or mischievous fairy or spirit of Orkney and Shetland folklore, a night creature living in earthen mounds and fond of music.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Trow atau drow (juga trowe atau dtrow) adalah peri atau roh yang jahat atau jahil dalam tradisi cerita rakyat kepulauan Orkney dan Shetland, di utara Skotlandia. Kadang trow dipandang sebagai raksasa mengerikan, kadang sebaliknya sebagai peri bertubuh pendek yang berpakaian kelabu. Ia sering dibandingkan dengan peri dan mungkin berasal dari selkie atau troll Nordik.",
      "en": "The trow or drow (also trowe or dtrow) is a malign or mischievous fairy or spirit in the folk traditions of Orkney and Shetland, north of Scotland. Trows are sometimes thought of as monstrous giants, sometimes as the opposite, short fairies dressed in grey. They are often compared with fairies and may derive from selkies or the Norse troll.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "trow-folklore-c01",
        "trow-folklore-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seperti troll dalam legenda Skandinavia, trow adalah makhluk malam: mereka keluar dari \"trowie knowes\", gundukan tanah tempat tinggal mereka, hanya pada malam hari, dan sering masuk ke rumah saat penghuninya tidur. Mereka sangat menyukai musik, dan kisah-kisah rakyat menceritakan kebiasaan mereka menculik para pemusik atau memikat mereka ke sarang.",
      "en": "Like the troll of Scandinavian legend, trows are creatures of the night: they leave their 'trowie knowes', earthen mound dwellings, only in the evening, and often enter houses while the occupants sleep. Music delights them, and folk stories say they would carry off fiddlers and other players or tempt them into their dwellings.",
      "claim_ids": [

      "id": "Kata trow dan drow dalam bahasa Scots kepulauan diwarisi dari Norn, dialek Nordik Kuno di Kepulauan Utara.       "en": "Insular Scots trow and drow are inherited from Norn, the Old Norse dialect of the Northern Isles.
