      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    },
    {
      "id": "trow-folklore-s2",
      "url": "https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trow",
      "title": "Trow",
      "author": null,
      "publisher": "Wikipedia (fr)",
      "published": null,
      "language": "fr",
      "type": "wikipedia",
      "accessed": "2026-10-02"
    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "trow-folklore-c01",
      "source_id": "trow-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "or drow (also trowe, or dtrow) is a malignant or mischievous fairy or spirit in the folkloric traditions of the Orkney and Shetland islands. Trows may be regarded as monstrous giants at times, or quite the opposite, short-statured fairies dressed in grey.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Trow atau drow (juga trowe atau dtrow) adalah peri atau roh jahat atau jahil dalam tradisi cerita rakyat kepulauan Orkney dan Shetland; kadang dipandang raksasa mengerikan, kadang sebaliknya peri pendek berpakaian kelabu.",
        "en": "The trow or drow (also trowe or dtrow) is a malign or mischievous fairy or spirit in Orkney and Shetland folk tradition; sometimes seen as monstrous giants, sometimes as short fairies dressed in grey."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trow-folklore-c02",
      "source_id": "trow-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Trows are nocturnal creatures, like the troll of Scandinavian legend with which the trow shares many similarities. They venture out of their 'trowie knowes' (earthen mound dwellings) solely in the evening, and often enter households as the inhabitants sleep. Trows traditionally have a fondness for music, and folktales tell of their habit of kidnapping musicians or luring them to their dens.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Trow adalah makhluk malam seperti troll legenda Skandinavia; mereka keluar dari \"trowie knowes\" (gundukan tanah tempat tinggal) hanya di malam hari, sering masuk rumah saat penghuni tidur, menyukai musik, dan menurut kisah rakyat menculik atau memikat pemusik ke sarang mereka.",
        "en": "Trows are nocturnal, like the Scandinavian troll; they leave their 'trowie knowes' (earthen mound dwellings) only in the evening, often enter houses as people sleep, love music, and in folktales kidnap or lure musicians to their dens."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trow-folklore-c03",
      "source_id": "trow-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "Insular Scots trow and drow are inherited words from Norn, the Old Norse dialect spoken in the Northern Isles before being driven out by Scots. The form drow derives from an unrecorded Norn: *drau (or thereof), from Old Norse: draugr , an old word for revenant, devil, troll, and thereof.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata trow dan drow dalam bahasa Scots kepulauan diwarisi dari Norn, dialek Nordik Kuno di Kepulauan Utara; bentuk drow berasal dari Norn *drau yang tak tercatat, dari Nordik Kuno draugr, kata lama untuk mayat hidup, setan, atau troll.",
        "en": "Insular Scots trow and drow are inherited from Norn, the Old Norse dialect of the Northern Isles; drow derives from an unrecorded Norn *drau, from Old Norse draugr, an old word for revenant, devil or troll."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trow-folklore-c04",
      "source_id": "trow-folklore-s1",
      "quote": "The form trow is thought to stem from L-vocalization of troll (\"troll\"), and then intermixing with drow via linguistic and figurative convergence. ... The trow [trʌu] , in the Scots language, is defined as a \"sprite or fairy\" of mischievous nature",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bentuk trow diduga berasal dari kata troll yang bunyi L-nya menjadi vokal, lalu bercampur dengan drow; dalam bahasa Scots, trow didefinisikan sebagai \"roh atau peri\" yang jahil.",
        "en": "The form trow is thought to come from troll with a vocalised L, then merged with drow; in Scots the trow is defined as a mischievous 'sprite or fairy'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "trow-folklore-c05",
      "source_id": "trow-folklore-s2",
      "quote": "Un trow ou drow est une créature surnaturelle issue des croyances et du folklore des îles Orcades et Shetland, au nord de l'Écosse. Les trows sont souvent comparées aux fées (faeries) et ils dérivent peut-être des selkies ou bien du troll nordique.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Trow atau drow adalah makhluk gaib dari kepercayaan dan cerita rakyat kepulauan Orkney dan Shetland di utara Skotlandia, sering dibandingkan dengan peri dan mungkin berasal dari selkie atau troll Nordik.",
        "en": "The trow or drow is a supernatural creature of the beliefs and folklore of Orkney and Shetland, north of Scotland, often compared with fairies and perhaps derived from selkies or the Norse troll."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

    {
      "id": "Bøyg (bahasa Norwegia: Bøygen), juga disebut \"Bøyg Besar dari Etnedal\", adalah makhluk legenda dalam cerita rakyat Skandinavia, troll besar dari Telemark dan Gudbrandsdalen. Ia biasanya digambarkan sebagai ular raksasa berlendir yang menghadang para pengembara, dan dapat juga tak terlihat; ia berusaha menghalangi jalan dan merusak urusan orang.",
      "en": "The Bøyg (Norwegian Bøygen), also called the 'Great Bøyg of Etnedal', is a legendary creature of Scandinavian folklore, a great troll from Telemark and Gudbrandsdalen. It is usually described as a giant slimy serpent that stands in travellers' way, and it may also be invisible; it tries to block people and spoil their doings.",

Secara kiasan, bøyg juga dipakai untuk hambatan besar yang tak jelas bentuknya. Kisah serupa dikenal pula dari Denmark dan Swedia.",
      "en": "Its name means 'bend', 'twist' or 'curve'; Old Norse baugr meant 'ring' or 'curve' and could be a poetic term for 'serpent'. Figuratively, bøyg is also used for a large, ill-defined obstacle. Similar stories are known from Denmark and Sweden too.",

    {
      "id": "b-yg-c02",
      "source_id": "b-yg-s1",
      "quote": "It is a great troll from Telemark and Gudbrandsdalen. It is commonly characterized as a giant, slimy serpent which stands as a hindrance to travellers. The name means 'bend', 'twist' or 'curve'. It appears in the fairy-tale of Per Gynt, the basis for Henrik Ibsen's play Peer Gynt.",

      "source_id": "b-yg-s2",
      "quote": "Bøyg, ofte omtalt i bestemt form Bøygen, det vil si «den som er bøyd», er et overnaturlig vesen eller troll som opptrer i form av en kjempeslange i norsk folketro. Skikkelsen, som også kan være usynlig, prøver å stille seg i veien og ødelegge for folk. I dag er figuren mest kjent fra Henrik Ibsens skuespill Peer Gynt fra 1867.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bøyg, sering disebut Bøygen (\"yang membungkuk\"), adalah makhluk gaib atau troll berwujud ular raksasa dalam kepercayaan rakyat Norwegia; ia bisa juga tak terlihat, berusaha menghalangi dan merusak urusan orang, dan kini paling dikenal dari drama Peer Gynt (1867) karya Henrik Ibsen.",
        "en": "Bøyg, often called Bøygen ('the bent one'), is a supernatural being or troll in the form of a giant serpent in Norwegian folk belief; it can be invisible, tries to block and spoil people's ways, and is best known today from Henrik Ibsen's 1867 play Peer Gynt."
      }

    {
      "id": "b-yg-c04",
      "source_id": "b-yg-s2",
      "quote": "Bøygen kan være et troll eller en kjempeslange kjent fra sagn og eventyr fra Telemark og Gudbrandsdalen, blant annet fra Asbjørnsens historier om storskytteren Per Gynt. Liknende historier er også kjent fra Danmark og Sverige.",

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bøygen bisa berupa troll atau ular raksasa yang dikenal dari legenda dan dongeng Telemark dan Gudbrandsdalen, antara lain dari kisah Asbjørnsen tentang pemburu ulung Per Gynt; kisah serupa juga dikenal dari Denmark dan Swedia.",
        "en": "Bøygen may be a troll or giant serpent known from legends and tales of Telemark and Gudbrandsdalen, including Asbjørnsen's stories of the great hunter Per Gynt; similar stories are known from Denmark and Sweden."

    {
      "id": "b-yg-c05",
      "source_id": "b-yg-s2",
      "quote": "Det norrøne ordet baugr betød «ring» eller «krumning», men kunne også brukes som en omskrivning for «orm». Ordene bøyg og Bøygen kan i overført betydning også brukes om en forhindring, gjerne stor og ubestemmelig, eller et problem.",

      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Old Norse baugr meant 'ring' or 'curve' but could also be a kenning for 'serpent'; bøyg and Bøygen are used figuratively for a large, vague obstacle or problem."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Drude adalah roh malam perempuan yang jahat dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, sejenis Alp atau nenek sihir yang duduk di dada orang tidur dan menimbulkan mimpi buruk, terutama di Jerman selatan.",
    "en": "The drude is a malevolent female night spirit of German folklore, a kind of Alp or witch-hag that sits on sleepers' chests and causes nightmares, especially in southern Germany.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "drude-c01",
      "drude-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, drude, juga trude, adalah roh malam perempuan yang jahat, sejenis Alp atau nenek sihir yang dikaitkan dengan mimpi buruk dan terutama dikenal di Jerman selatan. Ia duduk di dada orang yang tidur dan menimbulkan mimpi buruk, rasa tertekan, dan sesak napas; dalam tradisi Bayern kemudian, ada pula pasangan lelakinya yang disebut Truder atau Truderer.",
      "en": "In German folklore the drude, also trude, is a malevolent female night spirit, a kind of Alp or witch-hag linked with nightmares and known especially in southern Germany. She sits on sleepers' chests causing nightmares, oppression and breathlessness; later Bavarian tradition also gave her male counterparts called Truder or Truderer.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "drude-c01",
        "drude-c03",
        "drude-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kata ini tercatat sebagai trute dalam bahasa Jerman Hulu Pertengahan akhir dan paling mungkin berkaitan dengan kata Nordik Kuno troða, \"menginjak, menginjak-injak\"; pada abad ke-16 trute juga dipakai sebagai sebutan umum untuk penyihir. Menurut sebagian cerita, drude adalah penyihir yang dapat mengubah ukurannya, masuk rumah sebagai makhluk mungil atau menjadi raksasa, dan hanya rohnya yang keluar dari tubuh untuk menyiksa orang tidur.",
      "en": "The word is attested as trute in late Middle High German and most plausibly relates to Old Norse troða, 'to tread, trample'; in the 16th century trute was also a general word for 'witch'. In some lore the drude is a witch able to change size, entering houses as a tiny being or growing into a giantess, and only her spirit leaves the body to torment sleepers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "drude-c02",
        "drude-c03",
        "drude-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Di Jerman selatan dan Austria, drude adalah perempuan yang dikutuk mencari korban setiap malam, manusia, hewan, bahkan pohon dan batu; rohnya masuk lewat celah dan lubang kunci dalam rupa kucing, sedotan, atau bulu. Penangkalnya antara lain tanda Drudenfuß, yang konon berasal dari jejak kakinya yang mirip kaki burung, serta Drudenstein, batu kerikil berlubang yang digantung di loteng.",
      "en": "In southern Germany and Austria the drude is a woman condemned to seek a victim every night, people, animals, even trees and stones; her soul enters through cracks and keyholes in the form of a cat, a straw or a feather. Protections include the Drudenfuß sign, said to come from her bird-like footprint, and the Drudenstein, a holed pebble hung in the roof.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "drude-c01",
      "source_id": "drude-s1",
      "quote": "is a kind of malevolent female nocturnal spirit in Germanic mythology, a sort of alp or witch-hag associated with nightmares, prevalent especially in Southern Germany. It also occurs in the form or trude .",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, drude adalah sejenis roh malam perempuan yang jahat dalam mitologi Jermanik, semacam Alp atau nenek sihir yang dikaitkan dengan mimpi buruk, terutama di Jerman selatan, dan juga disebut trude.",
        "en": "In German folklore the drude is a malevolent female nocturnal spirit of Germanic mythology, a sort of Alp or witch-hag tied to nightmares, prevalent in southern Germany, also called trude."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drude-c02",
      "source_id": "drude-s1",
      "quote": "The word is attested as trute in late Middle High German. ... The word trute also came to be used as a generic term for \"witch\" in the 16th century (Hans Sachs).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata ini tercatat sebagai trute dalam bahasa Jerman Hulu Pertengahan akhir, dan pada abad ke-16 trute juga dipakai sebagai sebutan umum untuk \"penyihir\".",
        "en": "The word is attested as trute in late Middle High German, and in the 16th century trute also became a general term for 'witch'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drude-c03",
      "source_id": "drude-s1",
      "quote": "Middle High German trute is etymologically related to Old Norse troða (\"to tread, to trample\"), i.e., is related to modern German verb treten (\"to tread, to step\"); this is considered the most plausible etymology in modern philological opinion. ... In later Bavarian folklore tradition, the female Trude had male counterparts called Truder , Trudner , or Truderer",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kata Jerman Hulu Pertengahan trute berkaitan dengan Nordik Kuno troða (\"menginjak, menginjak-injak\") dan kata Jerman modern treten, etimologi yang dianggap paling masuk akal; dalam tradisi Bayern kemudian, Trude perempuan punya pasangan lelaki bernama Truder, Trudner, atau Truderer.",
        "en": "Middle High German trute relates to Old Norse troða ('to tread, trample') and German treten, the etymology deemed most plausible; in later Bavarian tradition the female Trude had male counterparts called Truder, Trudner or Truderer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drude-c04",
      "source_id": "drude-s1",
      "quote": "Drude is held responsible for causing the nightmare Alpdruck (\"elf pressure\"), thus considered just a subtype of an Alp, as far as its attack behavior is concerned. According to some folklore, the drude is a witch who can assume various sizes, small or large ... It is also said that only the witch's spirit drifts out of body to perform their handiwork tormenting people (in their sleep).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drude dianggap penyebab mimpi buruk Alpdruck (\"tekanan elf\") sehingga dalam cara menyerang dipandang subtipe Alp; menurut sebagian cerita ia penyihir yang bisa berukuran kecil atau besar, dan konon hanya rohnya yang keluar dari tubuh untuk menyiksa orang yang tidur.",
        "en": "The drude is blamed for the Alpdruck nightmare ('elf pressure') and so counted a subtype of the Alp in attack; in some lore she is a witch who can be small or large, and only her spirit leaves the body to torment sleepers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drude-c05",
      "source_id": "drude-s2",
      "quote": "Eine Drude (auch Drute, Trude, Trute, Trut, männlich Drud, Druder, Drudner, Trutner oder bairisch Truderer) ist ein Wesen des Volksglaubens, das sich nachts auf die Brust von Schlafenden setzt und Albträume, Beklemmungen und Atemnot verursacht. ... sowie im oldenburgischen Raum Walrider, Walriderske.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drude (juga Drute, Trude, Trute; lelaki Druder atau dalam bahasa Bayern Truderer) adalah makhluk kepercayaan rakyat yang pada malam hari duduk di dada orang tidur dan menimbulkan mimpi buruk, rasa tertekan, dan sesak napas; di Oldenburg ia disebut Walrider atau Walriderske.",
        "en": "The Drude (also Drute, Trude, Trute; male Druder or Bavarian Truderer) is a folk-belief being that sits on sleepers' chests at night causing nightmares, oppression and breathlessness; in the Oldenburg area it is called Walrider or Walriderske."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drude-c06",
      "source_id": "drude-s2",
      "quote": "Hier sind Frauen, die dazu verdammt sind, sich jede Nacht ein Opfer zum Drücken suchen zu müssen: Erwachsene, Kinder und Haustiere werden heimgesucht, aber auch Bäume und Steine. ... kann als Geist durch kleinste Spalten und Schlüssellöcher in ein Zimmer dringen und dann in verschiedener Gestalt erscheinen (z. B. als Katze, Strohhalm oder Feder).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di Jerman selatan dan Austria, drude adalah perempuan yang dikutuk mencari korban setiap malam, orang dewasa, anak, hewan, juga pohon dan batu; rohnya masuk lewat celah dan lubang kunci dan tampil dalam berbagai rupa seperti kucing, sedotan, atau bulu.",
        "en": "As drudes these pressing spirits are widespread in southern Germany and Austria: women condemned to find a victim each night, adults, children, animals, even trees and stones; their soul leaves the body, slips through cracks and keyholes and appears as a cat, straw or feather."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drude-c07",
      "source_id": "drude-s2",
      "quote": "Das Zauberzeichen Drudenfuß, auch Drudenkreuz genannt, soll gegen sie schützen. Der Ursprung dieses Zeichens liegt der Sage nach im vogelartigen Fußabdruck einer Drude.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tanda gaib Drudenfuß, juga disebut Drudenkreuz, konon melindungi dari drude; menurut sage, tanda ini berasal dari jejak kaki drude yang mirip kaki burung.",
        "en": "The magic sign Drudenfuß, also Drudenkreuz, is said to protect against them; by legend it comes from a drude's bird-like footprint."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drude-c08",
      "source_id": "drude-s2",
      "quote": "Ebenfalls abwehrende Wirkung sollten Drudensteine (auch Hühnergott genannt) haben, die Kieselsteine mit einem natürlichen Loch, dem sogenannten Auge sind. Diese wurden zur Abwehr mit einem Band oder einer Schnur im Dachstuhl des Hauses aufgehängt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drudenstein (juga Hühnergott), kerikil dengan lubang alami yang disebut \"mata\", juga dipercaya menangkal drude dan digantung dengan tali di rangka atap rumah.",
        "en": "Drudensteine (also Hühnergott), pebbles with a natural hole called the 'eye', were also believed to ward drudes off and were hung by a cord in the roof."
      }
    }
  ],

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ekke Nekkepenn adalah tokoh legenda Frisia Utara, digambarkan sebagai lelaki laut yang tinggal bersama istrinya Rahn di dasar Laut Utara dan mengusili para pelaut serta penduduk pulau.",
    "en": "Ekke Nekkepenn is a figure of North Frisian legend, pictured as a sea-dwelling man who shares the North Sea floor with his wife Rahn and teases sailors and islanders.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ekke-nekkepenn-c01",
      "ekke-nekkepenn-c06"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ekke Nekkepenn (juga Eke Nekepen dan ejaan lain) adalah tokoh legenda Frisia Utara. Pada pertengahan abad ke-19, peneliti lokal, ahli folklor, dan seniman grafis Christian Peter Hansen menggambarkannya sebagai lelaki laut yang tinggal bersama istrinya Rahn di dasar Laut Utara dan mengusili para pelaut serta penduduk kepulauan Frisia Utara. Dalam satu uraian ia disebut padanan Ægir dalam banyak hal.",
      "en": "Ekke Nekkepenn (also Eke Nekepen and other spellings) is a figure of North Frisian legend. Christian Peter Hansen, a Sylt folklorist, local historian and printmaker of the mid-1800s, cast him as a merman sharing the North Sea floor with his wife Rahn and teasing sailors and the people of the North Frisian Islands. One account calls him a counterpart of Ægir in many respects.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ekke-nekkepenn-c01",
        "ekke-nekkepenn-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah Hansen, Ekke Nekkepenn meminta istri seorang kapten Sylt, yang kapalnya diterjang badai dalam perjalanan ke Inggris, membantu persalinan anaknya; setelah berhasil, perempuan itu kembali ke permukaan dengan hadiah emas dan perak. Bertahun-tahun kemudian, karena Rahn telah \"tua dan keriput\", ia ingin menjadikan istri kapten itu istrinya dan menyuruh Rahn menggiling garam di dasar laut hingga pusarannya menenggelamkan kapal Sylt.",
      "en": "In Hansen's tale Ekke Nekkepenn asks the wife of a Sylt captain, whose England-bound ship is caught in a storm, to help with his child's birth; afterwards she returns to the surface with gifts of gold and silver. Years later, since Rahn has grown 'old and wrinkly', he wants the captain's wife for himself and has Rahn grind salt on the seabed until the whirlpool drags the Sylt ship down.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ekke-nekkepenn-c03",
        "ekke-nekkepenn-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam versi lain, ia jatuh cinta kepada gadis dari Rantum yang dipaksa menerimanya, lalu berjanji membebaskannya bila ia bisa menyebut nama lengkapnya; gadis itu akhirnya mendengar nyanyian dari bukit pasir yang menyebut nama Ekke Nekkepenn. Theodor Storm memasukkan sosok peri api bernama Eckeneckepenn yang membuat ladang layu dalam kisahnya Die Regentrude.",
      "en": "In another version he falls in love with a girl from Rantum who is forced to accept him, then promises her freedom if she can name him in full; she finally overhears a song from the dunes revealing the name Ekke Nekkepenn. Theodor Storm put a little fire-man called Eckeneckepenn, who withers the fields, into his tale Die Regentrude.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ekke-nekkepenn-c02",
        "ekke-nekkepenn-c05",
        "ekke-nekkepenn-c06"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "is a North Frisian legendary figure.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ekke Nekkepenn (juga Eke Nekepen) adalah tokoh legenda Frisia Utara; pada pertengahan abad ke-19 peneliti lokal, ahli folklor, dan seniman grafis Christian Peter Hansen menggambarkannya sebagai lelaki laut yang tinggal bersama istrinya Rahn di dasar Laut Utara dan mengusili pelaut serta penduduk kepulauan Frisia Utara.",
        "en": "Ekke Nekkepenn (also Eke Nekepen) is a North Frisian legendary figure; in the mid-19th century the local researcher, folklorist and artist Christian Peter Hansen portrayed him as a merman living with his wife Rahn on the North Sea floor and playing tricks on seafarers and North Frisian islanders."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ekke-nekkepenn-c02",
      "source_id": "ekke-nekkepenn-s2",
      "quote": "In Theodor Storms 1866 veröffentlichter Novelle Die Regentrude taucht ein Feuermännlein mit dem Namen Eckeneckepenn auf, das mit seinem Schadenzauber für das Verdorren der Felder sorgt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam novel Theodor Storm Die Regentrude yang terbit 1866, muncul peri api kecil bernama Eckeneckepenn yang dengan sihir jahatnya membuat ladang layu.",
        "en": "In Theodor Storm's novella Die Regentrude, published in 1866, a little fire-man called Eckeneckepenn appears who withers the fields with harmful magic."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ekke-nekkepenn-c03",
      "source_id": "ekke-nekkepenn-s2",
      "quote": "Die Erzählung beginnt damit, dass Ekke Nekkepenn die Frau des Kapitäns eines im Sturm nach England laufenden Sylter Schiffes um Hilfe bei der Geburt seines Kindes bittet. Die schöne und hilfsbereite Kapitänsfrau wird vom Meermann zu seiner auf dem Grunde der Nordsee lebenden Frau Rahn geführt und kommt nach gelungener Geburt reichbeschenkt mit Gold und Silber an die Meeresoberfläche zurück.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisahnya bermula ketika Ekke Nekkepenn meminta istri kapten kapal Sylt yang diterjang badai menuju Inggris membantu persalinan anaknya; perempuan itu dibawa ke istrinya Rahn di dasar Laut Utara dan, setelah persalinan berhasil, kembali ke permukaan dengan hadiah emas dan perak.",
        "en": "The tale begins with Ekke Nekkepenn asking the wife of the captain of a storm-tossed Sylt ship bound for England to help with his child's birth; she is led to his wife Rahn on the North Sea floor and, after a successful birth, returns to the surface richly gifted with gold and silver."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ekke-nekkepenn-c04",
      "source_id": "ekke-nekkepenn-s1",
      "quote": "Several years later, Ekke Nekkepenn remembered this incident and decides – now that Rahn has become \"old and wrinkly\" – to take the captain's wife as his own. When he sees the Rantumer Captain's ship one day, he persuades Rahn, who's sitting on the seabed, to grind salt, and the resulting powerful vortex pulls down the Sylt ship",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bertahun-tahun kemudian, karena Rahn telah \"tua dan keriput\", Ekke Nekkepenn ingin mengambil istri kapten itu; saat melihat kapal kapten dari Rantum, ia membujuk Rahn yang duduk di dasar laut untuk menggiling garam, dan pusaran kuat yang timbul menenggelamkan kapal Sylt itu.",
        "en": "Years later, with Rahn now 'old and wrinkly', Ekke Nekkepenn decides to take the captain's wife; seeing the Rantum captain's ship, he gets Rahn on the seabed to grind salt, and the strong vortex pulls the Sylt ship down."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ekke-nekkepenn-c05",
      "source_id": "ekke-nekkepenn-s3",
      "quote": "Ekke blev forelsket i en ung pige fra Rantum, som blev tvunget til at give ham jaordet. Senere fortrød hun sit ja. Ekke lovede hende omsider, at hvis hun indenfor den næste aften kunne nævne ham ved hans fulde navn, så skulle hun få sin frihed igen. Men ingen kendte navnet, indtil hun ved stranden hørte en sang, der lød ud fra klitterne:",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ekke jatuh cinta kepada gadis muda dari Rantum yang dipaksa menerimanya; ketika gadis itu menyesal, Ekke berjanji membebaskannya bila sebelum malam berikutnya ia dapat menyebut nama lengkapnya, dan tak seorang pun tahu nama itu sampai ia mendengar nyanyian dari bukit pasir di pantai.",
        "en": "Ekke fell in love with a young girl from Rantum who was forced to say yes; when she regretted it, he promised her freedom if by the next evening she could name him in full, and no one knew the name until she heard a song from the dunes by the beach."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ekke-nekkepenn-c06",
      "source_id": "ekke-nekkepenn-s3",
      "quote": "Ekke Nekkepenn (på førfrisisk Eke Nekepen) er en nordfrisisk sagnfigur. Ifølge sagnet lever figuren sammen med sin kone Ran på bunden af Vesterhavet og driller søfarere og beboerne på de nordfrisiske øer. Han modsvarer på mange punkter Ægir. ... Stoffet blev også indarbejdet i Theodor Storms novelle Die Regentrude fra 1863.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ekke Nekkepenn (Frisia Föhr: Eke Nekepen) adalah tokoh legenda Frisia Utara yang menurut sage tinggal bersama istrinya Ran di dasar Laut Utara dan mengusili pelaut serta penduduk pulau; dalam banyak hal ia padanan Ægir, dan kisahnya juga diolah dalam novel Theodor Storm Die Regentrude dari 1863.",
        "en": "Ekke Nekkepenn (Föhr Frisian Eke Nekepen) is a North Frisian legendary figure who by legend lives with his wife Ran on the North Sea floor teasing seafarers and islanders; he corresponds in many ways to Ægir, and the material was also worked into Theodor Storm's novella Die Regentrude of 1863."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Erlking (Erlkönig) adalah elf jahat dalam cerita rakyat Eropa yang berkeliaran di hutan dan membunuh anak-anak dengan sekali sentuh; ia terkenal lewat puisi Goethe.",
    "en": "The Erlking (Erlkönig) is a sinister elf of European folklore who haunts the woods and kills children with a single touch; he is famous from Goethe's poem.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "erlking-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat dan mitos Eropa, Erlking adalah elf jahat yang berdiam di hutan; ia mengintai anak-anak yang terlalu lama di hutan dan membunuh mereka dengan sekali sentuh. Nama \"Erlking\" (Jerman: Erlkönig, harfiah \"raja pohon alder\") dipakai dalam Romantisisme Jerman untuk roh atau \"raja para peri\", dan umumnya dianggap turunan dari ellekonge, \"raja elf\", dalam cerita rakyat Denmark.",
      "en": "Woodland is the haunt of the Erlking, a malevolent elf of European myth and folklore who watches for children lingering too long among the trees and slays them with one touch. German Romanticism used 'Erlking' (German Erlkönig, literally 'alder-king') for a spirit or 'king of the fairies', and the name is commonly traced to the ellekonge, 'elf-king', of Danish folk tradition.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "erlking-c01",
        "erlking-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama ini pertama kali dipakai Johann Gottfried Herder dalam baladanya Erlkönigs Tochter (1778), saduran balada rakyat Denmark Hr. Oluf han rider (1739), lalu diambil Johann Wolfgang von Goethe dalam puisinya Erlkönig (1782) yang digubah menjadi musik oleh Schubert. Goethe memberi makna baru, karena \"Erl\" berarti pohon alder hitam, bukan elf. Menurut Jacob Grimm, istilah ini berasal dari kata Skandinavia ellekonge, \"raja elf\".",
      "en": "Johann Gottfried Herder introduced the name in his 1778 ballad Erlkönigs Tochter, a reworking of the 1739 Danish folk ballad Hr. Oluf han rider; Johann Wolfgang von Goethe then used it for his 1782 poem Erlkönig, later set to music by Schubert. Goethe gave it a new sense, since 'Erl' means black alder, not elf.       "claim_ids": [

        "erlking-c04"

    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah yang diceritakan Herder, Sir Oluf berkuda menuju pernikahannya tetapi terpikat musik para elf; putri raja elf mengajaknya menari, dan ketika ia menolak tawaran serta hadiah emasnya, sang elf memukulnya hingga jatuh.       "en": "In the tale as Herder told it, Sir Oluf rides to his wedding but is drawn by elfin music; the elf-king's daughter invites him to dance, and when he refuses her offer and her gifts of gold she strikes him down.   "short_description": {
    "id": "Fjalar adalah kurcaci dalam mitologi Nordik yang bersama saudaranya Galar membunuh Kvasir yang bijak dan membuat madu puisi dari darahnya.",
    "en": "Fjalar is a dwarf of Norse mythology who with his brother Galar killed the wise Kvasir and made the mead of poetry from his blood.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fjalar-q11969058-c01",

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fjalar (Nordik Kuno Fjalarr, dari fela, \"menyembunyikan\"; \"si Penyembunyi\") adalah nama seorang kurcaci dalam mitologi Nordik. Menurut Edda karya Snorri, ia bersama saudaranya Galar membunuh Kvasir yang bijak dan dari darahnya membuat madu puisi, minuman para skald yang kemudian dicuri raksasa Suttung.",
      "en": "Fjalar (Old Norse Fjalarr, from fela, 'to hide'; 'the Hider') is the name of a dwarf in Norse mythology. According to Snorri's Edda he and his brother Galar killed the wise Kvasir and made from his blood the mead of poetry, the skalds' drink later stolen by the giant Suttung.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fjalar-q11969058-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Nama Fjalar, yang berarti \"penipu\" atau \"penyembunyi\", disandang beberapa tokoh mitologi Nordik, dan kurcaci ini yang terpenting. Völuspá mencantumkannya dalam daftar kurcaci, dan Hávamál menyebut \"Fjalar yang Bijak\", yang birnya membuat sang penutur, mungkin Odin, mabuk; bila itu merujuk madu puisi, Fjalar tersebut mungkin sama dengan kurcaci pembuat madu ini.",
      "en": "The name Fjalar, meaning 'deceiver' or 'hider', is borne by several figures of Norse mythology, and this dwarf is the most important. The Völuspá lists him among the dwarfs, and the Hávamál speaks of 'Fjalar the Wise', whose ale makes the narrator, probably Odin, drunk; if that refers to the mead of poetry, this Fjalar may be the same as the mead-making dwarf.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fjalar-q11969058-c01",
        "fjalar-q11969058-c03"
      ]
    }

      "quote": "Fjalar – imię kilku postaci w mitologii nordyckiej, oznaczające „oszusta” lub „ukrywającego”. ... Kolejną, najważniejszą z postaci o tym imieniu, był karzeł, brat Galara. Razem z bratem zabili Kvasera, a z jego krwi stworzyli miód poezji.",

        "en": "Fjalar is the name of several Norse mythological figures, meaning 'deceiver' or 'hider' (oszusta, ukrywającego); the most important is the dwarf (karzeł), brother of Galar, who with him killed Kvasir (Kvasera) and made the mead of poetry (miód poezji) from his blood."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Kyrië adalah raja para kabouter (gnom) dalam cerita rakyat Kempen, Brabant Utara, Belanda; setelah ia ditembak seorang pemburu, semua kabouter meninggalkan wilayah itu.",
    "en": "Kyrië is the king of the kabouters (gnomes) in the folklore of the Campine, North Brabant, the Netherlands; after a hunter shot him, all the gnomes left the region.",
    "claim_ids": [

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat setempat, Raja Kurcaci Kyrië (Kabouterkoning Kyrië) adalah pemimpin kabouter legendaris yang tinggal di wilayah Kempen di Brabant Utara, Belanda. Para kabouter berpangkalan di desa Hoogeloon, dan Kyrië sendiri tinggal di Kerkakkers, di Kabouterberg (\"gunung kurcaci\") atau Duivelsberg (\"gunung setan\"), sebuah tumulus.",
      "en": "According to local folklore, Gnome King Kyrië (Kabouterkoning Kyrië) was the leader of the legendary kabouters of the Campine in North Brabant, the Netherlands. The gnomes were based in the village of Hoogeloon, and Kyrië himself lived on the Kerkakkers in the Kabouterberg ('gnome mountain') or Duivelsberg ('devil's mountain'), a tumulus.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gnome-king-kyrie-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para kabouter Kempen adalah makhluk penolong yang membantu petani dan rumah tangga pada malam hari dan tak ingin dilihat manusia; siapa yang melihat mereka akan dihukum, seperti petani penasaran yang buta sebelah matanya setelah mengintip. Kyrië ditembak seorang pemburu di padang Riethoven, masih sempat mencapai Duivelsberg, dan pemburu itu mendengar kabouter berseru \"Kyrië sudah mati\"; setelah sang raja dimakamkan di Hoogeloon, semua kabouter pergi ke tempat yang tak diketahui.",
      "en": "The gnomes of the Campine were helpful beings who aided farmers and households at night and did not wish to be seen; those who saw them were punished, like a curious farmer who went blind in one eye after spying. Kyrië was shot by a hunter on the Riethoven heath, managed to reach the Duivelsberg, and the hunter heard the gnomes cry 'Kyrië is dead'; after the king was buried in Hoogeloon, all the gnomes left for an unknown place.",

        "gnome-king-kyrie-c02",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Kyrië ditembak seorang pemburu di padang Riethoven, masih sempat mencapai Duivelsberg; pemburu itu mendengar kabouter yang terkejut berkata \"Kyrië sudah mati\"; sang raja dimakamkan di Hoogeloon, dan setelah itu semua kabouter pergi ke tempat yang tak diketahui.",
        "en": "Kyrië was shot by a hunter on the Riethoven heath, just reaching the Duivelsberg; the hunter heard the shocked gnomes say 'Kyrië is dead'; the king was buried in Hoogeloon, after which all the gnomes left for an unknown place."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gnome-king-kyrie-c04",
      "source_id": "gnome-king-kyrie-s1",
      "quote": "In one version of the story, the king's death is announced by a traveler, a narrative structure that may come from Plutarch.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam satu versi, kematian sang raja diumumkan seorang pengembara, struktur cerita yang mungkin berasal dari Plutarkhos.",
        "en": "In one version the king's death is announced by a traveller, a narrative structure that may come from Plutarch."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gnome-king-kyrie-c05",
      "source_id": "gnome-king-kyrie-s2",
      "quote": "Kabouterkoning Kyrië is een personage dat in meerdere sagen voorkomt en is een gelijknamig standbeeld van Wim Gubbels uit 1985. Het standbeeld bevindt zich op het Valensplein midden in het centrum van de Nederlandse plaats Hoogeloon, wat tevens, volgens de kabouterverhalen, de plaats is waar de kabouterkoning zou zijn begraven.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kabouterkoning Kyrië adalah tokoh dalam beberapa sage sekaligus nama patung karya Wim Gubbels tahun 1985 di Valensplein, Hoogeloon, yang menurut kisah kabouter juga tempat sang raja dimakamkan.",
        "en": "Kabouterkoning Kyrië is a figure in several legends and also a 1985 statue by Wim Gubbels on the Valensplein in Hoogeloon, which by the gnome tales is where the gnome king was buried."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gnome-king-kyrie-c06",
      "source_id": "gnome-king-kyrie-s2",
      "quote": "Op 9 april 2001 werd dit beeld ten gevolge van de herinrichting van het Valensplein verplaatst naar een andere plek op hetzelfde plein. Tegelijkertijd werd ook het speciale 'Koning Kyrië Kruidenbitter' geïntroduceerd.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada 9 April 2001 patung itu dipindahkan ke tempat lain di alun-alun yang sama karena penataan ulang Valensplein, dan sekaligus diperkenalkan minuman khusus \"Koning Kyrië Kruidenbitter\".",
        "en": "On 9 April 2001 the statue was moved elsewhere on the same square because of the Valensplein's redesign, and at the same time a special 'Koning Kyrië Kruidenbitter' was introduced."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

      "field": "images",

    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gonger adalah hantu atau mayat hidup dalam kepercayaan rakyat pulau-pulau Frisia Utara Sylt dan Amrum, arwah orang yang tak tenang di kubur, termasuk pelaut yang mati di laut.",
    "en": "The gonger is a ghost or revenant in the folk belief of the North Frisian islands of Sylt and Amrum, the restless dead, including sailors who died at sea.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gonger-c01",
      "gonger-c02",
      "gonger-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gonger (dari kata Wiedergänger) adalah sebutan untuk hantu dalam kepercayaan rakyat pulau-pulau Frisia Utara, Sylt dan Amrum. Menurut cerita, orang yang dibunuh tanpa salah, serta orang yang memindahkan batu batas atau merusak dan membajak tanah orang, tidak tenang di kuburnya; penghujat, orang yang bunuh diri, dan orang yang mengutuk dirinya sendiri juga kembali sebagai gonger.",
      "en": "Gonger (from Wiedergänger, 'revenant') was the name for a ghost in the folk belief of the North Frisian islands of Sylt and Amrum. According to tales, the innocently murdered, and people who moved boundary stones or ploughed away or ravaged land, find no rest in the grave; blasphemers, suicides and those who cursed themselves also return as gongers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gonger-c01",
        "gonger-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bila seorang pelaut mati di laut, ia kembali sebagai gonger untuk membawa kabar kematiannya. Pada malam hari ia keluar dari laut dengan pakaian yang dikenakannya saat mati, mendatangi rumah keturunannya, memadamkan lampu, berbaring di atas selimut orang yang tidur, dan meninggalkan jejak air asin, terus begitu sampai orang mengingatnya dan kematiannya.",
      "en": "When a sailor dies at sea he returns as a gonger to bring news of his death. At night he rises from the sea in the clothes he died in, visits his descendants' house, puts out the light, lies on a sleeper's blanket and leaves a trail of salt water, doing so until he and his death are remembered.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gonger-c03",
        "gonger-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kepercayaan ini diangkat Hugo Wolfgang Philipp dalam lakon Der Gonger kommt pada 1930-an, dalam film televisi Gonger – Das Böse vergisst nie (2008), dan dalam lagu band folk-rock Versengold, Die Gonger kommen.",
      "en": "The belief was taken up by Hugo Wolfgang Philipp in his 1930s play Der Gonger kommt, in the TV film Gonger – Das Böse vergisst nie (2008) and in the folk-rock band Versengold's song Die Gonger kommen.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gonger-c01",
      "source_id": "gonger-s1",
      "quote": "Als Gonger (von Wiedergänger) bezeichnete man im Volksglauben auf den nordfriesischen Inseln Sylt und Amrum ein Gespenst.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat di pulau-pulau Frisia Utara Sylt dan Amrum, gonger (dari Wiedergänger) adalah sebutan untuk hantu.",
        "en": "In the folk belief of the North Frisian islands of Sylt and Amrum, a ghost was called a gonger (from Wiedergänger)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonger-c02",
      "source_id": "gonger-s1",
      "quote": "Erzählungen zufolge finden „unschuldig Ermordete“ sowie Menschen, die Grundsteine versetzt oder Land abgepflügt bzw. verwüstet haben, keine Ruhe im Grab. Ebenso kehren Gotteslästerer, Selbstmörder und Personen, die sich selbst verflucht haben, als Gonger wieder.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut cerita, orang yang \"dibunuh tanpa salah\" serta orang yang memindahkan batu batas atau membajak dan merusak tanah tidak tenang di kubur; penghujat, orang bunuh diri, dan orang yang mengutuk dirinya juga kembali sebagai gonger.",
        "en": "According to tales the 'innocently murdered' and those who moved boundary stones or ploughed away or ravaged land find no rest in the grave; blasphemers, suicides and self-cursers also return as gongers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonger-c03",
      "source_id": "gonger-s1",
      "quote": "Wenn ein Seemann auf dem Meer stirbt, kehrt er als Gonger zurück, um die Nachricht seines Todes zu überbringen. Dazu steigt er nachts in der Kleidung, die er zum Todeszeitpunkt getragen hat, aus dem Meer, und besucht das Haus seiner Nachkommen. Hier löscht er das Licht, legt sich auf die Decke eines Schlafenden und hinterlässt eine Spur aus Salzwasser.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila pelaut mati di laut, ia kembali sebagai gonger untuk membawa kabar kematiannya: malam hari ia keluar dari laut dengan pakaian saat mati, mendatangi rumah keturunannya, memadamkan lampu, berbaring di atas selimut orang yang tidur, dan meninggalkan jejak air asin.",
        "en": "When a sailor dies at sea he returns as a gonger to bring news of his death: at night he rises from the sea in the clothes he died in, visits his descendants' house, puts out the light, lies on a sleeper's blanket and leaves a trail of salt water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonger-c04",
      "source_id": "gonger-s2",
      "quote": "Gonger betegner på de nordfrisiske øer Amrum og Sild en genganger. Gongere kunne ifølge folketroen være uskyldigt dræbte, men også selvmordere eller gudsbespottere. Også sømænd, der døde på havet, kunne vende tilbage til øen og spøge.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di pulau-pulau Frisia Utara Amrum dan Sild (Sylt), gonger berarti arwah gentayangan; menurut kepercayaan rakyat, gonger bisa orang yang dibunuh tanpa salah, orang bunuh diri, atau penghujat, dan pelaut yang mati di laut juga bisa kembali ke pulau dan menghantui.",
        "en": "On the North Frisian islands of Amrum and Sild (Sylt) gonger means a revenant (genganger); by folk belief gongers could be the innocently killed, suicides or blasphemers, and sailors who died at sea could return to haunt the island."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gonger-c05",
      "source_id": "gonger-s1",
      "quote": "Hugo Wolfgang Philipp verarbeitete in den 1930er Jahren den Glauben in seinem Theaterstück Der Gonger kommt. 2008 entstand der Fernsehfilm Gonger – Das Böse vergisst nie. Musikalisch verarbeitet wurde die Thematik von der Folk-Rock-Band Versengold in dem Lied Die Gonger kommen",

      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Hugo Wolfgang Philipp used the belief in his 1930s play Der Gonger kommt; the TV film Gonger – Das Böse vergisst nie appeared in 2008, and the folk-rock band Versengold took it up in the song Die Gonger kommen."
      }

    "id": "Gulon atau jerff adalah makhluk legenda Skandinavia, campuran berbagai hewan seukuran anjing berkepala kucing, yang menjadi lambang kerakusan.",
    "en": "The gulon or jerff is a creature of Scandinavian legend, a dog-sized blend of animals with a cat-like head, known as a symbol of gluttony.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "gulon-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gulon adalah makhluk dalam legenda Skandinavia yang tampak seperti campuran berbagai hewan: biasanya seukuran dan sebentuk anjing, dengan kepala, telinga, dan cakar seperti kucing, bulu cokelat tebal yang kusut, serta ekor seperti rubah. Dalam legenda Skandinavia ia juga disebut jerff, atau dalam ejaan modern järv; di Jerman makhluk ini dikenal sebagai Vielfraß.",
      "en": "The gulon is a creature of Scandinavian legend that looks like a mix of animals: usually the size and shape of a dog, with a cat-like head, ears and claws, a thick shaggy brown coat and a fox-like tail. In Scandinavian legend it is also called jerff, or in modern spelling järv; in Germany the creature is known as Vielfraß.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gulon-c01",
        "gulon-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Gulon terkenal sebagai lambang kerakusan karena kebiasaan makannya yang ganjil. Setelah membunuh mangsa, ia melahapnya sampai perutnya menggembung dan tak sanggup makan lagi, lalu menjepitkan diri di antara dua pohon untuk menekan makanan melalui tubuhnya, kemudian kembali ke bangkai dan mengulanginya sampai tak tersisa apa pun. Legenda tentang jerff adalah catatan awal tentang hewan järv, yang dulu belum dikenal di Swedia selatan.",
      "en": "The gulon is notorious as a symbol of gluttony because of its strange eating habits. After a kill it gorges until swollen and unable to eat more, then squeezes itself between two trees to push the food through its body, returning to the carcass and repeating until nothing is left. Legends of the jerff are an early mention of the järv, then unknown in southern Sweden.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gulon-c02",
        "gulon-c03",
        "gulon-c04"
      ]
    }

      "id": "gulon-c01",
      "source_id": "gulon-s1",
      "quote": "It resembles a hodgepodge of various animals, usually described as being the size and shape of a dog, with some cat-like features such as the head, ears, and claws. It also supposedly has a thick coat of shaggy brown fur and a tail resembling that of a fox.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gulon tampak seperti campuran berbagai hewan, biasanya seukuran dan sebentuk anjing dengan kepala, telinga, dan cakar seperti kucing, berbulu cokelat tebal yang kusut dan berekor seperti rubah.",
        "en": "The gulon looks like a mix of animals, usually dog-sized and dog-shaped with cat-like head, ears and claws, a thick shaggy brown coat and a fox-like tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gulon-c02",
      "source_id": "gulon-s1",
      "quote": "The Gulon is notorious as a symbol of gluttony because of the strange eating habits it is supposed to have. It will make a kill and then gorge itself until it is swollen and unable to eat more, at which point it will find two trees and squeeze itself in between them, pushing the meat through its own body before returning to the kill and repeating the process.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gulon terkenal sebagai lambang kerakusan karena kebiasaan makannya yang ganjil: setelah membunuh, ia makan sampai menggembung, lalu menjepit diri di antara dua pohon untuk mendorong daging melalui tubuhnya, kemudian kembali makan dan mengulanginya.",
        "en": "The gulon is notorious as a symbol of gluttony for its strange eating: after a kill it gorges until swollen, squeezes between two trees to push the meat through its body, then returns and repeats."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gulon-c03",
      "source_id": "gulon-s2",
      "quote": "En Jerff eller med modern stavning Järv, är inom skandinavisk legend en blandning av olika djur men som ska likna en hund, med huvud och klor av en katt. Den ska även ha en tjock päls och en svans liknande den på en räv. I Tyskland är varelsen känd som Vielfraß och på engelska heter det Gulon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Jerff, dalam ejaan modern Järv, dalam legenda Skandinavia adalah campuran berbagai hewan yang mirip anjing dengan kepala dan cakar kucing, berbulu tebal dan berekor seperti rubah; di Jerman ia dikenal sebagai Vielfraß dan dalam bahasa Inggris Gulon.",
        "en": "The jerff, modern spelling järv, is in Scandinavian legend a blend of animals resembling a dog with a cat's head and claws, a thick coat and a fox-like tail; in Germany it is Vielfraß and in English Gulon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gulon-c04",
      "source_id": "gulon-s2",
      "quote": "Legender om jerffen är ett tidigt omnämnande av den i södra Sverige då okända järven.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda tentang jerff merupakan sebutan awal tentang järv (wolverine), yang dulu belum dikenal di Swedia selatan.",
        "en": "Legends of the jerff are an early mention of the järv (wolverine), then unknown in southern Sweden."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "en",
      "searched": "Kalimat en yang menyebut nama jerff/Vielfraß dan dugaan bahwa gulon adalah wolverine bertanda citation needed sehingga tidak dikutip; nama dan kaitan dengan järv diambil dari artikel sv."
    },
    {

    {

  "disposition": null,
  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hjúki adalah anak lelaki dalam mitologi Nordik yang bersama saudarinya Bil diambil oleh Máni, sang Bulan, dari bumi dan kini mengikutinya di langit.",
    "en": "Hjúki is a boy of Norse mythology who, with his sister Bil, was taken from the earth by Máni, the Moon, and now follows him across the sky.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hjuki-c01",
      "hjuki-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Hjúki (Nordik Kuno, mungkin berarti \"yang kembali sehat\") dan Bil adalah sepasang kakak-adik yang mengikuti Bulan yang dipersonifikasikan, Máni, melintasi langit. Keduanya hanya tercatat dalam Edda Prosa karya Snorri Sturluson dari abad ke-13.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Hjúki (Old Norse, possibly 'the one returning to health') and Bil are a brother-and-sister pair who follow the personified Moon, Máni, across the heavens. Both are attested only in the Prose Edda, written by Snorri Sturluson in the 13th century.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hjuki-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam bab 11 Gylfaginning, High menyatakan Hjúki dan Bil adalah anak Viðfinnr. Suatu kali, ketika keduanya berjalan dari sumur Byrgir sambil memikul kayu pikulan Simul yang menggantung ember Sæg, Máni mengambil mereka dari bumi, dan kini mereka mengikuti Máni di langit, \"seperti dapat dilihat dari bumi\". Selain itu Hjúki tak disebut lagi.",
      "en": "In chapter 11 of Gylfaginning, High says that Hjúki and Bil were fathered by Viðfinnr. Once, as they walked from the well Byrgir carrying on their shoulders the pole Simul from which hung the pail Sæg, Máni carried them off from the earth; since then they have followed him across the sky, 'as can be seen from the earth'. Hjúki is otherwise unmentioned.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hjuki-c02",
        "hjuki-c04",
        "hjuki-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Karena tak tercatat di luar Edda Prosa, ada dugaan keduanya tokoh mitis kecil atau rekaan Snorri, sedangkan Anne Holtsmark (1945) menduga Snorri mengenal sumber puisi yang hilang tempat Hjúki dan Bil mempersonifikasikan bulan yang membesar dan mengecil. Para sarjana juga menafsirkan keduanya sebagai lambang kegiatan bulan.",
      "en": "As they are unattested outside the Prose Edda, some suggest they were minor mythic figures or Snorri's invention, while Anne Holtsmark (1945) proposed that Snorri knew a lost verse source in which Hjúki and Bil personified the waxing and waning moon. Scholars have also read the pair as representing lunar activity.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hjuki-c01",
        "hjuki-c03",
        "hjuki-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam bab 11 Gylfaginning, High menyatakan Hjúki dan Bil anak Viðfinnr; suatu kali saat mereka berjalan dari sumur Byrgir, Máni mengambil mereka dari bumi, dan kini mereka mengikuti Máni di langit \"seperti dapat dilihat dari bumi\"; selain itu Hjúki tak disebut lagi.",
        "en": "In Gylfaginning chapter 11 High says Hjúki and Bil were fathered by Viðfinnr; once as they walked from the well Byrgir, Máni took them from the earth, and they now follow him in the heavens 'as can be seen from the earth'; Hjúki is otherwise unmentioned."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hjuki-c03",
      "source_id": "hjuki-s1",
      "quote": "As the two are otherwise unattested outside Snorri's Prose Edda, suggestions have been made that Hjúki and Bil may have been of minor mythic significance, or that they were made up outright by Snorri, while Anne Holtsmark (1945) posits that Snorri may have known or had access to a now lost verse source wherein Hjúki and Bil personified the waxing and waning moon.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena tak tercatat di luar Edda Prosa, ada dugaan Hjúki dan Bil tokoh mitis kecil atau rekaan Snorri, sedangkan Anne Holtsmark (1945) menduga Snorri mengenal sumber puisi yang kini hilang tempat keduanya mempersonifikasikan bulan yang membesar dan mengecil.",
        "en": "Since they are unattested outside the Prose Edda, some suggest Hjúki and Bil were minor figures or Snorri's invention, while Anne Holtsmark (1945) posits Snorri knew a now-lost verse source in which they personified the waxing and waning moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hjuki-c04",
      "source_id": "hjuki-s2",
      "quote": "Hiuki war in der nordischen Mythologie der Sohn des Wildfinnr und der Bruder der Bil. Er und seine Schwester wurden zum Brunnen Byrgir geschickt, um Wasser zu schöpfen, woraufhin sie von Mani, dem Riesen des Mondes, zu sich geholt wurden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hiuki dalam mitologi Nordik adalah putra Wildfinnr dan saudara Bil; ia dan saudarinya disuruh mengambil air ke sumur Byrgir, lalu diambil oleh Mani, raksasa bulan.",
        "en": "Hiuki in Norse mythology was the son of Wildfinnr and brother of Bil; he and his sister were sent to draw water at the well Byrgir and were then taken by Mani, the giant of the moon."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hjuki-c05",
      "source_id": "hjuki-s1",
      "quote": "carrying on their shoulders the pole Simul (Old Norse, possibly meaning \"eternal\") that held the pail Sæg between them",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keduanya memikul di bahu kayu pikulan Simul (mungkin berarti \"abadi\") yang menggantung ember Sæg di antara mereka.",
        "en": "They carried on their shoulders the pole Simul (possibly 'eternal') holding the pail Sæg between them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hjuki-c06",
      "source_id": "hjuki-s1",
      "quote": "Scholars have theorized that Hjúki and Bil may represent lunar activity",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para sarjana berteori bahwa Hjúki dan Bil mungkin melambangkan kegiatan bulan.",
        "en": "Scholars have theorised that Hjúki and Bil may represent lunar activity."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

  ]
}

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hödekin, \"Topi Kecil\", adalah kobold atau roh rumah dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang menghantui kastel Uskup Hildesheim; ia menolong tetapi membalas dendam dengan kejam bila dihina.",
    "en": "Hödekin, 'Little Hat', is a kobold or house spirit of German folklore who haunted the castle of the Bishop of Hildesheim; helpful, yet murderously vengeful when insulted.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hodekin-c01",
      "hodekin-c02",
      "hodekin-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Hödekin (juga Hödeken, Hütgin, Hüdekin, atau Hütchen) adalah kobold atau roh rumah dalam cerita rakyat Jerman. Namanya, bentuk kecil yang berarti \"Topi Kecil\", merujuk topi pileus yang dikenakannya. Ia terkenal menghantui kastel Uskup Bernhard dari Keuskupan-Pangeran Hildesheim di Niedersachsen; dalam sebagian versi ia menghuni Winzenburg dan menjadi roh kastelnya.",
      "en": "Hödekin (also Hödeken, Hütgin, Hüdekin or Hütchen) is a kobold or house spirit of German folklore. His name, a diminutive meaning 'Little Hat', refers to the pileus cap he wears. He is known for haunting the castle of Bishop Bernhard of the Prince-Bishopric of Hildesheim in Lower Saxony; in some versions he dwells at Winzenburg as its castle spirit.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hodekin-c01",
        "hodekin-c02",
        "hodekin-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Hödekin tidak memulai kejahatan, tetapi dendamnya mematikan: ia memotong-motong seorang bocah dapur yang terus menghinanya dan menuangkan kotoran dapur kepadanya, meracuni daging uskup dengan darah dan bisa kodok ketika juru masak mengeluh, lalu mendorong juru masak itu ke parit dari ketinggian hingga tewas. Namun ia juga menolong, misalnya dengan memberi juru tulis bodoh cincin daun salam yang membuatnya cerdas.",
      "en": "Hödekin did not start trouble, but his revenge was deadly: he cut up a kitchen boy who kept insulting him and pouring filth on him, tainted the bishop's meat with toad's blood and venom when the cook complained, and then pushed the cook into a ditch from a great height, killing him. Yet he also helped: for instance he gave a foolish clerk a ring of laurel leaves that made him clever.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hodekin-c03",
        "hodekin-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut Grimm bersaudara, ia mencegah penjaga malam tertidur, memberi uskup nasihat perang, memperingatkannya tentang bahaya yang akan datang, dan pernah diminta menjaga istri seseorang selama suaminya pergi. Masa tinggalnya di Hildesheim berakhir ketika uskup mengusirnya dengan mantra gerejawi. Jalan antara Winzenburg dan Domhof Hildesheim yang konon ditempuhnya untuk mengabarkan kematian Graf Winzenburg terakhir disebut \"Rennstieg\".",
      "en": "According to the Brothers Grimm, he kept the night watch from falling asleep, gave the bishop military advice, warned him of coming dangers, and was once asked to guard a man's wife while he was away. His time in Hildesheim ended when the bishop drove him out with ecclesiastical incantations. The road between Winzenburg and Hildesheim's Domhof, along which he reputedly ran to report the death of the last count of Winzenburg, is called the 'Rennstieg'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hodekin-c04",
        "hodekin-c05",
        "hodekin-c06"
      ]
    }

        "id": "Hödekin (juga Hödeken, Hütgin, Hüdekin, dan Hütchen) adalah kobold (roh rumah) dalam cerita rakyat Jerman; namanya bentuk kecil yang berarti \"Topi Kecil\", merujuk topi pileus yang dikenakannya.",
        "en": "Hödekin (also Hödeken, Hütgin, Hüdekin and Hütchen) is a kobold (house spirit) of German folklore; the name, a diminutive meaning 'Little Hat', refers to the pileus cap he wears."

      "id": "hodekin-c02",
      "source_id": "hodekin-s1",
      "quote": "Hödekin is known for haunting the castle of Bishop Bernard (Bernhardus), Prince-Bishopric of Hildesheim, located in Lower Saxony. In some versions of the legend, the spirit inhabits Winzenburg, a county which he helped the bishopric obtain.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hödekin dikenal menghantui kastel Uskup Bernhard (Bernhardus) dari Keuskupan-Pangeran Hildesheim di Niedersachsen; dalam sebagian versi ia menghuni Winzenburg, wilayah yang dibantunya diperoleh keuskupan.",

    {
      "id": "hodekin-c03",
      "source_id": "hodekin-s1",
      "quote": "Though Hödekin did not initiate harm, he was murderously vindictive. He dismembered a kitchen boy after the boy habitually insulted him and poured kitchen filth upon him.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Meski tidak memulai kejahatan, Hödekin pendendam yang mematikan: ia memotong-motong bocah dapur yang terus menghina dan menuangkan kotoran kepadanya, meracuni daging uskup dengan darah dan bisa kodok saat juru masak mengeluh, lalu mendorong juru masak ke parit dari ketinggian hingga tewas.",
        "en": "Though he did not start harm, Hödekin was murderously vindictive: he dismembered a kitchen boy who kept insulting him and pouring filth on him, tainted the bishop's meat with toad blood and venom when the cook griped, then pushed the cook into a ditch from a great height, killing him."
      }

    {
      "id": "hodekin-c04",
      "source_id": "hodekin-s2",
      "quote": "Der Hödeken ist der Burggeist der Winzenburg. Der Weg zwischen Winzenburg und Hildesheimer Domhof, den Hödeken nach dem Tod des letzten Grafen von Winzenburg entlanggeeilt sein soll, um die Todesnachricht zu überbringen, wird „Rennstieg“ genannt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hödeken adalah roh kastel Winzenburg, sosok mirip kurcaci (kobold) yang juga menjadi pembawa pesan antara Hildesheim dan Winzenburg; jalan antara Winzenburg dan Domhof Hildesheim yang konon dilaluinya untuk mengabarkan kematian Graf Winzenburg terakhir disebut \"Rennstieg\".",
        "en": "Hödeken is the castle spirit of Winzenburg, a dwarf-like figure (kobold) who also carried messages between Hildesheim and Winzenburg; the road between Winzenburg and Hildesheim's Domhof that he reputedly ran to report the last count of Winzenburg's death is called the 'Rennstieg'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hodekin-c05",
      "source_id": "hodekin-s2",
      "quote": "Der Hödeken war ein Geist, der sich am Hof des Bischofs von Hildesheim aufhielt. Einmal bat jemand den Hödeken, seine Frau während seiner Abwesenheit zu beschützen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut Grimm bersaudara, Hödeken adalah roh di istana Uskup Hildesheim yang mencegah penjaga malam tertidur, memberi uskup nasihat perang, memperingatkannya akan bahaya, kadang menolong warga Hildesheim lain, dan pernah diminta menjaga istri seseorang selama suaminya pergi.",
        "en": "Per the Brothers Grimm, Hödeken was a spirit at the court of the Bishop of Hildesheim who kept the night watch awake, gave the bishop military advice, warned him of dangers, sometimes helped other townsfolk, and was once asked to guard a man's wife in his absence."
      }

    {
      "id": "hodekin-c06",
      "source_id": "hodekin-s1",
      "quote": "He also helped an idiot clerk appointed to the synod by giving him a ring made of laurel leaves that granted him knowledge and intelligence.       "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ia juga menolong seorang juru tulis bodoh yang diangkat ke sinode dengan memberinya cincin daun salam yang memberi pengetahuan dan kecerdasan; akhirnya masa tinggalnya di Hildesheim berakhir ketika uskup mengusirnya dengan mantra gerejawi.",
        "en": "He also helped a foolish clerk appointed to the synod with a ring of laurel leaves granting knowledge and intelligence; his time in Hildesheim ended when the bishop exorcised him with ecclesiastical incantations and drove him out."

      "id": "hodekin-c07",
      "source_id": "hodekin-s2",
      "quote": "Als der Mann zurückkehrte, beschwerte sich der Hödeken: Er werde lieber alle Schweine von Sachsen hüten als nochmals eine solche Frau.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika lelaki itu kembali, Hödeken mengeluh bahwa ia lebih suka menggembala semua babi di Sachsen daripada menjaga perempuan seperti itu lagi.",
        "en": "When the man returned, Hödeken complained he would rather herd all the pigs of Saxony than guard such a woman again."
      }

      "id": "hodekin-c08",
      "source_id": "hodekin-s2",
      "quote": "„[…] auf dem Haupt trug er einen kleinen Filzhut, wovon man ihm den Namen Hütchen auf Niedersächsisch Hödeken gegeben hatte.“",

      "statement": {

        "en": "Per the Grimms, he wore a little felt hat, whence the name Hütchen, in Low Saxon Hödeken."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Lyngbakr, \"punggung semak heather\", adalah monster laut raksasa mirip paus dalam Örvar-Odds saga yang menyamar sebagai pulau berlapis heather dan menenggelamkan pelaut yang mendarat di punggungnya.",
    "en": "Lyngbakr, 'heather-back', is a giant whale-like sea monster of the Örvar-Odds saga that poses as a heather-covered island and drowns sailors who land on its back.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "lyngbakr-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Lyngbakr (bahasa Islandia lyngi, \"heather\", dan bak, \"punggung\") adalah nama monster laut raksasa mirip paus yang menurut Örvar-Odds saga hidup di Laut Greenland. Ia memancing pelaut dengan menyamar sebagai pulau yang ditumbuhi heather, dan ketika awak kapal mendarat di punggungnya, ia tenggelam ke laut sehingga mereka semua mati.",
      "en": "Lyngbakr (Icelandic lyngi, 'heather', and bak, 'back') is the name of a giant whale-like sea monster that, according to the Örvar-Odds saga, lived in the Greenland Sea. Pretending to be an island overgrown with heather, it tempted ships to put in; once sailors stepped onto its back it dived, and they all drowned.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lyngbakr-c01"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Lyngbakur dianggap hewan laut terbesar dan sering terapung setengah tenggelam sehingga tampak seperti pulau yang ditumbuhi heather, dan banyak kisah tentangnya tersebar di Islandia. Dalam saga, awak Örvar-Oddr melewati dua batu yang muncul dari laut lalu sebuah pulau berlapis heather; menurut Vignir, \"batu\" dan \"pulau\" itu sebenarnya dua monster laut, Lyngbakr, paus terbesar di dunia, dan hafgufa.",
      "en": "The lyngbakur was thought the largest of all sea creatures, often lying half-submerged so it looked like a heather-grown island, and many tales of it circulate in Iceland. In the saga Örvar-Oddr's crew passed two rocks rising from the sea and then a large heather-covered island; Vignir told them these were in truth a pair of sea monsters, the hafgufa and Lyngbakr, mightiest of whales.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "lyngbakr-c02",
        "lyngbakr-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sumber berbeda tentang nasib awak Oddr: satu uraian menyebut mereka berbalik dan pulau itu lenyap sebelum mereka mendarat, sedangkan kutipan teks saga menyebut lima orang Oddr naik ke pulau itu lalu tenggelam ketika pulau itu karam. Monster serupa dikenal dalam cerita rakyat Inuit Greenland sebagai Imap Umassoursa, dan dalam kisah Brendan sang pelaut sebagai paus Jasconius.",
      "en": "Sources differ on the fate of Oddr's men: one account says they turned back and the island vanished before they landed, whereas the quoted saga text says five of Oddr's men went onto the island and drowned when it sank. Similar monsters are known in Inuit lore of Greenland as Imap Umassoursa and in the tales of Brendan the Navigator as the whale Jasconius.",

    {
      "id": "lyngbakr-c01",
      "source_id": "lyngbakr-s1",
      "quote": "Lyngbakr (Icelandic, lyngi \"heather\" + bak \"back\") is the name of a massive whale-like sea monster reported in the Örvar-Odds saga to have existed in the Greenland Sea. According to the saga, Lyngbakr would bait seafarers by posing as a heather-covered island, and when a crew landed on his back, he sank into the sea, drowning the crew.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lyngbakr (Islandia: lyngi, \"heather\", + bak, \"punggung\") adalah monster laut raksasa mirip paus yang menurut Örvar-Odds saga hidup di Laut Greenland; ia memancing pelaut dengan menyamar sebagai pulau berlapis heather, dan bila awak mendarat di punggungnya, ia tenggelam dan menenggelamkan mereka.",
        "en": "Lyngbakr (Icelandic lyngi 'heather' + bak 'back') is a massive whale-like sea monster said in the Örvar-Odds saga to live in the Greenland Sea; it baited seafarers by posing as a heather-covered island, then sank when a crew landed on its back, drowning them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lyngbakr-c02",
      "source_id": "lyngbakr-s1",
      "quote": "Vignir explained to Oddr that, had they landed sooner, the crew would have surely drowned. The \"rocks\" and \"island\" must have been two sea monsters—Lyngbakr, the greatest whale in the world, and hafgufa",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Vignir menjelaskan kepada Oddr bahwa bila mereka mendarat lebih cepat, awak pasti tenggelam; \"batu\" dan \"pulau\" itu tentulah dua monster laut, Lyngbakr, paus terbesar di dunia, dan hafgufa.",
        "en": "Vignir explained to Oddr that had they landed sooner the crew would surely have drowned; the 'rocks' and 'island' must have been two sea monsters, Lyngbakr, the greatest whale in the world, and hafgufa."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lyngbakr-c03",
      "source_id": "lyngbakr-s2",
      "quote": "Lyngbakurinn var talinn vera stærstur allra dýra í sjó og lá hann oft þannig á hálfu kafi að hann virtist vera eyja og leit þá út eins og þar á sé vaxið lyng og því er hann kallaður lyngbakur. Fjölmargar sögur eru um lyngbaka víða frá á Íslandi.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Lyngbakur dianggap hewan laut terbesar dan sering terapung setengah tenggelam sehingga tampak seperti pulau yang ditumbuhi heather, karena itu disebut lyngbakur; banyak kisah tentangnya tersebar di Islandia.",
        "en": "The lyngbakur was held the largest of all sea creatures, often lying half-submerged so it seemed an island grown with heather, hence its name; many tales of it are told across Iceland."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lyngbakr-c04",
      "source_id": "lyngbakr-s1",
      "quote": "Curious, Oddr made up his mind to turn back and send five men to check out the island, but as they approached where the island had been before, they saw that it and the two rocks vanished.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Karena penasaran, Oddr memutuskan berbalik dan mengirim lima orang memeriksa pulau itu, tetapi saat mendekat mereka melihat pulau dan kedua batu telah lenyap.",
        "en": "Curious, Oddr decided to turn back and send five men to check the island, but as they approached they saw it and the two rocks had vanished."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lyngbakr-c05",
      "source_id": "lyngbakr-s2",
      "quote": "Oddr bað fimm menn ganga á land ok leita at vatni. ... En er menn Odds kómu á eyna, höfðu þeir litla stund þar verit, áðr eyin sökk, ok drukknuðu þeir allir.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kutipan saga, Oddr menyuruh lima orang naik ke darat mencari air; tak lama setelah mereka tiba di pulau itu, pulau itu tenggelam dan mereka semua mati tenggelam.",
        "en": "In the saga passage, Oddr sent five men ashore to look for water; soon after they reached the island it sank and they all drowned."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "lyngbakr-c06",
      "source_id": "lyngbakr-s2",
      "quote": "Í þjóðsögum inúíta á Grænlandi var er talað um svipað skrímsli sem hét Imap Umassoursa. ... Svipuð skepna kemur fyrir í sögunum um Brendan sæfara, sem þar er nefnt Jasconius.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Inuit Greenland disebut monster serupa bernama Imap Umassoursa, dan makhluk serupa muncul dalam kisah Brendan sang pelaut dengan nama Jasconius.",
        "en": "Inuit folklore of Greenland tells of a similar monster called Imap Umassoursa, and a similar creature appears in the tales of Brendan the Navigator as Jasconius."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Miming adalah \"satir hutan\" dalam kronik Saxo Grammaticus, penjaga pedang yang mampu membunuh Balder dan gelang yang melipatgandakan kekayaan pemiliknya.",
    "en": "Miming is the 'satyr of the woods' in the chronicle of Saxo Grammaticus, keeper of the sword able to kill Balder and of a bracelet that multiplies its owner's wealth.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "miming-c01",

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam kisah Saxo Grammaticus tentang Hother dan Balder, Raja Gewar menjelaskan bahwa tubuh Balder yang suci kebal terhadap baja, tetapi ada sebuah pedang yang dapat membunuhnya. Pedang itu tersimpan dalam ikatan ketat pada Miming, \"Satir hutan\", yang juga memiliki gelang berkhasiat rahasia yang menambah kekayaan pemiliknya. Jalan ke wilayahnya sulit ditempuh dan diliputi dingin yang luar biasa.",
      "en": "In Saxo Grammaticus's tale of Hother and Balder, King Gewar explains that Balder's sacred body is proof against steel, but that one sword can kill him. That sword is kept tightly bound by Miming, 'the Satyr of the woods', who also owns a bracelet of secret virtue that increases its owner's wealth. The way to his lands is hard to travel and gripped by extraordinary cold.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "miming-c01",
        "miming-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Atas nasihat Gewar, Hother menempuh perjalanan dengan kereta rusa kutub dan mendirikan tenda di bayang-bayang gua tempat Miming biasa tinggal. Ketika satir itu melemparkan bayangan ke tendanya, Hother menjatuhkannya dengan lembing, mengikatnya, dan menuntut pedang serta gelang itu; Miming pun menyerahkannya sebagai tebusan nyawanya.",
      "en": "On Gewar's advice Hother travelled by reindeer sledge and pitched his tent in the shadow of the cave where Miming used to dwell. When the satyr cast a shadow on his tent, Hother brought him down with a spear, bound him and demanded the sword and bracelet; Miming handed them over as ransom for his life.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "miming-c02",
        "miming-c03"
      ]

      "id": "Dalam pengantar terjemahannya, Oliver Elton mencatat bahwa Saxo menyebut para kurcaci sebagai satir, dan Miming adalah kurcaci penghuni tempat sunyi yang memiliki pedang setajam itu dan cincin yang melipatgandakan diri.
