  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "miming-c01",
      "source_id": "miming-s1",
      "quote": "For he said that the sacred strength of Balder's body was proof even against steel; adding, however, that he knew of a sword which could deal him his death, which was fastened up in the closest bonds; this was in the keeping of Miming, the Satyr of the woods, who also had a bracelet of a secret and marvellous virtue, that used to increase the wealth of the owner.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gewar berkata bahwa kekuatan suci tubuh Balder kebal bahkan terhadap baja, tetapi ia tahu sebuah pedang yang dapat membunuhnya, terikat sangat ketat dan disimpan oleh Miming, Satir hutan, yang juga memiliki gelang berkhasiat rahasia yang menambah kekayaan pemiliknya.",
        "en": "Gewar said that the sacred strength of Balder's body was proof even against steel, but he knew of a sword that could kill him, tightly bound and kept by Miming, the Satyr of the woods, who also had a bracelet of secret virtue that increased its owner's wealth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "miming-c02",
      "source_id": "miming-s1",
      "quote": "So he advised him to harness a car with reindeer, by means of whose great speed he could cross the hard-frozen ridges. And when he had got to the place, he should set up his tent away from the sun in such wise that it should catch the shadow of the cave where Miming was wont to be",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gewar menasihati Hother memakai kereta rusa kutub agar cepat melintasi punggung bukit yang membeku, lalu mendirikan tenda membelakangi matahari sehingga terkena bayangan gua tempat Miming biasa tinggal.",
        "en": "Gewar advised Hother to harness a reindeer car to cross the frozen ridges quickly, then pitch his tent away from the sun so it caught the shadow of the cave where Miming used to be."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "miming-c03",
      "source_id": "miming-s1",
      "quote": "when the Satyr cast a shadow on his tent. Aiming a spear at him, he brought him down with the blow, stopped him, and bound him, while he could not make his escape. Then in the most dreadful words he threatened him with the worst, and demanded the sword and bracelets. The Satyr was not slow to tender him the ransom of his life for which he was asked.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika satir itu melemparkan bayangan ke tendanya, Hother menjatuhkannya dengan lembing dan mengikatnya, mengancamnya, dan menuntut pedang serta gelang; satir itu segera menyerahkan tebusan nyawanya.",
        "en": "When the Satyr cast a shadow on his tent, Hother brought him down with a spear and bound him, threatened him and demanded the sword and bracelets; the Satyr promptly gave the ransom for his life."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "miming-c04",
      "source_id": "miming-s1",
      "quote": "These Saxo calls Satyrs, and but rarely mentions. The dwarf Miming, who lives in the desert, has a precious sword of sharpness (Mistletoe?) that could even pierce skin-hard Balder, and a ring (Draupnir) that multiplied itself for its possessor.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam pengantar terjemahan, kurcaci disebut Saxo sebagai satir dan jarang disinggung; kurcaci Miming yang tinggal di tempat sunyi memiliki pedang tajam berharga yang bahkan bisa menembus kulit keras Balder, serta cincin (Draupnir) yang melipatgandakan dirinya bagi pemiliknya.",
        "en": "In the translator's introduction, dwarfs are what Saxo calls Satyrs and rarely mentions; the dwarf Miming, living in the wilds, has a precious keen sword able to pierce even skin-hard Balder and a ring (Draupnir) that multiplied itself for its owner."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "miming-c05",
      "source_id": "miming-s2",
      "quote": "Nella mitologia norrena, Miming era un troll che abitava nella foresta, era il figlio di Hothbrod e l'allievo di Gevar. In una versione teutonica del mito della morte di Baldr, la spada di Miming è l'arma che Höðr usa per uccidere Baldr.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Italia, dalam mitologi Nordik Miming adalah troll penghuni hutan, putra Hothbrod dan murid Gevar; dalam satu versi Teutonik mitos kematian Baldr, pedang Miming adalah senjata yang dipakai Höðr untuk membunuh Baldr.",
        "en": "According to the Italian article, Miming in Norse mythology was a forest-dwelling troll, son of Hothbrod and pupil of Gevar; in a Teutonic version of Baldr's death myth, Miming's sword is the weapon Höðr uses to kill Baldr."
      }

  "traits": [],
  "short_description": {
    "id": "Tilberi atau snakkur adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Islandia yang diciptakan penyihir dari tulang rusuk manusia untuk mencuri susu ternak tetangga.",
    "en": "The tilberi or snakkur is a creature of Icelandic folklore, made by witches from a human rib to steal milk from the neighbours' livestock.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "tilberi-c01",
      "tilberi-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Tilberi (\"pembawa\") atau snakkur (\"kumparan\") adalah makhluk cerita rakyat Islandia yang dibuat penyihir untuk mencuri susu, dan hanya perempuan yang dapat membuat dan memilikinya. Kedua nama itu varian daerah; tidak ada catatan tertulis tentangnya sebelum abad ke-17, meski seorang penulis abad itu menyebut seorang penyihir dihukum karena memilikinya pada tahun 1500.",
      "en": "The tilberi ('carrier') or snakkur ('spindle') is an Icelandic folklore creature that witches made for stealing milk, and only women could create and keep one. The two names are regional variants; nothing was written about it before the 17th century, though one writer of that century mentions a witch punished in 1500 for owning one.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tilberi-c01",
        "tilberi-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Untuk membuatnya, seorang perempuan mencuri tulang rusuk dari jenazah yang baru dikubur pada dini hari Minggu Pentakosta, membungkusnya dengan wol kelabu curian, dan menyimpannya di antara payudaranya. Pada tiga Minggu berikutnya ia meludahkan anggur komuni ke bungkusan itu hingga makin hidup, lalu membiarkannya menyusu di paha bagian dalam, yang meninggalkan benjolan mirip kutil.",
      "en": "To make one, a woman stole a rib from a freshly buried corpse early on Whitsunday, wrapped it in stolen grey wool and kept it between her breasts. Over the following three Sundays she spat communion wine onto the bundle, which grew livelier each time, then let it suck at her inner thigh, leaving a wart-like mark.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tilberi-c03",
        "tilberi-c08"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Tilberi lalu diutus menyedot susu sapi dan domba orang lain: ia melompat ke punggung hewan dan memanjangkan diri untuk mencapai ambing, lalu kembali ke jendela ruang susu pemiliknya dan memuntahkan susu curian ke tempat pengocok mentega. Mentega dari susu itu menggumpal atau mencair menjadi buih bila diberi tanda salib atau tanda sihir smjörhnútur.",
      "en": "The tilberi was then sent to drain other people's cows and ewes: it leapt on the animal's back and stretched down to the udder, then returned to its owner's dairy window and vomited the stolen milk into her churn. Butter from such milk curdled into clumps or dissolved into foam when a cross or the smjörhnútur sign was made over it.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tilberi-c04",
        "tilberi-c05",
        "tilberi-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Untuk menyingkirkannya, pemilik menyuruh tilberi mengumpulkan kotoran domba di padang penggembalaan; makhluk itu bekerja sampai mati atau mati karena tak tahan angka tiga, dan hanya tulang manusia yang tertinggal. Bila dikejar, ia lari bersembunyi di bawah rok \"ibunya\", sehingga perempuan dan makhluk itu dapat dibakar atau ditenggelamkan bersama.",
      "en": "To be rid of it, the owner ordered the tilberi to gather sheep droppings on the pasture; it worked itself to death or died because it could not endure the number three, leaving only the human bone behind. When chased it fled under its 'mother's' skirts, so that woman and creature could be burnt or drowned together.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "tilberi-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "quote": "To create a tilberi, the woman steals a rib from a recently buried body early on Whitsunday, twists around it grey wool which she must steal for the purpose and keeps it between her breasts. ...       "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Perempuan itu mencuri tulang rusuk jenazah yang baru dikubur pada dini hari Minggu Pentakosta, melilitnya dengan wol kelabu curian, dan menyimpannya di antara payudaranya.",

    {
      "id": "tilberi-c04",
      "source_id": "tilberi-s1",
      "quote": "It will return to the window of her dairy and call out \"Full belly, Mummy!\" or \"Churn lid off, Mummy!\" and vomit the stolen milk into her butter churn. To suck the milk from the animal's udder, it jumps on her back and lengthens itself to reach down",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tilberi kembali ke jendela ruang susu, berseru \"Perut penuh, Ibu!\", dan memuntahkan susu curian ke pengocok mentega; untuk menyedot ambing ia melompat ke punggung hewan dan memanjangkan diri.",
        "en": "It returns to the dairy window, calls 'Full belly, Mummy!' and vomits the stolen milk into the churn; to suck an udder it leaps onto the animal's back and stretches itself down."

    {
      "id": "tilberi-c05",
      "source_id": "tilberi-s1",
      "quote": "Butter churned from milk stolen by a tilberi will clump together as if curdled, or even melt away into foam, if the sign of the cross is made over it or the smjörhnútur (butterknot) magical sign drawn in it.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mentega dari susu curian tilberi menggumpal atau mencair menjadi buih bila dibuat tanda salib di atasnya atau digambar tanda sihir smjörhnútur.",
        "en": "Butter from milk a tilberi stole clumps or melts into foam if a cross is made over it or the smjörhnútur magic sign drawn in it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "tilberi-c06",
      "source_id": "tilberi-s1",
      "quote": "The tilberi will then either work itself to death or die because as an evil creature it cannot tolerate the number three. Only the human bone will be left lying in the pasture.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Tilberi akan bekerja sampai mati atau mati karena, sebagai makhluk jahat, tak tahan angka tiga; hanya tulang manusia yang tertinggal di padang.",
        "en": "The tilberi either works itself to death or dies because, as an evil being, it cannot bear the number three; only the human bone remains in the pasture."
      }
    },

      "id": "tilberi-c07",
      "source_id": "tilberi-s2",
      "quote": "Un tilberi, ou Snakkur est une étrange créature du folklore de l'Islande. C'est une sorte de créature qui vole le lait du bétail des alentours et transforme le beurre fait à partir de ce lait en pièces d'or.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "The French article calls the tilberi or snakkur a thief of nearby livestock's milk that turns the butter made from it into gold coins."

      "source_id": "tilberi-s3",

      "locator": null,
      "context": "religious-tradition",
      "statement": {

        "en": "When it arrived it warned the housewife with the words 'Fullur beli, mamma' ('Full belly, Mummy')."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ulu Toyon adalah kepala antropomorfis roh-roh jahat (abasy) dalam mitologi Yakut, penghuni dunia atas, pelindung para dukun, dan ayah burung gagak.",
    "en": "Ulu Toyon is the anthropomorphic chief of the evil spirits (abasy) in Yakut mythology, a dweller of the upper world, patron of shamans and father of ravens.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ulu-toyon-c01",
      "ulu-toyon-c02",
      "ulu-toyon-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan orang Yakut di Siberia, Ulu Toyon (\"tuan agung\") adalah raja iblis yang memerintah para abasy. Ia tinggal di langit ketiga atau dunia atas dan menjadi pelindung para dukun serta beberapa marga Yakut.",
      "en": "In the belief of the Siberian Yakuts, Ulu Toyon ('great lord') is a demon king who rules the abasy. He lives in the third heaven, or upper world, and is the patron of shamans and of several Yakut clans.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulu-toyon-c01",
        "ulu-toyon-c02",
        "ulu-toyon-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia ayah burung gagak dan sering tampil dalam wujud gagak sendiri. Melalui gagak ia memberi manusia api dan menganugerahi mereka jiwa (sür), sementara wujud sejatinya tak terlihat oleh manusia fana. Dalam beberapa mitos ia muncul sebagai hewan besar berwarna hitam, seperti beruang, banteng, kuda jantan, atau rusa besar.",
      "en": "He is the father of ravens and often appears as a raven himself. Through the raven he gave people fire and granted them a soul (sür), while his true form remains invisible to mortals. In some myths he shows himself as a large black animal such as a bear, bull, stallion or elk.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulu-toyon-c01",
        "ulu-toyon-c03",
        "ulu-toyon-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sebagai pelindung dukun, ia mengadili perselisihan dan menghukum orang yang menyakiti seorang dukun; pengadilannya keras tetapi adil. Istrinya adalah Buus Dyalkyn Khotun, dan anak-anak mereka memimpin sembilan suku roh di langit selatan. Para peneliti berbeda pendapat apakah ia tergolong roh baik (aiyy) atau roh jahat (abasy).",
      "en": "As patron of shamans he settles disputes and punishes people who wrong a shaman; his judgement is harsh but just. His wife is Buus Dyalkyn Khotun, and their children lead nine tribes of spirits in the southern sky. Scholars disagree whether he belongs among the good spirits (aiyy) or the evil ones (abasy).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ulu-toyon-c04",
        "ulu-toyon-c05",
        "ulu-toyon-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "title": "Улу Тойон",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ulu-toyon-c01",
      "source_id": "ulu-toyon-s1",
      "quote": "Ulu Toyon (auch Ulu Toyo'n) ist im Glauben der sibirischen Jakuten ein Dämonenkönig. Er herrscht über die Abaasy, die Dämonen der Unterwelt. Demnach lebt Ulu Toyon im dritten Himmel und gab einst den Menschen das Geheimnis des Feuers.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan orang Yakut (Jakuten) Siberia, Ulu Toyon adalah raja iblis yang memerintah Abaasy, iblis dunia bawah; ia tinggal di langit ketiga dan pernah memberi manusia rahasia api.",
        "en": "In the belief of the Siberian Yakuts (Jakuten), Ulu Toyon is a demon king ruling the Abaasy, demons of the underworld; he lives in the third heaven and once gave people the secret of fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulu-toyon-c02",
      "source_id": "ulu-toyon-s2",
      "quote": "Улу Тойон (якут. Улуу Тойон; от тюрк.  — «великий господин») — антропоморфный глава злых духов (абасы), обитатель верхнего мира, покровитель шаманов в мифологии якутов.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ulu Toyon (Yakut: Uluu Toyon, dari bahasa Turk \"tuan agung\") adalah kepala antropomorfis roh jahat (abasy), penghuni dunia atas, dan pelindung dukun dalam mitologi Yakut.",
        "en": "Ulu Toyon (Yakut: Uluu Toyon, Turkic for 'great lord') is the anthropomorphic head of the evil spirits (abasy), dweller of the upper world and patron of shamans in Yakut mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulu-toyon-c03",
      "source_id": "ulu-toyon-s2",
      "quote": "Улу Тойон является отцом воронов, при этом сам нередко предстаёт в обличье ворона, в связи чем его называют величайший великий ворон господин (якут. Улуутуйар Улуу Суорун Тойон).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ulu Toyon adalah ayah para gagak dan sering muncul sebagai gagak, sehingga disebut \"tuan gagak agung terbesar\" (Yakut: Uluutuyar Uluu Suorun Toyon).",
        "en": "Ulu Toyon is the father of ravens and often appears as one, hence his title 'greatest great raven lord' (Yakut: Uluutuyar Uluu Suorun Toyon)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulu-toyon-c04",
      "source_id": "ulu-toyon-s2",
      "quote": "Являясь покровителем шаманов, Улу Тойон вершит справедливый суд, разрешая споры и наказывая обидевших того или иного шамана людей.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagai pelindung dukun, Ulu Toyon mengadili dengan adil, menyelesaikan perselisihan, dan menghukum orang yang menyakiti seorang dukun.",
        "en": "As patron of shamans, Ulu Toyon judges fairly, settling disputes and punishing those who wronged a shaman."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulu-toyon-c05",
      "source_id": "ulu-toyon-s2",
      "quote": "Эргис Г. У. полагает, что из-за проявления добрых черт (наделение душой людей, передача огня, справедливый суд, шаманы-лекари) Улу Тойона следует причислять к числу Айыы.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ergis berpendapat bahwa karena sifat-sifat baiknya (memberi jiwa, api, pengadilan adil), Ulu Toyon semestinya digolongkan ke roh baik Aiyy; Kulakovsky menilai dalam mitologi kini ia termasuk abasy.",
        "en": "Ergis holds that because of his good traits (granting souls and fire, fair judgement) Ulu Toyon should be counted among the Aiyy; Kulakovsky sees him as abasy in present-day mythology."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulu-toyon-c06",
      "source_id": "ulu-toyon-s3",
      "quote": "Дружиною Улу Тойона була Буус Дьалкин Хотун («крижана пані з розвилистою ходою»). Діти його вважалися главами дев'яти племен духів, що живуть на південному небі.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Istri Ulu Toyon adalah Buus Dyalkyn Khotun (\"nyonya es dengan langkah terhuyung\"); anak-anaknya dianggap pemimpin sembilan suku roh yang tinggal di langit selatan.",
        "en": "Ulu Toyon's wife was Buus Dyalkyn Khotun ('icy lady with a waddling gait'); his children were held to be the heads of nine tribes of spirits living in the southern sky."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ulu-toyon-c07",
      "source_id": "ulu-toyon-s3",
      "quote": "Вважалося, що його справжній вигляд недоступний спогляданню смертних, але в деяких міфах його бачать в образі якогось великої тварини. Так, прийнявши образ великого чорного бика або чорного жеребця, величезного ведмедя чи лося, він з ревом і шумом пробігає по землі.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wujud sejatinya diyakini tak bisa dilihat manusia fana, tetapi dalam beberapa mitos ia tampil sebagai banteng hitam besar, kuda jantan hitam, beruang raksasa, atau rusa besar yang berlari menderu di bumi.",
        "en": "His true form was believed hidden from mortal sight, but in some myths he appears as a great black bull or black stallion, a huge bear or an elk, running over the earth with a roar."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Alraune adalah akar mandragora berwujud manusia dalam kepercayaan Jerman, yang dianggap memegang nasib manusia dan, bila dirawat dengan baik, meramalkan masa depan.",
    "en": "The alraune is a human-shaped mandrake root in German belief, thought to hold people's fate in its power and, if well cared for, to foretell the future.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "alraune-c01",
      "alraune-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Alraune (kata Jerman untuk mandragora) adalah makhluk legenda yang konon dimiliki bangsa Jermanik kuno: akar mandragora yang menggenggam nasib manusia. Menurut legenda, pemiliknya harus memberinya pakaian rapi, membaringkannya dalam peti kecil, memandikannya setiap pekan dengan anggur dan air, serta memberinya makan dan minum setiap kali bersantap; bila tidak, alih-alih meramalkan masa depan, ia menjerit seperti bayi kelaparan.",
      "en": "Alraune (German for mandrake) is a legendary being said to have been kept by the ancient Germanic peoples: a mandrake root holding human fate. Legend says its owner had to dress it neatly, lay it in a small box, bathe it weekly in wine and water and feed it at every meal; otherwise, instead of foretelling the future, it would scream like a starving infant.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "alraune-c01",
        "alraune-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kisah ini berakar pada Abad Pertengahan Jerman. Para alkemis percaya akar mandragora yang samar-samar berwujud manusia tumbuh dari sperma orang yang digantung, sehingga akar itu sering dicari di bawah tiang gantungan. Hanya akar berwujud tubuh manusia yang dianggap bertuah; akar ini dipakai dalam ramuan cinta, dan buahnya diyakini membantu kehamilan.",
      "en": "The tale goes back to the German Middle Ages. Alchemists believed the vaguely human-shaped mandrake root grew from the semen of hanged men, so it was often sought beneath the gallows. Only roots shaped like a human body were thought to have power; the root went into love potions, and its fruit was believed to aid conception.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "alraune-c03",
        "alraune-c05",
        "alraune-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Mencabutnya memerlukan persiapan: tanah di sekitarnya digali, tiga lingkaran sihir digambar dengan pedang, lalu seekor anjing hitam yang diikat pada tanaman itu menariknya keluar. Akar yang tercabut diyakini menjerit dengan suara manusia yang membunuh siapa pun yang mendengarnya, sehingga anjing itulah yang mati. Akar itu juga dikatakan mengangguk bila jawaban atas suatu pertanyaan adalah \"ya\".",
      "en": "Pulling one up took preparation: the soil around it was dug away, three magic circles were drawn with a sword, and a black dog tied to the plant dragged it out. The uprooted root was believed to scream with a human voice that killed whoever heard it, so the dog was the one to die. The root was also said to nod when the answer to a question was 'yes'.",

      ]

      "id": "alraune-c02",
      "source_id": "alraune-s1",
      "quote": "ceux qui possédaient une Alrune devaient l'habiller proprement, la coucher avec attention dans un petit coffret, la laver chaque semaine avec du vin et de l'eau, et lui donner à boire et à manger à chaque repas, sinon, au lieu d'annoncer l'avenir (plus ou moins intelligiblement), elle criait comme un enfant affamé.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pemilik Alrune harus mendandaninya, membaringkannya dalam peti kecil, memandikannya tiap pekan dengan anggur dan air, serta memberinya makan-minum tiap bersantap; jika tidak, alih-alih meramal ia menjerit seperti anak kelaparan.",
        "en": "Owners of an Alrune had to dress it, lay it in a little box, wash it weekly with wine and water and feed it at each meal; otherwise, instead of predicting the future, it cried like a starving child."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alraune-c03",
      "source_id": "alraune-s2",
      "quote": "A base para a história de Alraune data da Idade Média alemã. A raiz da mandrágora (Mandragora officinarum), que possui um formato vagamente humanóide, era creditada pelos alquimistas como sendo produzida pela ação sobre a terra do esperma de enforcados",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kisah Alraune berakar pada Abad Pertengahan Jerman; para alkemis percaya akar mandragora (Mandragora officinarum) yang samar berwujud manusia tumbuh dari sperma orang yang digantung.",
        "en": "The Alraune story dates to the German Middle Ages; alchemists credited the vaguely humanoid mandrake root (Mandragora officinarum) to the semen of hanged men falling on the earth."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alraune-c04",
      "source_id": "alraune-s1",
      "quote": "Selon d'autres sources, l'Alrune serait un démon succube d'où serait issue la nation des Huns ; elles prendraient toutes sortes de formes, mais ne pourraient pas changer de sexe.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sumber lain, Alrune adalah iblis sukubus asal-usul bangsa Hun (Huns), yang bisa mengambil segala rupa tetapi tidak bisa berganti kelamin.",
        "en": "Other sources make the Alrune a succubus demon from which the Huns descended, able to take any form but not to change sex."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alraune-c05",
      "source_id": "alraune-s3",
      "quote": "Альрауна (з нім. Alraune) — корінь мандрагори, якому приписувались магічні властивості.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Alraune adalah akar mandragora yang dianggap memiliki kekuatan magis.",
        "en": "The Alraune is a mandrake root credited with magical properties."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alraune-c06",
      "source_id": "alraune-s3",
      "quote": "Магічні властивості приписувались винятково кореням, що мали форму людського тіла. Найчастіше мандрагору шукали під шибеницею, де рештки тіла повішеного повинні були забезпечувати рослині відповідне живлення.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kekuatan magis hanya diberikan pada akar berwujud tubuh manusia; mandragora paling sering dicari di bawah tiang gantungan, tempat jasad orang yang digantung diyakini menyuburkannya.",
        "en": "Magic was ascribed only to roots shaped like a human body; mandrake was mostly sought under the gallows, where a hanged man's remains were supposed to nourish it."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alraune-c07",
      "source_id": "alraune-s3",
      "quote": "Витягнути мандрагору з землі повинен був чорний пес, якому прив'язували до шиї шнурок, який іншим кінцем був прив'язаний до рослини. Вважалося, що мандрагора, вирвана з землі, кричала людським голосом, який вбивав того, хто її чув; таким чином помирав пес.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mandragora harus dicabut oleh anjing hitam yang lehernya diikat tali ke tanaman; mandragora yang tercabut diyakini menjerit dengan suara manusia yang membunuh pendengarnya, sehingga anjing itulah yang mati.",
        "en": "A black dog with a cord from its neck to the plant had to pull the mandrake out; the uprooted mandrake was believed to cry with a human voice that killed the listener, so the dog died."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alraune-c08",
      "source_id": "alraune-s3",
      "quote": "Для певності треба було ще накреслити мечем три магічні кола навколо рослини.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Untuk berjaga-jaga, tiga lingkaran sihir juga harus digambar dengan pedang di sekeliling tanaman itu.",
        "en": "To be sure, three magic circles also had to be drawn round the plant with a sword."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "alraune-c09",
      "source_id": "alraune-s2",
      "quote": "O escritor alemão Hanns Heinz Ewers publicou um romance intitulado Alraune em 1911.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Penulis Jerman Hanns Heinz Ewers menerbitkan novel berjudul Alraune pada 1911.",
        "en": "German writer Hanns Heinz Ewers published a novel titled Alraune in 1911."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",

      "field": "images",
      "searched": "Gambar Commons belum dicari pada putaran ini; dijadwalkan pada pemeriksaan gambar terpisah."
    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Bysen adalah makhluk hutan kecil dalam kepercayaan rakyat Gotland, Swedia, yang menyesatkan orang dan mengganggu para pekerja hutan.",
    "en": "Bysen is a small forest being in the folk belief of Gotland, Sweden, who leads people astray and plagues forest workers.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "bysen-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat pulau Gotland, Bysen adalah makhluk hutan kecil yang memikat orang hingga tersesat dan menyusahkan para penebang. Siapa pun yang terlihat olehnya di hutan menjadi kacau penglihatannya sehingga tak bisa menemukan jalan pulang; penglihatan itu pulih bila ia membalik salah satu pakaiannya.",
      "en": "In the folk belief of the island of Gotland, Bysen is a small forest being who lures people into losing their way and troubles woodcutters. Anyone he catches sight of in the forest has their vision confused so they cannot find the way home; it returns to normal if they turn a garment inside out.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bysen-c01",
        "bysen-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia menunda pengangkutan kayu dan menjungkirkan muatan kayu gelondongan, serta sering tampil sebagai tunggul pohon atau lelaki tua kelabu yang kecil dan tak mencolok, kadang bertopi rajut merah. Ia membawa kapak karena tugasnya menebang hutan Gotland, pekerjaan yang sangat lamban, kadang hanya satu pohon per abad. Ia dianggap manusia yang berbuat jahat semasa hidup dan dihukum mengembara selamanya, sekaligus penjaga hutan dan alam.",
      "en": "He delays timber transports and overturns loads of logs, and often shows himself as a stump or a small, unremarkable grey old man, sometimes in a red knitted cap. He carries an axe because his task is to fell Gotland's forest, which goes so slowly that sometimes only one tree falls in a century. He is held to be a person who committed a crime in life and is condemned to wander forever, yet also a keeper of the forest and nature.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "bysen-c02",
        "bysen-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut sebagian sumber, bysar adalah orang mati yang dulu merebut tanah orang lain dengan memindahkan patok batas. Sebagai hukuman mereka tak tenang di kubur, berjalan di sepanjang batas yang salah sambil memindahkan patok dan bergumam \"di sini benar, di sini salah\". Bila seseorang mengikuti Bysen dan mengembalikan patok-patok ke tempat yang benar, Bysen mendapat kedamaian. Di daratan Swedia padanannya disebut skälvrängare atau osaliga lantmätare.",
      "en": "According to some sources, bysar are dead people who once seized others' land by moving the boundary markers. As punishment they find no rest in the grave, walking the wrongful boundary, shifting the stakes and muttering 'here is right, here is wrong'. If a person follows Bysen and puts the stakes back in the right places, Bysen finds peace. On the Swedish mainland the counterpart is called skälvrängare or osaliga lantmätare.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "bysen-c01",
      "source_id": "bysen-s1",
      "quote": "Bysen är i gotländsk folktro ett litet skogsväsen som lockar människor att gå vilse eller ställer till förtret för skogsarbetarna.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat Gotland (Swedia), Bysen adalah makhluk hutan kecil yang memikat orang tersesat atau menyusahkan pekerja hutan.",
        "en": "In the folk belief of Gotland (Sweden), Bysen is a small forest being who lures people astray or causes trouble for forest workers."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bysen-c02",
      "source_id": "bysen-s1",
      "quote": "Bysen försenar också transporter och välter timmerlass. Han uppträder ofta som stubbe eller en liten grå, oansenlig gubbe, och beskylls för allt möjligt ofog.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bysen menunda pengangkutan dan menjungkirkan muatan kayu; ia sering muncul sebagai tunggul atau lelaki tua kelabu kecil yang tak mencolok, dan dituduh atas segala kenakalan.",
        "en": "Bysen also delays transports and tips over timber loads; he often appears as a stump or a small, insignificant grey old man and is blamed for all sorts of mischief."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bysen-c03",
      "source_id": "bysen-s2",
      "quote": "Sometimes he wears a red woven cap and an axe because one of his tasks is to cut down Gotland's forest. This goes slowly, however, and sometimes only one tree per century is felled. It's said Bysen is a humanoid who has done some crime within his lifetime and is therefore sentenced to wander the Earth forever. Bysen is also the ward of the forest and nature.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kadang ia bertopi rajut merah dan membawa kapak karena tugasnya menebang hutan Gotland, yang berjalan lambat, kadang satu pohon per abad; ia dihukum mengembara di Bumi (Earth) selamanya atas kejahatan semasa hidup, dan juga penjaga hutan dan alam.",
        "en": "He sometimes wears a red knitted cap and an axe since he must fell Gotland's forest, slowly, at times one tree a century; he is condemned to roam the Earth forever for a crime in life, and also guards forest and nature."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bysen-c04",
      "source_id": "bysen-s1",
      "quote": "Bysar är avlidna människor, enligt somliga källor, som lurat till sig någon annans mark genom att flytta de råmärken som markerade gränsen mellan två ägor. Som straff får de ingen ro i graven",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut sebagian sumber, bysar adalah orang mati yang merebut tanah orang lain dengan memindahkan patok batas antara dua bidang tanah; sebagai hukuman mereka tak tenang di kubur.",
        "en": "According to some sources, bysar are dead people who tricked others out of land by moving the markers between two holdings; as punishment they get no rest in the grave."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bysen-c05",
      "source_id": "bysen-s1",
      "quote": "Om en människa går efter Bysen och flyttar tillbaka de orätta gränsmärkena till rätt ställe, då får Bysen frid. På det svenska fastlandet kallas motsvarigheten skälvrängare",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila seseorang mengikuti Bysen dan mengembalikan patok yang salah ke tempatnya, Bysen mendapat damai; di daratan Swedia (svenska) padanannya disebut skälvrängare atau osaliga lantmätare, sementara mereka bergumam \"Här är rätt, här är orätt\".",
        "en": "If someone follows Bysen and moves the wrong markers back, Bysen finds peace; on the Swedish (svenska) mainland the counterpart is called skälvrängare or osaliga lantmätare, and they mutter 'Här är rätt, här är orätt'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "bysen-c06",
      "source_id": "bysen-s1",
      "quote": "Den som Bysen får syn på i skogen, får synen förvrängd, så att denne inte kan hitta hem igen. Den som drabbas kan, genom att vända ett plagg ut och in, återfå normal syn.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Siapa pun yang terlihat oleh Bysen di hutan menjadi kacau penglihatannya sehingga tak bisa pulang; ia dapat memulihkannya dengan membalik pakaian.",
        "en": "Whoever Bysen sees in the forest has their sight twisted so they cannot find home; turning a garment inside out restores normal sight."
      }

      "source_id": "bysen-s2",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Di sma undar jordi (\"yang kecil di bawah tanah\") adalah makhluk mirip vættir dalam cerita rakyat Gotland, Swedia, yang menjaga pertanian selama tidak dibuat marah.",
    "en": "Di sma undar jordi ('the little ones underground') are vættir-like beings in the folklore of Gotland, Sweden, who look after farms as long as they are not angered.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "di-sma-undar-jordi-c01",
      "di-sma-undar-jordi-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Di sma undar jordi, atau singkatnya di sma, adalah makhluk legenda mirip vættir dari cerita rakyat pulau Gotland, Swedia. Namanya dalam bahasa Gutnish berarti \"yang kecil di bawah tanah\", dan mereka merupakan varian lokal dari kepercayaan yang lebih luas tentang \"orang tersembunyi\" atau makhluk bawah tanah (underjordiska) di daratan Swedia.",
      "en": "Di sma undar jordi, or just di sma, are legendary vættir-like beings from the folklore of the island of Gotland, Sweden. In Gutnish the name means 'the little ones underground', and they are the local form of the wider belief in the 'hidden people' or subterraneans (underjordiska) of mainland Sweden.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "di-sma-undar-jordi-c01",
        "di-sma-undar-jordi-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut kepercayaan rakyat, mereka tinggal di dekat manusia dan pertanian mereka, serta merawat pertanian beserta penghuni dan hewan-hewannya selama tidak dibuat marah. Cara klasik membuat mereka murka adalah menyiram air panas ke luar rumah tanpa peringatan sehingga mereka tersiram. Mereka juga dianggap memastikan manusia merawat tanah yang diolahnya.",
      "en": "Folk belief holds that they live near people and their farms, tending the farm, its people and its animals so long as they are not angered. The classic way to anger them is to throw hot water outdoors without warning and scald them. They are also seen as making sure people care for the land they farm.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "di-sma-undar-jordi-c02",
        "di-sma-undar-jordi-c04",
        "di-sma-undar-jordi-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut kisah-kisah Gotland, di sma hanya dapat dilihat dengan mengintip melalui lubang pada selembar kertas.",
      "en": "According to Gotland tales, di sma can be seen only by peering through a hole in a sheet of paper.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "di-sma-undar-jordi-c03"
      ]
    }

    {
      "id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-c01",
      "source_id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-s1",
      "quote": "or simply di sma, are legendary vættir-like creatures found in folklore from the island of Gotland, Sweden.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di sma undar jordi (Gutnish untuk \"yang kecil di bawah tanah\", Swedia: de små under jorden), atau singkatnya di sma, adalah makhluk legenda mirip vættir dalam cerita rakyat pulau Gotland, Swedia.",
        "en": "Di sma undar jordi (Gutnish for 'the small ones underground', Swedish: de små under jorden), or just di sma, are legendary vættir-like beings in the folklore of the island of Gotland, Sweden."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-c02",
      "source_id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-s1",
      "quote": "Di sma are said to take care of farms and the people and animals that live there, as long as one does not anger them. The most classical example of doing so is to mindlessly scald them when disposing hot water outdoors.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di sma konon merawat pertanian beserta manusia dan hewannya selama tidak dibuat marah; contoh paling klasik membuat mereka marah adalah menyiram mereka dengan air panas yang dibuang ke luar tanpa berpikir.",
        "en": "Di sma reportedly look after farms and their people and animals unless angered; the classic way to anger them is to scald them carelessly when throwing out hot water."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-c03",
      "source_id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-s1",
      "quote": "According to Gotlandic tales, di sma can only be seen by looking through a hole in a sheet of paper.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut kisah Gotland, di sma hanya dapat dilihat melalui lubang di selembar kertas.",
        "en": "Gotland tales say di sma can only be seen through a hole in a sheet of paper."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-c04",
      "source_id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-s2",
      "quote": "Di sma undar jordi (även stavat di sma undar jårdi) är en gotländsk variant på vättar. Enligt folktron är de levande väsen som bor i närheten av människorna och deras gårdar. Man bör inte hälla vatten på dem utan förvarning.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di sma undar jordi (juga dieja di sma undar jårdi) adalah varian Gotland dari vättar; menurut kepercayaan rakyat mereka makhluk hidup yang tinggal dekat manusia dan pertanian mereka, dan tidak boleh disiram air tanpa peringatan.",
        "en": "Di sma undar jordi (also spelt di sma undar jårdi) are a Gotland variant of the vättar; folk belief makes them living beings dwelling near people and their farms, who must not have water poured on them without warning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-c05",
      "source_id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-s1",
      "quote": "It is really the regional variation of the broader folklore of the \"hidden people\" (mainland Swedish: underjordiska , \"subterraneans\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka sebenarnya varian daerah dari cerita rakyat yang lebih luas tentang \"orang tersembunyi\" (Swedia daratan: underjordiska, \"penghuni bawah tanah\").",
        "en": "They are really a regional form of the broader lore of the 'hidden people' (mainland Swedish: underjordiska, 'subterraneans')."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-c06",
      "source_id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-s2",
      "quote": "De ansvarar för att människorna vårdar jorden och marken.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka bertanggung jawab memastikan manusia merawat tanah dan lahan.",
        "en": "They are responsible for people caring for the soil and the land."

    },
    {

      "source_id": "di-sma-undar-jordi-s1",
      "quote": "The Swedish rauk'n'roll band Di sma undar jårdi , active in the 1980s, was named after these creatures.",

      "statement": {

        "en": "Di sma unda jordi (Gutnish for 'the small ones under the earth' or 'the subterraneans') are a Gotland variant of the vætter."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Drak adalah roh rumah tangga terbang dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, berwujud naga kecil berekor menyala, yang membawakan gandum, mentega, dan emas curian ke pemiliknya.",
    "en": "The Drak is a flying household spirit of German folklore, shaped like a small dragon with a glowing tail, which brings stolen grain, butter and gold to its owner.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "drak-mythology-c03",
      "drak-mythology-c04",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Drak, yang juga disebut Fürdrak atau Drakel, adalah roh rumah tangga dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang sering disamakan dengan kobold atau iblis. Meski kadang dinamai Drache (naga) dan tampak seperti naga, ia hampir tak berhubungan dengan naga reptil. Namanya diduga bukan turunan langsung dari kata Latin draco, melainkan dari mandragora.",
      "en": "The Drak, also called Fürdrak or Drakel, is a household spirit of German folklore often equated with the kobold or the devil. Although sometimes named Drache (dragon) and dragon-like in looks, it has little to do with the reptilian dragon. Its name is thought to come not directly from Latin draco but from mandragora.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "drak-mythology-c01",
        "drak-mythology-c02",
        "drak-mythology-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Drak dikenal di Jerman utara dan tengah serta wilayah Slavia di sekitarnya. Ia terbang lewat cerobong untuk membawa gandum, mentega, daging asap, ham, dan emas kepada petani, sehingga banyak petani kaya diisukan memelihara Drak. Wujudnya naga kecil berekor menyala, tetapi di Pomerania timur ia juga digambarkan sebagai ular api berkepala bebek atau kuali menyala berekor; ia bisa pula menjadi angsa kelabu atau ayam basah.",
      "en": "The Drak is known in northern and central Germany and nearby Slavic lands. It flies down the chimney with grain, butter, bacon, ham and gold for the farmer, so many prosperous farmers were rumoured to keep one. It looks like a small dragon with a glowing tail, but in eastern Pomerania it is also pictured as a fiery snake with a duck's head or a glowing kettle with a tail; it can also become a grey goose or a wet hen.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "drak-mythology-c04",
        "drak-mythology-c05",
        "drak-mythology-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kepemilikan Drak sering dikaitkan dengan perjanjian dengan iblis. Ia tinggal di perapian atau jauh di dalam tungku, harus diberi makan kue, daging, susu, atau bubur jawawut, dan bila tidak diberi makan ia membalas dendam, biasanya dengan menyulut kebakaran. Orang yang melihatnya terbang dapat memaksanya menjatuhkan sebagian harta dengan seruan seperti \"lat fallen, wat nich dien hört\" (\"jatuhkan yang bukan milikmu\").",
      "en": "Owning a Drak was often tied to a pact with the devil. It lives in the hearth or deep in the oven, must be fed with cakes, meat, milk or millet porridge, and if left unfed it takes revenge, typically by starting a fire. Anyone who sees it flying can force it to drop part of its treasure with a formula such as 'lat fallen, wat nich dien hört' ('drop what isn't yours').",
      "claim_ids": [
        "drak-mythology-c07",
        "drak-mythology-c08",
        "drak-mythology-c09"
      ]
    }

    {

      "title": "Drac",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c01",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "is a household spirit from German folklore often identified with the Kobold or the devil (German Teufel), both of which are also used as synonymous terms for Drak. Otherwise it is also known as Drache (dragon) but has nothing much to do with the reptilian monster in general.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drak (juga Fürdrak, Drakel) adalah roh rumah tangga dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang sering disamakan dengan Kobold atau iblis (Teufel); ia juga disebut Drache (naga) tetapi hampir tak berhubungan dengan naga reptil.",
        "en": "The Drak (also Fürdrak, Drakel) is a household spirit of German folklore often equated with the Kobold or devil (Teufel); it is also called Drache (dragon) but has little to do with the reptile monster."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c02",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s1",
      "quote": "The etymology of drak, it has been argued, does not descend directly from Latin draco (German: Drachen, Middle High German: trachen ) for \"dragon\", but rather from the name mandragora",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Telah diajukan bahwa kata drak tidak diturunkan langsung dari kata Latin draco (\"naga\"), melainkan dari nama mandragora.",
        "en": "It has been argued that drak does not come straight from Latin draco ('dragon') but from the name mandragora."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c03",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Der Drak (oder auch Fürdrak, Drakel, Alf, Stöpke und Gluswanz genannt) ist ein fliegender Hausgeist. Obwohl er äußerlich einem Drachen ähnelt, hat er mit diesem wohl nichts gemeinsam.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drak (juga disebut Fürdrak, Drakel, Alf, Stöpke, dan Gluswanz) adalah roh rumah terbang; meski tampak seperti naga, ia agaknya tak ada kaitannya dengan naga.",
        "en": "The Drak (also called Fürdrak, Drakel, Alf, Stöpke and Gluswanz) is a flying house spirit; though it looks like a dragon it probably has nothing in common with one."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c04",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Er ist in Nord- und Mitteldeutschland beheimatet, sowie auch in angrenzenden slawischen Gebieten und bringt dem Bauern durch den Kamin Korn, Getreide, Butter, Speck, Schinken und Gold. Daher wurde vielen wohlhabenden Bauern auch nachgesagt, dass sie einen Drak in ihren Diensten hätten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drak berasal dari Jerman utara dan tengah (Nord- und Mitteldeutschland) serta wilayah Slavia di sekitarnya, dan membawa gandum, mentega, daging asap, ham, dan emas kepada petani lewat cerobong; karena itu banyak petani kaya diisukan memelihara Drak.",
        "en": "The Drak is at home in northern and central Germany (Nord- und Mitteldeutschland) and adjacent Slavic areas and brings the farmer corn, grain, butter, bacon, ham and gold through the chimney; hence many wealthy farmers were said to have a Drak in service."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c05",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Das Erscheinungsbild eines Drak ist das eines kleinen Drachen mit glühendem Schweif, mit dem er durch die Lüfte zieht. Im östlichen Pommern kennt man ihn als Alf und mancherorts auch als feurige Schlange mit Entenkopf oder als glühenden Kessel mit Schwanz.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drak berwujud naga kecil berekor menyala yang melayang di udara; di Pomerania (Pommern) timur ia dikenal sebagai Alf, dan di beberapa tempat sebagai ular api berkepala bebek atau kuali menyala berekor.",
        "en": "The Drak looks like a small dragon with a glowing tail as it travels through the air; in eastern Pomerania (Pommern) it is called Alf, and in places a fiery snake with a duck's head or a glowing kettle with a tail."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c06",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Er kann aber auch die Gestalt ändern und erscheint dann als feuriger Wiesbaum mit breitem Kopf ... er kann sich aber auch in Tiere verwandeln, zum Beispiel in eine graue Gans oder ein nasses Huhn. Man kann ihn, mithilfe bestimmter Formeln, dazu zwingen einen Teil seiner Schätze abzuwerfen. Beispiele für solche Formeln sind Halb Part oder lat fallen, wat nich dien hört",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drak bisa berubah wujud menjadi batang kayu menyala berkepala lebar, atau hewan seperti angsa kelabu dan ayam basah; dengan seruan tertentu seperti \"Halb Part\" atau \"lat fallen, wat nich dien hört\" ia bisa dipaksa menjatuhkan sebagian hartanya.",
        "en": "The Drak can change shape into a fiery broad-headed pole, or animals such as a grey goose or a wet hen; certain formulas like 'Halb Part' or 'lat fallen, wat nich dien hört' compel it to drop part of its treasure."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c07",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Drac, Drak či Drachen, je domácí duch z německého folklóru, který přináší do domácnosti, kterou obývá, bohatství.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drac atau Drak adalah roh rumah tangga dari cerita rakyat Jerman yang membawa kekayaan ke rumah yang dihuninya.",
        "en": "Drac or Drak is a household spirit of German folklore that brings wealth to the home it lives in."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c08",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Drac létá vzduchem a do domu vstupuje skrze komín či díru ve střeše. Má podobu hořícího bidla, mužíčka v červené vestě, nebo zvířete jako kuře, kohout či ještěrka. Jindy vypadá jako ohnivá koule či planoucí hvězda. Je úzce spjat s ohněm, což vedlo k ztotožňování s ďáblem, a přebývá v krbu nebo hluboko v peci.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drac terbang di udara dan masuk rumah lewat cerobong atau lubang atap; ia berwujud batang menyala, lelaki kecil berompi merah, atau hewan seperti ayam dan kadal, kadang bola api; ia erat dengan api dan tinggal di perapian atau jauh di dalam tungku.",
        "en": "Drac flies through the air and enters by the chimney or a hole in the roof; it takes the form of a burning pole, a little man in a red vest, or an animal such as a hen or lizard, at times a fireball; it is closely bound to fire and lives in the hearth or deep in the oven."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c09",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Drac je získáván skrze smlouvu s ďáblem, ale může se zrodit jinými způsoby. Líhne se například z vejce bez žloutku, proto se má takové vejce vyhodit. ... Draca bylo nutno živit, podávaly se mu tak pokrmy jako koláče, maso, mléko nebo jáhlová kaše. Když není krmen, tak se mstí, typicky založením požáru.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Drac diperoleh lewat perjanjian dengan iblis atau menetas dari telur tanpa kuning; ia harus diberi makan kue, daging, susu, atau bubur jawawut, dan bila tidak diberi makan ia membalas dendam, biasanya dengan menyulut kebakaran.",
        "en": "Drac is gained through a pact with the devil or hatches from a yolkless egg; it must be fed cakes, meat, milk or millet porridge, and if unfed it takes revenge, usually by setting a fire."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c10",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Es ist wahrscheinlich, dass der Glaube an den Drak durch Beobachtungen von Meteoriten, Sternschnuppen, Nordlicht oder dem Feuerschein eines Kamins entstand.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kepercayaan akan Drak kemungkinan muncul dari pengamatan meteor, bintang jatuh, cahaya utara, atau cahaya api cerobong.",
        "en": "Belief in the Drak probably arose from observations of meteorites, shooting stars, northern lights or a chimney's firelight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c11",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s2",
      "quote": "Ehm Welk beschreibt in seinem Roman Die Heiden von Kummerow das Wesen eines Draks, welchen angeblich der böse Müller Düker beherbergt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam novel Die Heiden von Kummerow, Ehm Welk menggambarkan Drak yang konon dipelihara si penggiling jahat Düker.",
        "en": "In his novel Die Heiden von Kummerow, Ehm Welk describes a Drak supposedly kept by the wicked miller Düker."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "drak-mythology-c12",
      "source_id": "drak-mythology-s3",
      "quote": "Obilí se před krádeží dracem mělo chránit vyskládáním snopů v každém z rohů stodoly do kříže.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gandum dilindungi dari pencurian Drac dengan menyusun berkas gandum bersilang di setiap sudut lumbung.",
        "en": "Grain was protected from the Drac's theft by laying sheaves crosswise in each corner of the barn."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Duneyrr adalah salah satu dari empat rusa jantan dalam mitologi Nordik yang menggerogoti dahan pohon dunia Yggdrasill.",
    "en": "Duneyrr is one of the four stags of Norse mythology that gnaw the branches of the world tree Yggdrasill.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "duneyrr-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Duneyrr adalah salah satu dari empat rusa jantan yang hidup di pohon dunia Yggdrasill bersama Dáinn, Dvalinn, dan Duraþrór. Menurut Edda Puitis, rusa-rusa itu menjulurkan leher ke atas untuk menggigit dahan-dahannya, dan embun pagi yang terkumpul di tanduk mereka mengalir menjadi sungai-sungai dunia.",
      "en": "Duneyrr is one of four stags living on the world tree Yggdrasill, together with Dáinn, Dvalinn and Duraþrór. The Poetic Edda says these deer stretch their necks upward to bite its branches, while morning dew pooling in their antlers flows off as the rivers of the world.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "duneyrr-c01",
        "duneyrr-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu-satunya puisi Nordik Kuno yang menyebut rusa-rusa ini adalah Grímnismál. Snorri Sturluson meringkas baitnya dalam Gylfaginning: keempat rusa berlari di dahan pohon ash dan memakan dedaunannya. Di pohon yang sama tinggal pula ular Nidhogg di akarnya dan tupai Ratatosk yang berlari naik-turun.",
      "en": "Grímnismál is the one Old Norse poem that names these stags. Snorri Sturluson summarised the stanza in Gylfaginning: the four run along the branches of the ash and eat its leaves. The same tree also holds the serpent Nidhogg at its root and the squirrel Ratatosk running up and down.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "duneyrr-c02",
        "duneyrr-c03",
        "duneyrr-c08",
        "duneyrr-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada 1824 Finnur Magnússon mengusulkan bahwa rusa-rusa itu melambangkan angin. Ia mengartikan Duneyrr sebagai \"Yang Bergemuruh di Telinga\" dan menggolongkannya sebagai angin kencang, sementara gigitan rusa pada daun pohon ia baca sebagai angin yang mengoyak awan.",
      "en": "In 1824 Finnur Magnússon proposed that the stags stood for winds. He read Duneyrr as 'Thundering in the Ear' and classed it among the strong winds, while the deer biting the tree's leaves he took as winds ripping at clouds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "duneyrr-c04",
        "duneyrr-c05",
        "duneyrr-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Banyak sarjana menganggap bait yang menyebut keempat rusa itu lebih muda daripada bait di sekitarnya. Finnur Jónsson menduga mula-mula hanya ada satu rusa yang kemudian dilipatgandakan menjadi empat.",
      "en": "Many scholars regard the stanza naming the four stags as younger than those around it. Finnur Jónsson guessed that there was at first a single stag, later multiplied into four.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "duneyrr-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c01",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, four stags or harts (male red deer) eat among the branches of the world tree Yggdrasill. According to the Poetic Edda, the stags crane their necks upward to chomp at the branches. The morning dew gathers in their horns and forms the rivers of the world. Their names are given as Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr and Duraþrór.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, empat rusa jantan (rusa merah jantan) makan di antara dahan pohon dunia Yggdrasill; menurut Edda Puitis (Poetic Edda) mereka menjulurkan leher untuk menggerogoti dahan, embun pagi terkumpul di tanduk mereka dan menjadi sungai-sungai dunia; nama mereka Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr, dan Duraþrór.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology four stags (male red deer) feed among the branches of the world tree Yggdrasill; per the Poetic Edda they stretch their necks up to gnaw the branches, and dew collecting in their horns becomes the world's rivers; they are named Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr and Duraþrór."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c02",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s1",
      "quote": "The poem Grímnismál, a part of the Poetic Edda, is the only extant piece of Old Norse poetry to mention the stags.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Puisi Grímnismál dalam Edda Puitis (Poetic Edda) adalah satu-satunya puisi Nordik Kuno (Old Norse) yang masih ada yang menyebut rusa-rusa itu.",
        "en": "Grímnismál, in the Poetic Edda, is the only surviving Old Norse poem that names the stags."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c03",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s1",
      "quote": "En fjórir hirtir renna í limum asksins ok bíta barr, þeir heita svá: Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr, Duraþrór.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Gylfaginning, bagian Edda Prosa karya Snorri Sturluson: empat rusa berlari di dahan pohon ash dan menggigit dedaunannya; nama mereka Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr, Duraþrór.",
        "en": "In Gylfaginning, part of Snorri Sturluson's Prose Edda: four harts run in the ash's limbs and bite its foliage; they are called Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr, Duraþrór."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c04",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s1",
      "quote": "and Duneyrr (\"Thundering in the Ear\") and Duraþrór (\"Thriving Slumber\", perhaps referencing snoring) to be heavy winds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Finnur Magnússon menafsirkan Duneyrr sebagai \"Yang Bergemuruh di Telinga\" dan menganggapnya angin kencang.",
        "en": "Finnur Magnússon took Duneyrr to mean 'Thundering in the Ear' and saw it as a strong wind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c05",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s1",
      "quote": "He interpreted the stags biting the leaves of the tree as winds tearing at clouds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Finnur Magnússon menafsirkan rusa-rusa yang menggigit dedaunan pohon itu sebagai angin yang mengoyak awan.",
        "en": "Finnur Magnússon read the stags biting the tree's leaves as winds tearing at clouds."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c06",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s1",
      "quote": "Finnur Jónsson surmised that there was originally only one stag which had later been turned into four, probably one on each side.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Finnur Jónsson menduga awalnya hanya ada satu rusa yang kemudian dijadikan empat, mungkin satu di tiap sisi.",
        "en": "Finnur Jónsson supposed that there was first only a single stag, later made into four, likely one per side."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c07",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s2",
      "quote": "Dain, Dwalin (auch Dvalin oder Dvalar), Duneyr und Durathror sind vier Hirsche in der nordischen Mythologie, die zu den Tieren am Weltenbaum Yggdrasil gehören.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dain, Dwalin, Duneyr, dan Durathror adalah empat rusa dalam mitologi Nordik yang termasuk hewan-hewan di pohon dunia Yggdrasil.",
        "en": "Dain, Dwalin, Duneyr and Durathror are four stags in Norse mythology belonging to the animals of the world tree Yggdrasil."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c08",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s3",
      "quote": "Dåin, Dvalin, Dunøyr og Duratro (norrønt: Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr og Duraþrór) er fire hjortedyr som ifølge Gylvaginning i Den yngre Edda løper omkring på greinene til verdenstreet Yggdrasil og beiter i løvverket.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dåin, Dvalin, Dunøyr, dan Duratro (Nordik Kuno: Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr, Duraþrór) adalah empat rusa yang menurut Gylfaginning dalam Edda Muda berlari di dahan pohon dunia Yggdrasil dan merumput di dedaunannya.",
        "en": "Dåin, Dvalin, Dunøyr and Duratro (Old Norse: Dáinn, Dvalinn, Duneyrr, Duraþrór) are four deer that, per Gylfaginning in the Younger Edda, run about Yggdrasil's branches and graze its foliage."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c09",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s3",
      "quote": "Rundt rota på treet snor slangen Nidhogg seg, mens ekornet Ratatosk løper opp og ned og bringer hatefulle ord mellom dem.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di sekitar akar pohon itu ular Nidhogg melilit, sementara tupai Ratatosk berlari naik-turun membawa kata-kata kebencian.",
        "en": "Round the tree's root coils the serpent Nidhogg, while the squirrel Ratatosk runs up and down carrying hateful words."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "duneyrr-c10",
      "source_id": "duneyrr-s1",
      "quote": "In his influential 1824 work, Finnur Magnússon suggested that the stags represented winds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam karyanya yang berpengaruh tahun 1824, Finnur Magnússon mengusulkan bahwa rusa-rusa itu melambangkan angin.",
        "en": "In an influential 1824 work, Finnur Magnússon proposed that the stags stood for winds."
      }
    }
  ],

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Duraþrór adalah salah satu dari empat rusa jantan yang hidup di pohon dunia Yggdrasill bersama Dáinn, Dvalinn, dan Duneyrr. Menurut Edda Puitis, rusa-rusa itu menjulurkan leher ke atas untuk menggigit dahan-dahannya, dan embun pagi yang terkumpul di tanduk mereka mengalir menjadi sungai-sungai dunia.",
      "en": "Duraþrór is one of four stags living on the world tree Yggdrasill, together with Dáinn, Dvalinn and Duneyrr. The Poetic Edda says these deer stretch their necks upward to bite its branches, while morning dew pooling in their antlers flows off as the rivers of the world.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dura-ror-c01",
        "dura-ror-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Satu-satunya puisi Nordik Kuno yang menyebut rusa-rusa ini adalah Grímnismál. Snorri Sturluson meringkas baitnya dalam Gylfaginning: keempat rusa berlari di dahan pohon ash dan memakan dedaunannya. Di pohon yang sama tinggal pula ular Nidhogg di akarnya dan tupai Ratatosk yang berlari naik-turun.",
      "en": "Grímnismál is the one Old Norse poem that names these stags. Snorri Sturluson summarised the stanza in Gylfaginning: the four run along the branches of the ash and eat its leaves. The same tree also holds the serpent Nidhogg at its root and the squirrel Ratatosk running up and down.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dura-ror-c02",
        "dura-ror-c03",
        "dura-ror-c08",
        "dura-ror-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Pada 1824 Finnur Magnússon mengusulkan bahwa rusa-rusa itu melambangkan angin. Ia mengartikan Duraþrór sebagai \"Tidur Lelap yang Subur\", barangkali merujuk dengkuran, dan menggolongkannya sebagai angin kencang; gigitan rusa pada daun ia baca sebagai angin yang mengoyak awan.",
      "en": "In 1824 Finnur Magnússon proposed that the stags stood for winds. He read Duraþrór as 'Thriving Slumber', possibly hinting at snoring, and classed it among the strong winds; the deer biting the leaves he took as winds ripping at clouds.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "dura-ror-c04",
        "dura-ror-c05",
        "dura-ror-c10"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Banyak sarjana menganggap bait yang menyebut keempat rusa itu lebih muda daripada bait di sekitarnya. Finnur Jónsson menduga mula-mula hanya ada satu rusa yang kemudian dilipatgandakan menjadi empat.",
      "en": "Many scholars regard the stanza naming the four stags as younger than those around it. Finnur Jónsson guessed that there was at first a single stag, later multiplied into four.",

    },
    {
      "id": "dura-ror-c09",
      "source_id": "dura-ror-s3",
      "quote": "Rundt rota på treet snor slangen Nidhogg seg, mens ekornet Ratatosk løper opp og ned og bringer hatefulle ord mellom dem.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di sekitar akar pohon itu ular Nidhogg melilit, sementara tupai Ratatosk berlari naik-turun membawa kata-kata kebencian.",
        "en": "Round the tree's root coils the serpent Nidhogg, while the squirrel Ratatosk runs up and down carrying hateful words."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "dura-ror-c10",
      "source_id": "dura-ror-s1",
      "quote": "In his influential 1824 work, Finnur Magnússon suggested that the stags represented winds.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam karyanya yang berpengaruh tahun 1824, Finnur Magnússon mengusulkan bahwa rusa-rusa itu melambangkan angin.",
        "en": "In an influential 1824 work, Finnur Magnússon proposed that the stags stood for winds."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ellert adalah salah satu dari dua raksasa perampok dalam legenda Belanda dari Drenthe, bersama Brammert, yang konon merampok para pelancong di padang Ellertsveld dekat Schoonoord.",
    "en": "Ellert is one of two robber giants in a Dutch legend from Drenthe, alongside Brammert, said to have robbed travellers on the Ellertsveld heath near Schoonoord.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "ellert-c01",
      "ellert-c02"

  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Ellert dan Brammert adalah raksasa legenda dari cerita rakyat Belanda yang konon merampok para pelancong di Drenthe. Kisahnya dilokalkan di sekitar Schoonoord, dan para peneliti cerita rakyat menggolongkannya ke dalam tipe cerita internasional \"Robbers' Alarm Bell\"; kisah itu dikaitkan dengan tempat tersebut untuk menjelaskan nama Ellertsveld dan Brammershoop.",
      "en": "Ellert and Brammert are legendary giants of Dutch folklore who supposedly robbed travellers in Drenthe. The tale is set around Schoonoord, and folklorists class it under the international tale type 'Robbers' Alarm Bell'; it was tied to the area to explain the names Ellertsveld and Brammershoop.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ellert-c01",
        "ellert-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi tertua dicatat pendeta Johan Picardt pada 1660 dalam Annales Drenthiae. Di sana Ellert dan Brammer adalah sepasang pembunuh, ayah dan anak, yang menjarah kereta, membunuh pelancong, dan berbuat banyak kejahatan; padang Ellers-veldt konon dinamai menurut Ellert. Menurut kisah itu, seorang gadis yang mereka culik akhirnya menggorok leher sang ayah yang sedang tidur lalu melarikan diri.",
      "en": "The oldest version was written down by the minister Johan Picardt in 1660 in the Annales Drenthiae. There Ellert and Brammer are a pair of murderers, father and son, who plundered wagons, killed travellers and did much evil; the Ellers-veldt heath was said to be named after Ellert. In that account a girl they had abducted finally cut the sleeping father's throat and escaped.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ellert-c03",
        "ellert-c04",
        "ellert-c12"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam versi populer yang lebih baru, kedua perampok memasang tali berlonceng untuk mengetahui kedatangan pelancong di dekat gua mereka. Seorang gadis tawanan mengurus rumah tangga mereka selama tujuh tahun, lalu menceritakan rahasianya di depan gereja dan menandai jalan ke gua dengan kacang polong, sehingga warga desa dapat menangkap para perampok.",
      "en": "In a later popular version the two robbers stretched a rope with a bell to learn when travellers came near their cave. A captive girl kept house for them for seven years, then told her story outside the church and marked the way to the cave with peas, so the villagers could seize the robbers.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "ellert-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Para perampok itu baru menjadi raksasa pada akhir abad ke-19, di bawah pengaruh legenda Drenthe bahwa hunebed (makam batu besar) dibangun oleh raksasa; sejak abad ke-20, Ellert dan Brammert makin sering dianggap pembangun hunebed. Nama para perampok kemungkinan diciptakan dari nama tempat Ellertsveld dan Brammershoop yang maknanya sudah tak dipahami.",
      "en": "The robbers only became giants at the end of the 19th century, under the influence of the Drenthe legend that the hunebeds (megalithic tombs) were built by giants; from the 20th century Ellert and Brammert were increasingly seen as builders of hunebeds.
