  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "ellert-c01",
      "source_id": "ellert-s1",
      "quote": "Ellert and Brammert are legendary giants from Dutch folklore who supposedly robbed travellers in Drenthe, Netherlands.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ellert dan Brammert adalah raksasa legenda dari cerita rakyat Belanda (Dutch) yang konon merampok para pelancong di Drenthe, Belanda (Netherlands).",
        "en": "Ellert and Brammert are legendary giants of Dutch folklore said to have robbed travellers in Drenthe, Netherlands."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c02",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "De sage van Ellert en Brammert wordt door volksverhaalonderzoekers geclassificeerd onder het internationale verhaaltype Robbers' Alarm Bell en is aan Schoonoord en omgeving gehecht om de namen Ellertsveld en Brammershoop te verklaren.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Para peneliti cerita rakyat menggolongkan legenda Ellert dan Brammert ke dalam tipe cerita internasional Robbers' Alarm Bell; legenda itu dilekatkan pada Schoonoord dan sekitarnya untuk menjelaskan nama Ellertsveld dan Brammershoop.",
        "en": "Folklorists class the legend of Ellert and Brammert under the international tale type Robbers' Alarm Bell; it was attached to Schoonoord and its surroundings to explain the names Ellertsveld and Brammershoop."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c03",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "Omtrent dese tijdt meent men dat geleeft hebben die twee vermaerde Moordenaars ghenaemt Ellert en Brammer, Vader en Soon die haer een tijdt langh onthouden hebben op dat groote ruyme Veldt tusschen Scharloo en Sweel, dat men seydt van desen Ellert ghenaemt te zijn Ellers-veldt, en Brammers-hoop van desen Brammer.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam versi tertua (Johan Picardt, 1660), Ellert dan Brammer (Brammert) adalah dua pembunuh termasyhur, ayah dan anak (Vader en Soon), yang tinggal di padang luas antara Scharloo dan Sweel; padang itu konon dinamai Ellers-veldt menurut Ellert, dan Brammers-hoop menurut Brammer.",
        "en": "In the oldest version (Johan Picardt, 1660), Ellert and Brammer (Brammert) were two notorious murderers, father and son (Vader en Soon), living on the wide heath between Scharloo and Sweel; it was said to be called Ellers-veldt after Ellert, and Brammers-hoop after Brammer."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c04",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "Dese hebben Waghens en Karren gheplondert Passagiers en Landsaten vermoordt Brand-brieven uyt-geschreven en seer veel quaedt ghedaen",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Mereka menjarah kereta dan gerobak, membunuh pelancong dan penduduk, menulis surat ancaman pembakaran, dan berbuat banyak kejahatan; seorang gadis yang mereka culik kemudian menggorok leher sang ayah yang tidur lalu kabur.",
        "en": "They plundered wagons and carts, murdered travellers and inhabitants, wrote arson threats and did much evil; a girl they abducted later cut the sleeping father's throat and fled."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c05",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "vertelt dat de twee rovers een touw spannen waaraan een bel is vastgemaakt. Zo kunnen ze erachter komen of er voorbijgangers langs komen in de buurt van hun grot.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Versi populer yang lebih baru menceritakan kedua perampok merentangkan tali berlonceng untuk mengetahui pelintas di dekat gua mereka; gadis tawanan mereka mengurus rumah tujuh tahun, bercerita di depan gereja, dan menandai jalan dengan kacang polong sehingga warga menangkap mereka.",
        "en": "A later popular version tells that the two robbers stretched a rope with a bell to detect passers-by near their cave; their captive girl kept house seven years, spoke out at the church and marked the path with peas, so villagers caught them."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c06",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "De persoonsnamen van de rovers zijn waarschijnlijk bedacht naar aanleiding van de niet meer begrepen aardrijkskundige benamingen Ellertsveld en Brammershoop.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama pribadi para perampok kemungkinan diciptakan dari nama geografis Ellertsveld dan Brammershoop yang maknanya sudah tak dipahami lagi.",
        "en": "The robbers' personal names were probably made up from the no-longer-understood place names Ellertsveld and Brammershoop."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c07",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "Aan het eind van de 19e eeuw zijn de rovers uitgegroeid tot reuzen, onder invloed van de bekende Drentse hunebeddensage, waarin wordt beweerd dat de hunebedden door reuzen zouden zijn gebouwd. Ellert en Brammert worden vanaf de 20e eeuw in de mondelinge overlevering steeds vaker als bouwers van hunebedden gezien.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pada akhir abad ke-19 para perampok berkembang menjadi raksasa karena pengaruh legenda hunebed Drenthe (Drentse) bahwa hunebed dibangun raksasa; sejak abad ke-20 Ellert dan Brammert makin sering dianggap pembangun hunebed.",
        "en": "At the end of the 19th century the robbers grew into giants under the influence of the Drenthe (Drentse) hunebed legend that giants built the hunebeds; from the 20th century Ellert and Brammert were increasingly seen as hunebed builders."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c08",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "Een literaire variant, die is verzonnen door volksverhalenschrijver Joseph Cohen, vertelt dat de twee rovende reuzen mensen beroven, omdat ze zoeken naar de sleutel van geluk.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Varian sastra rekaan penulis cerita rakyat Joseph Cohen menceritakan kedua raksasa merampok karena mencari kunci kebahagiaan; Ellert membunuh Brammert karena mengira ia telah menemukannya, dan padang itu dinamai Ellertsveld.",
        "en": "A literary variant invented by folk-tale writer Joseph Cohen tells that the two giants rob because they seek the key to happiness; Ellert kills Brammert thinking he has found it, and the heath is named Ellertsveld."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c09",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "Volgens de sage van Ellert en Brammert waren de hoofdrolspelers twee broers, of vader en zoon, die in een hol in de grond leefden in wat nu nog steeds het Ellertsveld heet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda, Ellert dan Brammert adalah dua bersaudara atau ayah dan anak yang tinggal di lubang di tanah di tempat yang kini masih disebut Ellertsveld.",
        "en": "According to the legend, Ellert and Brammert were two brothers, or father and son, living in a hole in the ground in what is still called the Ellertsveld."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "ellert-c10",
      "source_id": "ellert-s2",
      "quote": "In 2004 verscheen het stripboek 'Twei Drentse donderstienen', waarin de sage in het Drents wordt uitgebeeld.",

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Endill adalah jötunn, dewa, atau raja laut dalam mitologi Nordik yang namanya bertahan terutama dalam kenning puitis untuk laut dan kapal.",
    "en": "Endill is a jötunn, god or sea-king of Norse mythology whose name survives mainly in poetic kennings for the sea and ships.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "endill-c01",
      "endill-c03",
      "endill-c05"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Endill digambarkan sebagai jötunn, dewa, atau raja laut. Dalam daftar Nafnaþulur di Edda Prosa ia hanya muncul sebagai sebuah nama di antara 75 raja laut.",
      "en": "In Norse mythology Endill is described as a jötunn, a god or a sea-king. In the Nafnaþulur list of the Prose Edda he appears only as a name among 75 sea-kings.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "endill-c01",
        "endill-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya lebih banyak muncul dalam kenning, ungkapan puitis kiasan. Dalam Þórsdrápa, \"á Endils mó\" (\"ke padang Endill\") berarti laut, dan pada batu rune Karlevi \"[Æ]ndils iarmungrundaʀ\" (\"tanah luas Endill\") juga menunjuk laut. Kenning lain menyebut kapal sebagai \"kuda Endill\" (Endils eykur), dan laut sebagai \"bumi Endill\" (Endils fold).",
      "en": "His name turns up mostly in kennings, figurative poetic phrases. In Þórsdrápa, 'á Endils mó' ('onto Endill's moor') stands for the sea, and on the Karlevi runestone '[Æ]ndils iarmungrundaʀ' ('Endill's vast ground') likewise means the sea. Other kennings call a ship 'Endill's horse' (Endils eykur) and the sea 'Endill's earth' (Endils fold).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "endill-c03",
        "endill-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Proyek Rundata dari Skandinavia menerjemahkan kenning batu Karlevi sebagai \"hamparan Endill\" atau \"wilayah kekuasaan Endill yang perkasa\", dan menduga Endill mungkin dewa kapal-kapal di laut.",
      "en": "The Scandinavian Rundata project renders the Karlevi kenning as 'Endill's expanses' or 'Endill's mighty dominion', and suggests Endill may have been a god of seagoing vessels.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "endill-c04",
        "endill-c07"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "endill-c01",
      "source_id": "endill-s1",
      "quote": "In Norse mythology, Endill is a jötunn, god or sea-king. He is attested in the Prose Edda list Nafnaþulur, and in various kennings.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam mitologi Nordik, Endill adalah jötunn, dewa, atau raja laut yang tercatat dalam daftar Nafnaþulur di Edda Prosa (Prose Edda) dan berbagai kenning.",
        "en": "In Norse mythology Endill is a jötunn, god or sea-king, attested in the Prose Edda list Nafnaþulur and in various kennings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "endill-c02",
      "source_id": "endill-s1",
      "quote": "In Nafnaþulur, he is mentioned as only a name in a list of 75 sea kings.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Nafnaþulur ia hanya disebut sebagai nama dalam daftar 75 raja laut.",
        "en": "In Nafnaþulur he is listed merely as a name among 75 sea kings."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "endill-c03",
      "source_id": "endill-s1",
      "quote": "In Þórsdrápa his name appears in the kenning á Endils mó which means \"onto Endil's moor\" and refers to the sea. Likewise, on the Karlevi Runestone his name appears in [Æ]ndils iarmungrundaʀ (\"Endill's vast ground\") which is a kenning for the sea.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Þórsdrápa namanya muncul dalam kenning á Endils mó (\"ke padang Endill\") yang berarti laut; demikian pula pada batu rune Karlevi (Karlevi Runestone) dalam [Æ]ndils iarmungrundaʀ (\"tanah luas Endill\"), kenning untuk laut.",
        "en": "In Þórsdrápa his name occurs in the kenning á Endils mó ('onto Endill's moor'), meaning the sea; likewise on the Karlevi Runestone in [Æ]ndils iarmungrundaʀ ('Endill's vast ground'), a sea kenning."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "endill-c04",
      "source_id": "endill-s1",
      "quote": "The Scandinavian Rundata project translates it as \"Endill's expanses\" or \"Endill's mighty dominion\", and suggests that Endill may have been a god of the vessels of the sea.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Proyek Rundata dari Skandinavia (Scandinavian Rundata) menerjemahkannya sebagai \"hamparan Endill\" atau \"wilayah kekuasaan Endill yang perkasa\" dan menduga Endill mungkin dewa kapal-kapal laut.",
        "en": "The Scandinavian Rundata project renders it 'Endill's expanses' or 'Endill's mighty dominion' and suggests Endill may have been a god of sea vessels."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "endill-c05",
      "source_id": "endill-s1",
      "quote": "Other kennings where he appears are Endils öndur meaning \"Endil's ski\" and Endils eykur meaning \"Endil's horse\" both referring to ships. Another kenning Endils fold which means \"Endil's earth\" is a kenning for the sea.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kenning lain: Endils öndur (\"ski Endill\") dan Endils eykur (\"kuda Endill\") untuk kapal, serta Endils fold (\"bumi Endill\") untuk laut.",
        "en": "Further kennings: Endils öndur ('Endill's ski') and Endils eykur ('Endill's horse') for ships, and Endils fold ('Endill's earth') for the sea."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "endill-c06",
      "source_id": "endill-s2",
      "quote": "Endill es el nombre de varios personajes legendarios que aparecen en diversas fuentes literarias y sagas nórdicas como jotun, rey del mar o dios nórdico.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Spanyol, Endill adalah nama beberapa tokoh legenda dalam berbagai sumber sastra dan saga Nordik sebagai jotun, raja laut, atau dewa Nordik.",
        "en": "The Spanish article says Endill is the name of several legendary figures in various literary sources and Norse sagas, as a jotun, sea-king or Norse god."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "endill-c07",
      "source_id": "endill-s3",
      "quote": "O projecto Rundata o traduz como a \"expansão de Endill\" ou o \"poderoso domínio de Endill\", e sugere que Endill possa ser um deus dos navios que navegavam os mares.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Proyek Rundata menerjemahkannya sebagai \"hamparan Endill\" atau \"kekuasaan perkasa Endill\" dan menduga Endill dewa kapal-kapal yang mengarungi laut.",
        "en": "The Rundata project renders it 'Endill's expanse' or 'Endill's mighty dominion' and suggests Endill was a god of ships sailing the seas."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "endill-c08",
      "source_id": "endill-s2",
      "quote": "Otros kenning relacionados se refieren a Endils öndur que significa el \"cielo de Endil\" y Endils eykur que significa el \"caballo de Endil\" ambos se refieren a barcos",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol mengartikan Endils öndur sebagai \"langit Endill\" (cielo de Endil), dan Endils eykur sebagai \"kuda Endill\", keduanya untuk kapal.",
        "en": "The Spanish article glosses Endils öndur as 'Endill's sky' (cielo de Endil) and Endils eykur as 'Endill's horse', both for ships."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Erdhenne (\"ayam betina tanah\") adalah roh rumah dalam cerita rakyat Jerman dari kawasan Alpen, Oberpfalz, dan Bayern yang berkotek untuk memperingatkan penghuni rumah akan bahaya.",
    "en": "The Erdhenne ('earth hen') is a house spirit of German folklore from the Alp region, the Upper Palatinate and Bavaria that clucks to warn a household of danger.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "erdhenne-c01",
      "erdhenne-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Erdhenne, yang juga disebut Erdhühnlein, Erdglucke, Erdglutsch, Herdhendl, atau Coluber domesticus, adalah roh rumah dari cerita rakyat Jerman di kawasan Alpen, Oberpfalz, dan Bayern. Suaranya konon terdengar dari dalam tanah atau sudut gelap ruangan, mirip kotek induk ayam yang menuntun anak-anaknya, dan dari situlah namanya berasal.",
      "en": "The Erdhenne, also called Erdhühnlein, Erdglucke, Erdglutsch, Herdhendl or Coluber domesticus, is a house spirit of German folklore in the Alp region, the Upper Palatinate and Bavaria. Its voice is said to come out of the ground or a dark corner of the room, like a mother hen leading her chicks, which is how it got its name.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "erdhenne-c01",
        "erdhenne-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Seperti katak Unke atau ular rumah, Erdhenne dianggap roh pelindung yang baik hati. Biasanya ia tak terlihat dan hanya berkotek keras untuk memperingatkan penghuni akan bahaya yang mengancam mereka atau rumah; menurut beberapa legenda, ia bahkan menjawab pertanyaan tentang bahaya itu bila ditanya langsung.",
      "en": "Like the Unke toad or the house snake, the Erdhenne is held to be a kindly guardian spirit. Usually unseen, it just clucks loudly to warn the household of dangers threatening them or the house; some legends say it will even answer questions about the danger if asked directly.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "erdhenne-c02",
        "erdhenne-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Bila menampakkan diri, ia berupa ayam betina tua berbulu kusut kelabu abu dengan leher pendek, dan kemunculannya meramalkan kematian dalam setahun, biasanya orang yang melihatnya. Menurut legenda lain, bila ia berkotek dan mengepakkan sayap sembilan kali, kepala rumah tangga akan jatuh sakit parah.",
      "en": "When it does show itself it looks like an old, shaggy, ash-grey hen with a short neck, and its appearance foretells a death within the year, usually of whoever sees it. Another legend says that if it clucks and flaps its wings nine times, the head of the house will fall mortally ill.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "erdhenne-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Kepercayaan ini sudah lama diolok-olok. I. G. Schmidt dalam kumpulan takhayulnya tahun 1718 mengaku pernah menangkap \"Erd-Hüngen\" di gudang bawah tanah yang ternyata hanya seekor tikus, dan Johann Andreas Schmeller dalam Bayerisches Wörterbuch menganggapnya hasil salah tafsir cahaya bulan yang masuk lewat kaca jendela bulat.",
      "en": "The belief has long been mocked. In his 1718 collection of superstitions I. G. Schmidt claimed to have caught an 'Erd-Hüngen' in a cellar that turned out to be a mere mouse, and Johann Andreas Schmeller in his Bayerisches Wörterbuch put it down to moonlight misread as it shone through round-paned windows.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "erdhenne-c05",
        "erdhenne-c06"
      ]
    }
  ],

      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Erdhenne biasanya tak terlihat dan hanya berkotek keras seperti memanggil anak ayam untuk memperingatkan penghuni akan bahaya bagi mereka atau rumah; beberapa legenda menyebut ia menjawab pertanyaan tentang bahaya itu bila ditanya langsung.",
        "en": "The Erdhenne mostly stays unseen, clucking loudly like a hen calling chicks to warn residents of dangers to them or the house; some legends say it answers questions about the danger when asked directly."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "erdhenne-c03",
      "source_id": "erdhenne-s1",
      "quote": "the spirit appears as an old, ash grey, shaggy hen with a short neck. By becoming visible, the Erdhenne foretells death within a year, usually that of the person it shows itself to. However, according to another legend, should it cluck and flutter its wings nine times, the head of the house would fall deadly ill.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh itu tampil sebagai ayam betina tua kelabu abu berbulu kusut berleher pendek; penampakannya meramalkan kematian dalam setahun, biasanya orang yang melihatnya; menurut legenda lain, bila ia berkotek dan mengepakkan sayap sembilan kali, kepala rumah tangga jatuh sakit parah.",
        "en": "The spirit appears as an old, ash-grey, shaggy short-necked hen; becoming visible, it foretells a death within a year, usually of the one who sees it; another legend says nine clucks and wing-flaps mean the head of the house will fall mortally ill."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "erdhenne-c04",
      "source_id": "erdhenne-s2",
      "quote": "Die Stimme dieses Geistwesens soll aus dem Erdboden oder einer dunklen Ecke der Stube dringen und wie das Glucksen, beziehungsweise Piepsen, einer Henne klingen, die Küklein führt, was ihm den Namen einbrachte. Die Erdhenne soll, wie die Unke oder die Hausotter ein gutartiger Schutzgeist sein",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Suara roh ini konon keluar dari tanah atau sudut gelap ruangan, terdengar seperti kotek induk ayam yang menuntun anak-anaknya, sehingga ia dinamai demikian; seperti Unke atau Hausotter (ular rumah), Erdhenne dianggap roh pelindung yang baik.",
        "en": "This spirit's voice is said to come from the ground or a dark corner of the room, sounding like a hen clucking to her chicks, which gave it its name; like the Unke or the Hausotter (house snake), the Erdhenne is held to be a benign guardian spirit."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "erdhenne-c05",
      "source_id": "erdhenne-s2",
      "quote": "Es hieß einst, dass derjenige, der die Erdhenne sehe, noch innerhalb desselben Jahres sterben müsste, was der Autor I. G. Schmidt in seiner 1718 erschienenen Aberglaubensammlung lächerlich machte. Dieses Recht nahm er sich, weil er einst in einem Keller ein angebliches Erd-Hüngen, eben jene Erdhenne, gefangen haben wollte, das sich aber als schlichte Maus herausstellte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dulu dikatakan orang yang melihat Erdhenne akan mati dalam tahun itu; penulis I. G. Schmidt mengolok-oloknya dalam kumpulan takhayul 1718, karena ia mengaku pernah menangkap \"Erd-Hüngen\" di gudang bawah tanah yang ternyata seekor tikus.",
        "en": "It was once said that whoever saw the Erdhenne would die within the year, which author I. G. Schmidt mocked in his 1718 collection of superstitions, having supposedly caught an 'Erd-Hüngen' in a cellar that proved to be a plain mouse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "erdhenne-c06",
      "source_id": "erdhenne-s1",
      "quote": "Johann Andreas Schmeller ridiculed the myths of the Erdhenne in his Bayerisches Wörterbuch, claiming that reports about it were created by misinterpreting moonlight shining through crown glass windows.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Johann Andreas Schmeller mengejek mitos Erdhenne dalam Bayerisches Wörterbuch dan menyatakan laporan tentangnya muncul dari salah tafsir cahaya bulan yang masuk lewat kaca jendela bulat.",
        "en": "Johann Andreas Schmeller mocked the Erdhenne myths in his Bayerisches Wörterbuch, saying reports of it arose from misreading moonlight shining through crown-glass windows."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [
    {
      "field": "sources",
      "searched": "Artikel an (Aragon) hanya meringkas artikel de, tanpa rujukan, dan menambahkan deskripsi wujud (tupai berparuh bebek) yang tidak terdapat di artikel lain; tidak dipakai."

    }
  ]
}

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Fasolt adalah raksasa atau kesatria dalam legenda heroik Jerman, dikenal dari Eckenlied dan Thidrekssaga, dan kelak menjadi tokoh raksasa dalam opera Das Rheingold karya Richard Wagner.",
    "en": "Fasolt is a giant or knight of German heroic legend, known from the Eckenlied and the Thidrekssaga, who later became a giant in Richard Wagner's opera Das Rheingold.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "fasolt-c01",
      "fasolt-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Fasolt (juga Fasold atau Vasolt) adalah raksasa atau kesatria yang muncul dalam puisi heroik bahasa Jerman Tinggi Pertengahan Eckenlied (sekitar 1230) dan dalam Thidrekssaga, kumpulan legenda Jerman berbahasa Nordik Kuno (sekitar 1250).",
      "en": "Known also as Fasold or Vasolt, Fasolt features as either a giant or a knight in the Eckenlied, a Middle High German heroic poem from around 1230, and in the Thidrekssaga, an Old Norse compendium of German legends from around 1250.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fasolt-c01",
        "fasolt-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Namanya kemungkinan berasal dari akar kata yang mirip dengan kata Jerman Tinggi Kuno faso, \"benang\", yang agaknya merujuk rambutnya yang panjang dan dikepang dalam Eckenlied. Ia mungkin semula setan badai: sebuah doa abad ke-17 kepada para penyihir di gunung Jochgrimm dekat Bozen memohon agar \"ffasolt\" menjauhkan badai.",
      "en": "His name probably comes from a root akin to Old High German faso, 'thread', most likely pointing to the long braided hair the Eckenlied gives him. He may first have been a storm demon: a 17th-century prayer to the witches on Mount Jochgrimm near Bozen asks 'ffasolt' to send storms far away.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "fasolt-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam Das Rheingold karya Wagner, Fasolt dan saudaranya Fafner adalah dua raksasa yang membangun benteng Walhall bagi para dewa dengan imbalan dewi Freia, yang ingin dinikahi Fasolt. Atas desakan Fafner, mereka menerima harta Nibelung milik kurcaci Alberich sebagai gantinya; kutukan Alberich pada cincin itu memicu pertengkaran, dan Fasolt dibunuh Fafner ketika menuntut cincin itu.",
      "en": "In Wagner's Das Rheingold, Fasolt and his brother Fafner are two giants who build the fortress Walhall for the gods in return for the goddess Freia, whom Fasolt wants to marry. At Fafner's urging they accept the dwarf Alberich's Nibelung hoard instead; Alberich's curse on the ring sparks a quarrel, and Fafner kills Fasolt when he claims the ring.",

      "source_id": "fasolt-s1",
      "quote": "Fasolt, Fasold or Vasolt is a giant or knight who appears in the following works: The Middle High German heroic poem Eckenlied (c. 1230). The Old Norse compendium of German legends known as the Thidrekssaga (c. 1250).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Fasolt (Fasold, Vasolt) adalah raksasa atau kesatria yang muncul dalam puisi heroik Jerman Tinggi Pertengahan (Middle High German) Eckenlied (sekitar 1230) dan kumpulan legenda Jerman berbahasa Nordik Kuno (Old Norse) Thidrekssaga (sekitar 1250).",

      "source_id": "fasolt-s1",
      "quote": "The name Fasolt likely derives from a root similar to Old High German faso, thread, and most likely refers to the long braided hair he is described as having in the Eckenlied. He may have originally been a storm demon, as evidenced by a 17th-century prayer to witches at the mountain Jochgrimm outside of Bozen to cause \"ffasolt\" to send storms far away.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Nama Fasolt kemungkinan dari akar yang mirip kata Jerman Tinggi Kuno (Old High German) faso, \"benang\", merujuk rambut panjangnya yang dikepang dalam Eckenlied; ia mungkin semula setan badai, dibuktikan doa abad ke-17 kepada penyihir di gunung Jochgrimm dekat Bozen agar \"ffasolt\" menjauhkan badai.",
        "en": "The name Fasolt likely stems from a root like Old High German faso, 'thread', referring to his long braided hair in the Eckenlied; he may first have been a storm demon, as a 17th-century prayer to witches on Mount Jochgrimm near Bozen asks 'ffasolt' to send storms away."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fasolt-c04",
      "source_id": "fasolt-s2",
      "quote": "Fasolt und sein Bruder Fafner sind zwei Riesen, die den Göttern die Burg Walhall errichten. Ursprünglich war zwischen Göttern und Riesen vereinbart, dass die Riesen als Dank für den Burgbau die Göttin Freia erhalten sollten, die Fasolt ehelichen wollte.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam Das Rheingold, Fasolt dan saudaranya Fafner adalah dua raksasa yang membangun benteng Walhall bagi para dewa; semula disepakati mereka akan menerima dewi Freia, yang ingin dinikahi Fasolt, sebagai upah.",
        "en": "In Das Rheingold, Fasolt and his brother Fafner are two giants who build Walhall for the gods; it was first agreed that they would receive the goddess Freia, whom Fasolt wished to marry, as payment."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fasolt-c05",
      "source_id": "fasolt-s3",
      "quote": "Il compendio antico norvegese delle leggende tedesche noto come Thidrekssaga (1250 circa)",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Italia juga mencatat Fasolt dalam Thidrekssaga, kumpulan legenda Jerman berbahasa Nordik Kuno (sekitar 1250).",
        "en": "The Italian article likewise lists Fasolt in the Thidrekssaga, an Old Norse compendium of German legends (c. 1250)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fasolt-c06",
      "source_id": "fasolt-s2",
      "quote": "Nachdem diese an die Riesen ausgehändigt wurden, kommt es zwischen Fasolt und Fafner zum Streit um den Ring – eine Folge des von Alberich ausgesprochenen Fluches.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Setelah harta dan cincin diserahkan kepada para raksasa, Fasolt dan Fafner bertengkar memperebutkan cincin itu akibat kutukan Alberich.",
        "en": "After the treasure and ring are handed to the giants, Fasolt and Fafner quarrel over the ring as a result of Alberich's curse."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fasolt-c07",
      "source_id": "fasolt-s2",
      "quote": "Als Fasolt gleichwohl wenigstens den Ring für sich begehrt, wird er von Fafner erschlagen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Ketika Fasolt tetap menginginkan setidaknya cincin itu, ia dibunuh Fafner.",
        "en": "When Fasolt still wants at least the ring for himself, Fafner strikes him dead."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "fasolt-c08",
      "source_id": "fasolt-s2",
      "quote": "Auf Betreiben Fafners erklären sich indes beide Riesen, also auch Fasolt, bereit, statt der Göttin auch den Nibelungenschatz des Zwergen Alberich als Vergütung zu akzeptieren",

      "statement": {
        "id": "Atas desakan Fafner, kedua raksasa bersedia menerima harta Nibelung milik kurcaci Alberich sebagai ganti sang dewi.",
        "en": "At Fafner's urging both giants agree to take the dwarf Alberich's Nibelung hoard instead of the goddess."
      }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Feldgeister (\"roh ladang\") atau Korndämonen (\"setan gandum\") adalah roh-roh pertanian dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang menghuni ladang gandum, berwujud hewan atau manusia.",
    "en": "Feldgeister ('field spirits') or Korndämonen ('corn demons') are agricultural spirits of German folklore who inhabit the grain fields in animal or human form.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "feldgeister-c01",
      "feldgeister-c02"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Feldgeister atau Korndämonen adalah roh gandum dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, dan sering juga roh angin yang mendatangkan kilat dan hujan. Istilah Korndämonen diperkenalkan Wilhelm Mannhardt sebagai sebutan kolektif bagi makhluk gaib di ladang gandum atau tanaman lain seperti rami, kacang, dan opium; menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, sebutan \"Korngeist\" sebenarnya lebih tepat.",
      "en": "Feldgeister or Korndämonen are corn spirits of German folklore, and often also wind spirits that bring lightning and rain. The term Korndämonen was coined by Wilhelm Mannhardt as a collective name for supernatural beings in grain fields or other crops such as flax, beans and poppies; the German article notes that 'Korngeist' would really be the better label.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "feldgeister-c01",
        "feldgeister-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Feldgeister dikenal dalam banyak bentuk, ada yang berwujud hewan seperti serigala gandum (Roggenwolf), anjing, kucing, babi, kambing, dan ayam jantan, ada pula yang berwujud manusia seperti Roggenmuhme, Hafermann, dan Kornkind. Banyak di antaranya ditakuti karena mencuri atau menyakiti anak-anak; kehadiran setan gandum terlihat pada gandum yang bergelombang, seperti dalam ungkapan \"Ibu Gandum ada di ladang\".",
      "en": "Feldgeister come in many shapes: animal forms such as the rye wolf (Roggenwolf), dogs, cats, pigs, goats and roosters, and human forms such as the Roggenmuhme, the Hafermann and the Kornkind. Many are feared for stealing or harming children; a corn demon's presence is seen in the waving of the grain, as in the saying 'the Corn Mother is in the field'.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "feldgeister-c02",
        "feldgeister-c03",
        "feldgeister-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Saat panen, roh-roh itu lari makin dalam ke ladang menghindari para penyabit dan akhirnya terperangkap di batang gandum terakhir. Ia lalu dibunuh dengan dipotong atau diirik, atau dibawa ke desa dengan upacara dalam wujud boneka gandum; bulir terakhir dan buah pohon sering ditinggalkan sebagai persembahan. Sentuhan langsung dengan roh ladang menyebabkan sakit.",
      "en": "At harvest the spirits flee deeper into the field away from the mowers and are finally trapped in the last stalks. The spirit is then killed by cutting or threshing, or carried ceremonially to the village as a corn doll; the last ears and tree fruits are often left as an offering. Direct contact with a field spirit brings illness.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "feldgeister-c01",
        "feldgeister-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Korndämonen dianggap berasal dari dewa-dewa tumbuhan bangsa petani yang dahulu dipuja untuk melindungi tanaman. Seiring waktu makna aslinya hilang dan mereka didemonisasi, menjadi sosok menakutkan yang mencegah anak-anak memasuki dan merusak ladang yang sedang matang. Konsep roh gandum dipopulerkan pada awal abad ke-20 oleh antropolog Inggris James Frazer.",
      "en": "Korndämonen are thought to stem from the vegetation gods of farming peoples, once worshipped to protect the crops. Over time their original meaning faded and they were demonised into bogeys that kept children from entering and trampling the ripening fields. The idea of the corn spirit was popularised in the early 20th century by the British anthropologist James Frazer.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "feldgeister-c08",
        "feldgeister-c09"
      ]
    }
  ],

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c01",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s1",
      "quote": "During harvest season Feldgeister flee deeper into the fields to escape the mowers. With the last cornstalks the corn spirit becomes trapped. Either it is killed by cutting the grain heads, threshing the corn or it is brought to the village in a ceremonial manner, shaped as a corn doll. Direct contact to the Feldgeist causes illness.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Feldgeister (Feldgeist, Korndämonen) adalah roh gandum dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang sering juga roh angin; saat panen mereka lari makin dalam ke ladang, terperangkap di batang terakhir, lalu dibunuh dengan dipotong atau diirik, atau dibawa ke desa dengan upacara sebagai boneka gandum; sentuhan langsung menyebabkan sakit.",
        "en": "Feldgeister (Feldgeist, Korndämonen) are corn spirits of German folklore, often also wind spirits; at harvest they flee deeper into the field, are trapped in the last stalks, then killed by cutting or threshing, or brought ceremonially to the village as a corn doll; direct contact causes illness."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c02",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s1",
      "quote": "Numerous Feldgeister are known in German folklore, some shaped as animals, some in human form. The last grain heads and tree fruits are often left at their place as a sacrifice for the agricultural spirits.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Banyak Feldgeister dikenal dalam cerita rakyat Jerman, sebagian berwujud hewan dan sebagian berwujud manusia; bulir terakhir dan buah pohon sering ditinggalkan sebagai persembahan.",
        "en": "Many Feldgeister are known in German folklore, some animal-shaped, some human; the last ears of grain and tree fruits are often left in place as an offering."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c03",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s1",
      "quote": "The Roggenwolf (\"rye wolf\"), Getreidewolf (\"grain wolf\") or Kornwolf (\"corn wolf\") is a field spirit shaped as a wolf. The Roggenwolf steals children and feeds on them.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roggenwolf (\"serigala gandum hitam\") adalah roh ladang berwujud serigala yang mencuri dan memakan anak-anak; contoh lain mencakup Roggenmuhme, Hafermann, dan Kornkind yang berwujud manusia.",
        "en": "The Roggenwolf ('rye wolf') is a wolf-shaped field spirit that steals and eats children; other examples include the human-shaped Roggenmuhme, Hafermann and Kornkind."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c04",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s1",
      "quote": "Feldgeister are often also wind spirits, causing lightning and rain.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Feldgeister sering juga roh angin yang mendatangkan kilat dan hujan.",
        "en": "Feldgeister are often wind spirits too, bringing lightning and rain."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c05",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s1",
      "quote": "The Bilwis rolls through the corn shaped as a destructive giant ball or appears as a whirlwind to steal grain during harvest season. Both can be repelled by throwing a knife with three crosses on its blade on the Bilwis",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bilwis menggelinding di ladang sebagai bola raksasa perusak atau muncul sebagai angin puyuh untuk mencuri gandum saat panen; ia dapat diusir dengan melempar pisau bertanda tiga salib.",
        "en": "The Bilwis rolls through the corn as a destructive giant ball or appears as a whirlwind to steal grain at harvest; it can be repelled by throwing a knife bearing three crosses."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c06",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s2",
      "quote": "Korndämonen ist ein von Wilhelm Mannhardt eingeführter Sammelbegriff für übernatürliche Wesen, die sich im Kornfeld oder anderen Pflanzungen wie Flachs, Bohnen, Mohn usw. aufhalten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korndämonen adalah istilah kolektif yang diperkenalkan Wilhelm Mannhardt untuk makhluk gaib di ladang gandum atau tanaman lain seperti rami, kacang, dan opium; menurut artikel bahasa Jerman, sebutan Korngeist sebenarnya lebih tepat.",
        "en": "Korndämonen is a collective term introduced by Wilhelm Mannhardt for supernatural beings in grain fields or other crops like flax, beans and poppy; the German article says Korngeist would be more apt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c07",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s2",
      "quote": "Die Anwesenheit eines Korndämons wird im Wogen des Korns gesehen und findet sich auch in Redensarten wie „Die Kornmutter ist im Feld“ wieder.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Kehadiran setan gandum terlihat pada gandum yang bergelombang dan tercermin dalam ungkapan seperti \"Ibu Gandum (Kornmutter) ada di ladang\" (Corn Mother).",
        "en": "A corn demon's presence is seen in the waving of the grain and appears in sayings like 'the Corn Mother (Kornmutter) is in the field'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c08",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s2",
      "quote": "Ihren Ursprung haben Korndämonen in früheren Vegetationsgöttern agrarisch orientierter Völker. Diese Götter wurden angebetet, damit sie die Feldfrüchte schützen und fördern. Im Lauf der Zeit verschwand jedoch die ursprüngliche Bedeutung dieser Götter und sie wurden dämonisiert. Sie dienten nun als Schreckgestalt dazu, Kinder vom Betreten und damit Zerstören der reifenden Felder abzuhalten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Korndämonen berasal dari dewa tumbuhan bangsa petani yang dipuja agar melindungi tanaman; seiring waktu makna asli itu hilang, mereka didemonisasi, dan menjadi sosok menakutkan yang mencegah anak-anak memasuki dan merusak ladang yang matang.",
        "en": "Korndämonen originate in the vegetation gods of farming peoples, worshipped to protect crops; over time that meaning faded, they were demonised and became bogeys keeping children out of ripening fields."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c09",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s3",
      "quote": "Polní duch je druh mytické bytosti, která přebývá v obilném poli a střeží ho. Termín a koncept obilného ducha se poprvé objevil v díle německého folkloristy Wilhelma Mannhardta v 60. letech 19. století, dále byl pak popularizován britským antropologem Jamesem Frazerem na počátku 20. století.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Roh ladang (polní duch) adalah makhluk mitis yang menghuni dan menjaga ladang gandum; istilah dan konsepnya muncul dalam karya folklorist Jerman Wilhelm Mannhardt pada 1860-an dan dipopulerkan antropolog Inggris (British) James Frazer pada awal abad ke-20.",
        "en": "The field spirit (polní duch) is a mythical being that lives in and guards the grain field; the term and idea first appeared in the 1860s work of German folklorist Wilhelm Mannhardt and were popularised by British anthropologist James Frazer in the early 20th century."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "feldgeister-c10",
      "source_id": "feldgeister-s3",
      "quote": "Národopisná badatelka Drahomíra Stránská uznala Mannhardtův přínos, ale domnívala se, že dané zvyky mají původ spíše v praktických aspektech zemědělského života než v náboženství.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Etnografer Drahomíra Stránská mengakui sumbangan Mannhardt tetapi berpendapat kebiasaan tersebut berakar pada aspek praktis kehidupan pertanian, bukan agama.",
        "en": "Ethnographer Drahomíra Stránská acknowledged Mannhardt's contribution but held that the customs arose from practical sides of farm life rather than from religion."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Gravso (\"babi betina kubur\"), juga disebut gloso atau glumso, adalah hantu berwujud babi betina dalam kepercayaan rakyat Denmark dan Skåne yang muncul di pemakaman sebagai pertanda maut.",
    "en": "The gravso ('grave sow'), also called gloso or glumso, is a ghostly sow of folk belief in Denmark and Scania that haunts churchyards as an omen of death.",

      "gravso-c01",
      "gravso-c03",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Gravso, gloso, atau glumso adalah babi gaib dalam kepercayaan rakyat Denmark; bila berperan sebagai penjaga gereja ia disebut kirkeso. Ia muncul di atau dekat pemakaman, terutama di Denmark dan Skåne, tetapi juga sering berkeliaran di tempat lain. Di Swedia selatan ia dikenal sebagai gloson atau gluffsuggan.",
      "en": "The gravso, gloso or glumso is a supernatural pig of Danish folk belief; when it acts as a church guardian it is called the kirkeso. It appears at or near churchyards, chiefly in Denmark and Scania, though it often roams elsewhere too. In southern Sweden it is known as the gloson or gluffsuggan.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gravso-c01",
        "gravso-c02",
        "gravso-c06",
        "gravso-c07"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Wujudnya digambarkan beragam: hitam, merah, atau tanpa kepala, kadang ditemani tujuh atau dua belas anak babi. Matanya merah atau menyala, punggungnya setajam pisau dengan bulu kaku yang panjang; di Swedia selatan dikatakan ia suka berlari di antara kaki orang untuk merobek mereka.",
      "en": "Its looks vary: black, red or headless, sometimes followed by a litter of seven or twelve piglets. Its eyes are red or glowing and its back is knife-sharp with long bristles; in southern Sweden it was said to like running between people's legs to slit them open.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "gravso-c02",
        "gravso-c07",
        "gravso-c08",
        "gravso-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Catatan tertua berasal dari Kopenhagen tahun 1587, ketika pendeta Hans Lauritzen menulis tentang hantu pertanda maut yang disebut \"babi kelabu Santo Clemens\". Menurut sebagian tradisi, gravso sebenarnya arwah anak yang lahir dan dibunuh secara diam-diam; pendeta Joachim Junge mencatat kepercayaan ini di Sjælland Utara dan menambahkan bahwa babi itu sangat berbahaya bagi orang yang membawa dompet dari kandung kemih babi.",
      "en": "The oldest record comes from Copenhagen in 1587, when the minister Hans Lauritzen wrote of a death-omen ghost known as 'Saint Clemens' grey sow'.       "title": "Gloson",

    }
  ],
  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "gravso-c01",
      "source_id": "gravso-s1",
      "quote": "En gloso er et overnaturligt svin, som findes i dansk folketro. Den kan have andre betegnelser, og fx også kaldes glumso, gravso eller, når den optræder som kirkevare, kirkeso.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gloso adalah babi gaib dalam kepercayaan rakyat Denmark (dansk); ia juga disebut glumso, gravso, atau kirkeso bila berperan sebagai penjaga gereja.",
        "en": "The gloso is a supernatural pig of Danish folk belief, also called glumso, gravso or, when acting as church guardian, kirkeso."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c02",
      "source_id": "gravso-s1",
      "quote": "Glosoen kan findes ved eller på kirkegården, men har også ofte sin gang andre steder. Dens udseende beskrives forskelligt. Den kan bl.a. være sort, rød eller hovedløs og den kan være ifølge med et kuld på fx syv eller tolv smågrise.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gloso dapat ditemukan di atau dekat pemakaman, tetapi sering juga berkeliaran di tempat lain; wujudnya beragam, misalnya hitam, merah, atau tanpa kepala, dan bisa ditemani tujuh atau dua belas anak babi.",
        "en": "The gloso is found at or in the churchyard but often roams elsewhere; its appearance varies, being black, red or headless, and it may be followed by a litter of seven or twelve piglets."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c03",
      "source_id": "gravso-s1",
      "quote": "Den ældste omtale af en sådan so stammer fra 1587, hvor den optræder som dødsvarsel. Præsten Hans Lauritzen beretter, at der er \"et genfærd, som lader sig tilsyne imod folk skal dø, som sankt Clemens' grå so, der folk taler om her i København.\"",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Catatan tertua berasal dari 1587 sebagai pertanda maut: pendeta Hans Lauritzen menulis tentang hantu yang menampakkan diri menjelang orang mati, seperti babi kelabu Santo Clemens (sankt Clemens) yang dibicarakan orang di Kopenhagen (København).",
        "en": "The earliest mention dates from 1587 as a death omen: minister Hans Lauritzen reports a ghost that shows itself when people are about to die, like Saint Clemens' (sankt Clemens) grey sow talked of in Copenhagen (København)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c04",
      "source_id": "gravso-s1",
      "quote": "Nogle overleveringer mener, at glosoen i virkeligheden er genfærdet af et barn, der er født og dræbt i dølgsmål. Præsten Joachim Junge refererer denne tro fra Nordsjælland, og tilføjer, at glosøer er særligt farlige for personer, der bærer en pung, fremstillet af en svineblære.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebagian tradisi menganggap gloso arwah anak yang lahir dan dibunuh diam-diam; pendeta Joachim Junge mencatat kepercayaan ini dari Sjælland Utara (Nordsjælland) dan menambahkan gloso sangat berbahaya bagi pembawa dompet dari kandung kemih babi.",
        "en": "Some traditions hold the gloso to be the ghost of a child born and killed in secret; minister Joachim Junge records this belief from North Zealand (Nordsjælland), adding that glosos are especially dangerous to those carrying a purse made of a pig's bladder."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c05",
      "source_id": "gravso-s2",
      "quote": "Presten Joachim Junge, som sent på 1700-tallet registrerte den nordsjællandske almuens overtro, skrev at dyret i virkeligheten representerte sjelene til drepte barn",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pendeta Joachim Junge, yang pada akhir abad ke-18 mencatat takhayul rakyat Sjælland Utara, menulis bahwa hewan itu sebenarnya mewakili jiwa anak-anak yang dibunuh.",
        "en": "Minister Joachim Junge, recording North Zealand peasant superstition in the late 1700s, wrote that the animal really represented the souls of murdered children."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c06",
      "source_id": "gravso-s2",
      "quote": "Gravso, også kalt glumso og gloso, er ifølge folketroen et spøkelsesdyr i form av en sugge (dansk: so) som blant annet opptrådte på kirkegårder i Danmark og Skåne.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gravso, juga disebut glumso dan gloso, menurut kepercayaan rakyat adalah hewan hantu berwujud babi betina yang antara lain muncul di pemakaman di Denmark (Danmark) dan Skåne.",
        "en": "The gravso, also called glumso and gloso, is in folk belief a ghost animal in the form of a sow that appeared in churchyards in Denmark (Danmark) and Scania (Skåne), among other places."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c07",
      "source_id": "gravso-s3",
      "quote": "Gloson även kallad Gravson eller Gluffsuggan är ett sydsvenskt folktroväsen i form av en gris eller ett vildsvin. \"Gloson\" (\"gloso\" i obestämd form) kommer av glo, att stirra eller att glöda, och so, en sugga.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gloson, juga disebut Gravson atau Gluffsuggan, adalah makhluk kepercayaan rakyat Swedia selatan (sydsvenskt; Swedia) berwujud babi atau babi hutan; namanya dari glo, \"menatap\" atau \"membara\", dan so, \"babi betina\".",
        "en": "Gloson, also called Gravson or Gluffsuggan, is a southern Swedish (sydsvenskt; Sweden) folk being shaped like a pig or wild boar; the name comes from glo, 'to stare' or 'to glow', and so, 'sow'."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c08",
      "source_id": "gravso-s3",
      "quote": "Gloson sades även ha en vass sågtandad rygg och ville gärna springa mellan benen på folk och således sprätta upp dem.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gloson konon berpunggung tajam bergerigi dan suka berlari di antara kaki orang sehingga merobek mereka.",
        "en": "The gloson was said to have a sharp, saw-toothed back and liked to run between people's legs, ripping them open."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "gravso-c09",
      "source_id": "gravso-s2",
      "quote": "Gravsoen kjentes på sine gloende øyne og en knivskarp rygg med strittende børster. Det varslet død å møte henne.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Gravso dikenali dari matanya yang menyala dan punggungnya yang setajam pisau dengan bulu kaku; bertemu dengannya pertanda maut.",
        "en": "The gravso was known by her glowing eyes and knife-sharp back with bristling hairs; meeting her foretold death."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [],
  "gaps": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Hobangoas atau Habergeiß adalah sosok setan dalam kepercayaan rakyat Bayern dan Austria, berwujud kambing berkuku kuda atau burung cacat, yang kini tampil dalam arak-arakan Perchten dan Krampus.",
    "en": "The Hobangoas or Habergeiß is a demonic figure of folk belief in Bavaria and Austria, shaped like a goat with horse's hooves or a deformed bird, who now appears in Perchten and Krampus processions.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "hobangoas-c01",
      "hobangoas-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Habergeiß (dalam dialek Bayern Habergoaß atau Hobangoas) adalah sosok setan berwujud kambing berkuku kuda, atau burung yang bersuara kambing atau cacat dengan cara lain. Ia digambarkan sebagai makhluk chthonic, setan tumbuhan, atau roh unsur, campuran kambing dan burung, dan dahulu berperan penting dalam kepercayaan rakyat.",
      "en": "The Habergeiß (Bavarian Habergoaß or Hobangoas) is a demonic figure shaped like a goat with horse's hooves, or a bird with a goat's voice or some other deformity. It is described as a chthonic beast, vegetation demon or elemental spirit, a goat-bird hybrid, and once played an important role in folk belief.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hobangoas-c01",
        "hobangoas-c05"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Dalam legenda rakyat ia biasanya kambing jantan berkaki tiga dengan mata menyala dan janggut panjang, kadang berbulu burung alih-alih rambut, dan melihatnya dianggap pertanda buruk. Dalam wujud burung ia digambarkan jelek dan berkaki tiga, berbulu merah darah atau kuning menurut daerah, dengan embikan yang menakutkan, dan konon mengisap darah petani dan ternak.",
      "en": "In folk legend it is usually a three-legged billy goat with glowing eyes and a long beard, sometimes feathered instead of furred, and seeing it is a bad omen. In bird form it is ugly and three-legged, blood-red or yellow depending on the region, with a frightening bleat, and is said to suck the blood of farmers and livestock.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "hobangoas-c02",
        "hobangoas-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Sosok ini masih hidup dalam adat Austria, terutama di Kärnten, Salzburg, dan Steiermark: dalam arak-arakan Krampus pada 5–6 Desember ia mengiringi para Perchten sambil memanggul keranjang \"Zistl\", dan anak-anak diberi tahu bahwa ia membawa pergi anak nakal di dalamnya. Ia juga muncul dalam karnaval dan adat tiang Mei; menurut legenda lama, ia hewan peliharaan para Perchten dan jauh lebih kuat daripada mereka.",
      "en": "The figure lives on in custom in Austria, especially in Carinthia, Salzburg and Styria: in the Krampus runs of 5–6 December it accompanies the Perchten carrying a 'Zistl' basket, and children are told it carries off naughty children in it. It also appears at carnival and in maypole customs; an old legend makes it the pet of the Perchten, far stronger than they are.",

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c01",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s1",
      "quote": "Die Habergeiß (bair. Habergoaß) ist eine Dämonengestalt in Form einer Ziege mit Pferdehufen oder eines Vogels, der entweder die Stimme einer Ziege hat oder in einer anderen Weise verunstaltet ist.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Habergeiß (Bayern: Habergoaß) adalah sosok setan berwujud kambing berkuku kuda atau burung yang bersuara kambing atau cacat dengan cara lain.",
        "en": "The Habergeiß (Bavarian dialect of Bavaria: Habergoaß) is a demon figure shaped like a goat with horse's hooves or a bird that has a goat's voice or is otherwise deformed."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c02",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s1",
      "quote": "In der Volkssage wird die Habergeiß meist als dreibeiniger Geißbock mit glühenden Augen und langem Bart beschrieben. In einigen Sagen besitzt dieser Geißbock kein Fell, sondern ein Gefieder. Die Habergeiß zu sehen gilt als böses Omen. Laut einer alten Sage ist die Habergeiß das Haustier der Perchten und übertrifft sie in der Stärke überragend.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda rakyat Habergeiß biasanya kambing jantan berkaki tiga bermata menyala dan berjanggut panjang, kadang berbulu burung; melihatnya pertanda buruk; menurut legenda lama ia hewan peliharaan para Perchten dan jauh lebih kuat dari mereka.",
        "en": "In folk legend the Habergeiß is mostly a three-legged billy goat with glowing eyes and a long beard, sometimes feathered; seeing it is an evil omen; an old legend makes it the Perchten's pet, far surpassing them in strength."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c03",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s1",
      "quote": "Bei den Krampusläufen Anfang Dezember (vor allem 5./6. Dezember, Nikolaus) ist die sogenannte Habergeiß unterwegs, die Begleitung der Perchten ist. Sie trägt dabei immer eine „Zistl“ auf dem Rücken, einen Tragekorb, wie er früher bei Bauern üblich war. Den Kindern wird gerne erzählt, die Habergeiß nähme in dieser Zistl die Kinder mit sich fort.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam arak-arakan Krampus awal Desember (5–6 Desember, Nikolaus), Habergeiß mengiringi para Perchten sambil memanggul keranjang \"Zistl\"; anak-anak diberi tahu ia membawa pergi anak-anak di dalamnya; bentuk ini terutama di Kärnten, Salzburg, dan Steiermark (Austria).",
        "en": "At the early-December Krampus runs (5–6 December, Nikolaus) the Habergeiß accompanies the Perchten carrying a 'Zistl' basket; children are told it takes children away in it; this form is found mainly in Carinthia (Kärnten), Salzburg and Styria (Steiermark), Austria."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c04",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s1",
      "quote": "Hierbei wird die Habergeiß als hässlicher, dämonischer Vogel, manchmal mit drei Beinen, beschrieben. Das Fell (manchmal auch Gefieder) ist je nach Gegend rot wie Blut oder gelb. Das Meckern klingt immer erschreckend und furchteinflößend. Es wird ihr nachgesagt, dass sie Bauern und Vieh das Blut aus den Adern saugt.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Habergeiß juga digambarkan sebagai burung setan yang jelek, kadang berkaki tiga, berbulu merah darah atau kuning menurut daerah, dengan embikan menakutkan, dan konon mengisap darah petani dan ternak.",
        "en": "The Habergeiß is also described as an ugly demonic bird, sometimes three-legged, blood-red or yellow by region, with a frightening bleat, said to suck the blood from the veins of farmers and livestock."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c05",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s2",
      "quote": "De Hobangoas oda Howangoas, aa Habergoaß, is a chtonisches Viach, Vegetationsdämon oda Elementargeist in da Gstoit vo oana Goas, de wo imma auf irgendaa Oart vaunstoidt is (Mischwesn vo Goas und Vogl).",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Hobangoas atau Howangoas, juga Habergoaß, adalah makhluk chthonic, setan tumbuhan, atau roh unsur berwujud kambing yang selalu cacat dengan suatu cara (campuran kambing dan burung).",
        "en": "The Hobangoas or Howangoas, also Habergoaß, is a chthonic beast, vegetation demon or elemental spirit in goat form, always deformed in some way (a goat-bird hybrid)."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c06",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s1",
      "quote": "Die Habergeiß ist fest verankert im Brauchtum und wird in Teilen Österreichs auch bei unterschiedlichen lokalen Bräuchen eingesetzt, vor allem im Fasching, im Maibaum-Brauchtum und bei den Perchtenläufen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "modern-popular-culture",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Habergeiß berakar kuat dalam adat dan dipakai dalam berbagai adat lokal di sebagian Austria (Österreichs), terutama karnaval, adat tiang Mei, dan arak-arakan Perchten.",
        "en": "The Habergeiß is firmly rooted in custom and used in various local customs in parts of Austria (Österreichs), above all carnival, maypole customs and Perchten runs."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c07",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s1",
      "quote": "Das Kompositionsglied Haber bezeichnet ‚Ziegenbock‘ (vgl. mundartlich Häberling ‚einjähriger Ziegenbock‘), Geiß ‚Ziege, Zicke‘. Dieser Name sowie gleichbedeutende Bockgeiß sind tautologische Komposita, und die oft gehörte Darstellung, nach der gleichlautende Haber ‚Hafer‘ bedeute, lässt sich etymologisch nicht halten.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Unsur Haber berarti \"kambing jantan\" dan Geiß \"kambing betina\"; nama ini kata majemuk tautologis, dan anggapan umum bahwa Haber berarti \"haver/oat\" (Hafer) tidak dapat dipertahankan secara etimologis; dalam bahasa Islandia Kuno hafr berarti kambing jantan.",
        "en": "The element Haber means 'billy goat' and Geiß 'goat'; the name is a tautological compound, and the common view that Haber means 'oat' (Hafer) cannot be upheld etymologically; in Old Icelandic hafr means buck."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c08",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s1",
      "quote": "Mythologisch findet sie in Skandinavien ihre Entsprechung im Julbock, in Rumänien in der Capra.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Secara mitologis padanannya di Skandinavia adalah Julbock, dan di Rumania Capra.",
        "en": "Mythologically its counterpart in Scandinavia is the Julbock, and in Romania the Capra."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c09",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s4",
      "quote": "The female equivalent of the Haferbock is the Habergeiß (\"oat goat\"), also known as Korngeiß (\"corn goat\"), Weizengeiß (\"wheat goat\") Roggengeiß (\"rye goat\"), Hobagoaß (\"oat goat\") or Heugeiß (\"hay goat\").",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel Feldgeister menyebut Habergeiß (\"kambing oat\") padanan betina Haferbock, juga disebut Korngeiß, Roggengeiß, atau Hobagoaß.",
        "en": "The Feldgeister article makes the Habergeiß ('oat goat') the female equivalent of the Haferbock, also called Korngeiß, Roggengeiß or Hobagoaß."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "hobangoas-c10",
      "source_id": "hobangoas-s3",
      "quote": "Volgens een oude legendes is de Habergeiß het huisdier van de Perchten en is hij hen in kracht ver te boven.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda lama, Habergeiß adalah hewan peliharaan para Perchten dan jauh melebihi mereka dalam kekuatan.",
        "en": "According to an old legend the Habergeiß is the Perchten's pet and far exceeds them in strength."
      }
    }
  ],
  "conflicts": [
    {
      "topic": {
        "id": "Arti nama",
        "en": "Meaning of the name"
      },
      "positions": [
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Haber berarti kambing jantan; tafsiran \"oat\" keliru.",
            "en": "Haber means billy goat; the 'oat' reading is wrong."
          },
          "claim_ids": [

          ]
        },
        {
          "summary": {
            "id": "Nama dimaknai \"kambing oat\" (oat goat) sebagai roh ladang gandum.",
            "en": "The name is glossed as 'oat goat', a corn-field spirit."
          },
          "claim_ids": [
            "hobangoas-c09"
          ]
        }
      ]
    }
  ],
  "gaps": [

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Klagmuhme (\"bibi peratap\") adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Jerman yang meratap dan merintih sebagai pertanda kematian, padanan banshee di Jerman.",
    "en": "The Klagmuhme ('wailing aunt') is a female sprite of German folklore whose wailing and whining foretell death, the German counterpart of the banshee.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "klagmuhme-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Klagmuhme atau Klagemuhme (\"bibi peratap\"), juga disebut Klagmutter (\"ibu peratap\"), adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Jerman. Dengan ratapan dan rintihannya ia mengabarkan kematian yang akan datang, sehingga dianggap padanan banshee di Jerman. Ia pertama tercatat pada abad ke-15 dalam bahasa Jerman Tinggi Pertengahan sebagai klagmuoter, sebutan untuk burung hantu, dan sering disamakan dengan burung itu.",
      "en": "The Klagmuhme or Klagemuhme ('wailing aunt'), also called Klagmutter ('wailing mother'), is a female sprite of German folklore. Through her wailing and whimpering she announces a coming death, making her the German counterpart of the banshee. She is first recorded in 15th-century Middle High German as klagmuoter, a name for the owl, and is often identified with that bird.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klagmuhme-c01",
        "klagmuhme-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ia sering tampil sebagai hewan: anjing hitam berbulu panjang yang melolong mengitari rumah, angsa putih, merpati di talang, kucing kelabu besar berkerudung, atau domba putih berkaki tiga yang membesar menjadi raksasa bila diganggu. Dalam wujud manusia ia perempuan tua bergaun hitam berkerudung putih, perempuan kecil berwajah tertutup sarang laba-laba, atau sosok setinggi menara gereja berbalut kain kafan; ia juga bisa berupa gumpalan benang biru yang menyemburkan bunga api.",
      "en": "She often takes animal form: a long-haired black dog howling round the house, a white goose, a dove in the gutter, a large grey cat in a headscarf, or a white three-legged sheep that swells to giant size if teased. In human form she is an old woman in a black dress and white scarf, a little woman whose face is veiled in cobwebs, or a figure as tall as a church steeple in a burial robe; she may also be a tangled blue clew spitting sparks.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klagmuhme-c03",
        "klagmuhme-c04"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Ratapannya pertanda maut dan bencana. Bila ia meratap di depan rumah orang sakit, pakaian si sakit dilempar keluar: jika Klagmuhme membawanya pergi, orang itu akan mati; jika ditinggalkannya, ia akan sembuh. Biasanya ia tak terlihat, melayang di atas rumah tanpa pernah masuk, aktif tengah malam, dan rintihannya (\"u-u-u!\") dapat membuat orang yang ketakutan jatuh sakit saraf, tetapi selain itu ia tidak mencelakai siapa pun.",
      "en": "Her wailing heralds death and disaster. If she wails before a house with a sick person inside, the patient's clothes are thrown out of the door: if the Klagmuhme carries them off, the patient will die; if she leaves them, he will recover. She is usually invisible, hovering over houses without ever going in, active at midnight, and her whimper ('u-u-u!') can give frightened people a nervous illness, though otherwise she harms no one.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klagmuhme-c05",
        "klagmuhme-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Asal-usulnya berbeda menurut daerah: di Austria ia arwah orang mati; di Sachsen arwah ibu malang yang mencari putranya yang tenggelam; di Kärnten dan Swiss ia bagian dari Perburuan Liar; di pegunungan Fichtel ia roh hutan perempuan (Waldweibchen).",
      "en": "Her origin differs by region: in Austria she is the soul of a dead person; in Saxony the soul of an unhappy mother searching for her drowned son; in Carinthia and Switzerland she belongs to the Wild Hunt; in the Fichtel Mountains she is a female wood sprite (Waldweibchen).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klagmuhme-c08",
        "klagmuhme-c09"
      ]
    }

  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c01",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "is a female sprite from German folklore also known as Klagmutter or Klagemutter (both: wailing mother; German: [ˈklaːɡˌmʊtɐ, ˈklaːɡəˌmʊtɐ] ). She heralds imminent death through wailing and whining and is thus the German equivalent of the banshee.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Klagmuhme atau Klagemuhme (\"bibi peratap\"), juga disebut Klagmutter atau Klagemutter (\"ibu peratap\"), adalah roh perempuan dari cerita rakyat Jerman yang mengabarkan kematian dengan ratapan dan rintihan, sehingga menjadi padanan banshee di Jerman.",
        "en": "The Klagmuhme or Klagemuhme ('wailing aunt'), also called Klagmutter or Klagemutter ('wailing mother'), is a female sprite of German folklore who heralds imminent death by wailing and whining, the German equivalent of the banshee."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c02",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "Further terms are Klagmütterchen (wailing mother), Winselmutter (whining mother), Haulemutter (wailing mother), Klinselweib (wailing woman), and Klagweh (wailing). The Klagmuhme is first attested in 15th century Middle High German as klagmuoter (wailing mother), denoting an owl.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Sebutan lain termasuk Winselmutter (\"ibu perintih\") dan Haulemutter; Klagmuhme pertama tercatat pada abad ke-15 dalam bahasa Jerman Tinggi Pertengahan (Middle High German) sebagai klagmuoter, yang menunjuk burung hantu.",
        "en": "Other names include Winselmutter ('whining mother') and Haulemutter; the Klagmuhme is first attested in 15th-century Middle High German as klagmuoter, meaning an owl."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c03",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "The Klagmuhme often appears as an animal. So she roves howling around the concerned house in the shape of a longhaired black dog, or she sits whiningly in the corner as a white goose or in the eaves gutter as a dove.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Klagmuhme sering tampil sebagai hewan: anjing hitam berbulu panjang yang melolong mengitari rumah bersangkutan, angsa putih yang merintih di sudut, atau merpati di talang.",
        "en": "The Klagmuhme often takes animal form: a long-haired black dog howling round the house concerned, a white goose whimpering in the corner, or a dove in the eaves gutter."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c04",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "The Klagmuhme's human appearance is that of an old woman in a black dress with a white scarf. Otherwise, she is described as a small woman with a face covered with cobwebs who is wearing a little three-cornered hat.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam wujud manusia Klagmuhme adalah perempuan tua bergaun hitam berkerudung putih, atau perempuan kecil berwajah tertutup sarang laba-laba bertopi tiga sudut.",
        "en": "In human form the Klagmuhme is an old woman in a black dress and white scarf, or a small woman with a cobweb-covered face in a little three-cornered hat."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c05",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "If the Klagmuhme wails in front of a house where an ill person is inside, clothing belonging to the diseased is thrown outside the door. If the Klagmuhme carries the clothing away, the diseased will undoubtedly die; if she leaves it behind, the diseased will recover. The prophesied disaster can be averted by immediately telling the Klagmuhme an alternative.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Bila Klagmuhme meratap di depan rumah orang sakit, pakaian si sakit dilempar keluar; jika ia membawanya pergi, orang itu pasti mati, jika ditinggalkan ia sembuh; bencana yang diramalkan dapat dicegah dengan segera menyebutkan alternatif kepadanya.",
        "en": "If the Klagmuhme wails before a house with a sick person, the patient's clothes are thrown outside; if she carries them away he will surely die, if she leaves them he will recover; the foretold disaster can be averted by at once telling her an alternative."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c06",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "She is usually invisible when she gets close to the houses without ever entering them and floats over them. Her whining (which sounds like \"u-u-u!\") can cause frightened people to fall ill with a nervous disease. Otherwise, she does nobody harm.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Biasanya ia tak terlihat saat mendekati rumah, tanpa pernah masuk, dan melayang di atasnya; rintihannya (\"u-u-u!\") dapat membuat orang yang ketakutan sakit saraf, tetapi selain itu ia tidak mencelakai siapa pun; ia biasanya aktif tengah malam.",
        "en": "She is usually invisible as she nears houses, never entering, floating above them; her whine ('u-u-u!') can give frightened people a nervous illness, but otherwise she harms nobody; she is usually active at midnight."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c07",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s2",
      "quote": "es un duende femenino del folklore alemán, también conocido como Klagmutter o Klagemutter (ambas: madre que llora). Ella anuncia una muerte inminente a través de lamentos y lloriqueos",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Artikel bahasa Spanyol juga menyebut Klagmuhme roh perempuan cerita rakyat Jerman (alemán), dikenal pula sebagai Klagmutter, yang mengabarkan kematian dengan ratapan.",
        "en": "The Spanish article likewise calls the Klagmuhme a female sprite of German (alemán) folklore, also known as Klagmutter, who announces death by wailing."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c08",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "Regarding its origin, the Klage in Austria is the soul of a deceased. In Saxony, she is the soul of an unlucky mother looking for her drowned son. In the Allgäu, there is a midnight procession of Klagefrauen (wailing women) or of ghostly men carrying a coffin. In Carinthia and Switzerland, the Klagmuhme is part of the wild hunt",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Asal-usulnya: di Austria (Austria) Klage adalah arwah orang mati; di Sachsen (Saxony) arwah ibu malang yang mencari putranya yang tenggelam; di Allgäu ada arak-arakan tengah malam Klagefrauen; di Kärnten (Carinthia) dan Swiss (Switzerland) ia bagian dari Perburuan Liar (Wild Hunt).",
        "en": "As to origin, in Austria the Klage is a dead person's soul; in Saxony an unlucky mother's soul seeking her drowned son; in the Allgäu there is a midnight procession of Klagefrauen; in Carinthia and Switzerland she is part of the Wild Hunt."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klagmuhme-c09",
      "source_id": "klagmuhme-s1",
      "quote": "In the Fichtel Mountains, the Klagmütterlein is a female wood sprite, a Waldweibchen (forest woman). In the Harz, mythologists wrongly identified the Haulemutter with the similar-sounding Frau Holle.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Di pegunungan Fichtel (Fichtel Mountains), Klagmütterlein adalah roh hutan perempuan (Waldweibchen); di Harz para mitolog keliru menyamakan Haulemutter dengan Frau Holle yang namanya mirip.",
        "en": "In the Fichtel Mountains the Klagmütterlein is a female wood sprite (Waldweibchen); in the Harz mythologists wrongly equated the Haulemutter with the similar-sounding Frau Holle."
      }
    }
  ],

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Klushund adalah anjing setan dalam legenda Vorarlberg, Austria: anjing hitam raksasa bermata menyala sebesar piring yang menghantui hutan di Lembah Rhein dan membawa penyakit serta kemalangan.",
    "en": "The Klushund is a demonic dog of Vorarlberg legend in Austria: a giant black hound with glowing, plate-sized eyes that haunts the forests of the Rhine Valley and brings illness and misfortune.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "klushund-c01"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Klushund (bahasa Jerman Alemannik untuk \"anjing Klaus\") adalah anjing setan mitologis dari Vorarlberg, Austria. Ia digambarkan sebagai anjing hitam raksasa dengan mata menyala sebesar piring yang berkeliaran di hutan gelap sekitar Lembah Rhein; bertemu dengannya membawa penyakit, penderitaan, atau wabah.",
      "en": "Known in Alemannic German as the 'dog of Klaus', the Klushund is a demonic hound from the folklore of Vorarlberg, Austria. People picture it as an enormous black dog whose eyes glow as big as plates, prowling the dark woods around the Rhine Valley; meeting it brings sickness, suffering or plague.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klushund-c01",
        "klushund-c09"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda yang paling umum, Klushund dahulu seorang pengkhianat yang dalam Perang Tiga Puluh Tahun menunjukkan kepada tentara Swedia di bawah Jenderal Carl Gustaf Wrangel jalan rahasia melewati pos benteng Klause di Lochau, sehingga Bregenz jatuh. Wrangel menjanjikan kepadanya permainan boling emas dari Kastel Hohenbregenz; sebagai hukuman, sang pengkhianat diubah menjadi anjing hitam besar yang harus berlari selamanya di bekas jalan Romawi di kaki gunung dari utara ke selatan.",
      "en": "In the most common legend, the Klushund was once a traitor who, during the Thirty Years' War, showed the Swedish army under General Carl Gustaf Wrangel a secret way past the fortified Klause post at Lochau, so that Bregenz fell. Wrangel promised him the golden skittles set of Hohenbregenz Castle; as punishment the traitor was turned into a great black dog that must run forever along the old Roman road at the mountain's edge from north to south.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klushund-c02",
        "klushund-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Legenda lain berkisar pada penampakannya di Kluser Wald, hutan antara Götzis dan Klaus yang dahulu gelap dan ditakuti, yang juga dianggap asal nama makhluk ini. Para sejarawan mencatat bahwa tak ada dokumen yang mendukung adanya pengkhianat; kisah itu diduga dibuat untuk menutupi kegagalan para pemimpin militer dan politik dalam kekalahan melawan Swedia pada 4 Januari 1647.",
      "en": "Other legends centre on sightings in the Kluser Wald, a once dark and feared forest between Götzis and Klaus, which is also thought to be the source of the creature's name. Historians note that no records support the existence of a traitor; the tale is suspected to have been invented to hide the failures of the military and political leaders in the defeat by the Swedish forces on 4 January 1647.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "klushund-c04",
        "klushund-c05",
        "klushund-c06"
      ]
    }
