  "claims": [
    {
      "id": "klushund-c01",
      "source_id": "klushund-s1",
      "quote": "The Klushund (Alemannic German for \"dog of Klaus\") is a mythical demonic dog from Vorarlberg, Austria. It is described as a giant black hound with glowing, plate-sized eyes, which haunts in dark forest around the Rhine Valley. Encountering it brings illness and misfortune.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Klushund (bahasa Jerman Alemannik, \"anjing Klaus\") adalah anjing setan mitologis dari Vorarlberg, Austria: anjing hitam raksasa bermata menyala sebesar piring yang menghantui hutan gelap sekitar Lembah Rhein (Rhine Valley); bertemu dengannya membawa penyakit dan kemalangan.",
        "en": "The Klushund (Alemannic German, 'dog of Klaus') is a mythical demonic dog from Vorarlberg, Austria: a giant black hound with glowing plate-sized eyes haunting the dark forest around the Rhine Valley; meeting it brings illness and misfortune."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c02",
      "source_id": "klushund-s1",
      "quote": "According to one of the most common legends, the entity originates from a human traitor who betrayed the city of Bregenz to the Swedish army under the General of Carl Gustaf Wrangel during the Thirty Year's war. Wrangel promised a local man a treasure and in return he showed them a secret passage through the fortified checkpoint in Lochau called Klause.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda paling umum, makhluk ini berasal dari pengkhianat yang menyerahkan kota Bregenz kepada tentara Swedia (Swedish) di bawah Jenderal Carl Gustaf Wrangel dalam Perang Tiga Puluh Tahun (Thirty Year's war); Wrangel menjanjikan harta, dan orang itu menunjukkan jalan rahasia melewati pos benteng Klause di Lochau.",
        "en": "In one of the commonest legends, the being stems from a traitor who betrayed Bregenz to the Swedish army under General Carl Gustaf Wrangel in the Thirty Years' War; Wrangel promised him treasure, and he showed them a secret way past the fortified Klause post at Lochau."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c03",
      "source_id": "klushund-s2",
      "quote": "Wrangel habe dem Verräter für seine Tat das goldene Kegelspiel auf dem Schloss Hohenbregenz versprochen. Der Sage nach muss der Verräter für seine Freveltat nun ewig als großer schwarzer Hund, meist beschrieben mit tellergroßen leuchtenden Augen, auf der ehemaligen Römerstraße am Bergrand von Norden nach Süden durchs Land laufen.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wrangel menjanjikan kepada pengkhianat itu permainan boling emas di Kastel Hohenbregenz; menurut legenda, sebagai hukuman ia harus berlari selamanya sebagai anjing hitam besar bermata menyala sebesar piring di bekas jalan Romawi di kaki gunung dari utara ke selatan.",
        "en": "Wrangel promised the traitor the golden skittles set at Hohenbregenz Castle; per the legend he must forever run as a great black dog with glowing plate-sized eyes along the former Roman road at the mountain edge from north to south."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c04",
      "source_id": "klushund-s2",
      "quote": "Seine bevorzugten Umgangsgebiete sind das Gebiet von Bregenz und vor allem der so genannte Kluser Wald, ein einst sehr finsteres und daher gefürchtetes Waldgebiet zwischen den Gemeinden Götzis und Klaus.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Wilayah berkeliaran kesukaannya adalah sekitar Bregenz dan terutama Kluser Wald, hutan yang dulu sangat gelap dan ditakuti di antara Götzis dan Klaus.",
        "en": "Its favourite haunts are the Bregenz area and above all the Kluser Wald, a once very dark and feared forest between Götzis and Klaus."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c05",
      "source_id": "klushund-s1",
      "quote": "The existence of a traitor as depicted in the legend is historically unsupported by documents and records, leading historians to suspect the tale was fabricated to cover up the military and political leadership's own failures in the defeat against the Swedish forces.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Keberadaan pengkhianat seperti dalam legenda tidak didukung dokumen sejarah, sehingga sejarawan menduga kisah itu dibuat untuk menutupi kegagalan pimpinan militer dan politik sendiri dalam kekalahan melawan pasukan Swedia (Swedish).",
        "en": "No documents or records support the traitor of the legend, so historians suspect the tale was made up to hide the military and political leaders' own failures in the defeat by the Swedish forces."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c06",
      "source_id": "klushund-s2",
      "quote": "Beim Angriff der Schweden am 4. Januar 1647 auf Bregenz wurden die Befestigungswerke teilweise umgangen, waren schlecht besetzt bzw. die Motivation der Verteidiger zum Kampf gering und diese waren auch schlecht ausgerüstet.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam serangan Swedia ke Bregenz pada 4 Januari (Januar) 1647, benteng sebagian dikelilingi, dijaga sedikit orang, dan para pembela kurang semangat serta buruk perlengkapannya.",
        "en": "In the Swedish attack on Bregenz on 4 January (Januar) 1647 the fortifications were partly bypassed, thinly manned, and the defenders poorly motivated and equipped."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c07",
      "source_id": "klushund-s3",
      "quote": "Namnet syftar på ett trångt ställe mellan en bergvägg och en sjö (tyska Klause eller Kluse) vid förorten Lochau, där Wrangel lyckades gå förbi sina motståndare.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "etymology",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Swedia, nama itu merujuk celah sempit antara tebing dan danau (Jerman: Klause atau Kluse) di Lochau, tempat Wrangel berhasil melewati lawannya.",
        "en": "The Swedish article says the name refers to a narrow pass between a cliff and a lake (German Klause or Kluse) at Lochau, where Wrangel got past his opponents."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c08",
      "source_id": "klushund-s3",
      "quote": "Enligt sägnen hade Wrangel fått tag i ett gyllene kägelspel som fanns i palatset (slottet) i Hohenems. Han erbjöd enligt berättelsen spelet till förrädaren.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut artikel bahasa Swedia, Wrangel memperoleh permainan boling emas dari istana di Hohenems dan menawarkannya kepada pengkhianat.",
        "en": "Per the Swedish article, Wrangel had obtained a golden skittles set from the palace at Hohenems and offered it to the traitor."
      }
    },
    {
      "id": "klushund-c09",
      "source_id": "klushund-s2",
      "quote": "Eine Begegnung mit dem bedrohlichen Untier bringt in den Sagen Krankheit, Leid oder Plage.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Dalam legenda, bertemu dengan binatang mengancam itu membawa penyakit, penderitaan, atau wabah.",
        "en": "In the legends, meeting the menacing beast brings sickness, suffering or plague."
      }
    }

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Ksatria Ålleberg adalah arwah para ksatria dalam legenda Swedia yang tidur di dalam gunung Ålleberg dekat Falköping, menunggu perang baru untuk bangkit menyelamatkan negeri.",
    "en": "The Knights of Ålleberg are ghostly knights of Swedish legend who sleep inside the mountain Ålleberg near Falköping, waiting for a new war to wake them to save the country.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "knights-of-alleberg-c01",
      "knights-of-alleberg-c03"
    ]
  },
  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Menurut legenda Swedia, Ksatria Ålleberg adalah arwah dua belas ksatria yang gugur dalam Pertempuran Åsle tahun 1389, di dalam atau sekitar Falköping, antara pasukan Raja Albert dari Swedia dan Margaret I dari Denmark. Legenda ini contoh motif \"raja yang tidur di dalam gunung\" dalam cerita rakyat.",
      "en": "According to Swedish legend, the Knights of Ålleberg are the ghosts of twelve knights who fell at the Battle of Åsle in 1389, fought in or near Falköping between the forces of King Albert of Sweden and Margaret I of Denmark. The legend is an instance of the folklore motif of the king asleep in the mountain.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knights-of-alleberg-c01",
        "knights-of-alleberg-c02"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Arwah-arwah itu terkurung di dalam gunung Ålleberg, menunggu perang baru yang akan membangunkan mereka untuk berjuang menyelamatkan negeri; gunung itu juga dipercaya dihuni troll. Artikel Swedia mencatat bahwa jumlah penunggang dalam legenda disebut \"ribuan demi ribuan\", dan angka dua belas berasal dari \"dua belas penunggang emas\" yang konon bergabung dengan pasukan Nordik dalam pertempuran Åsle.",
      "en": "The ghosts are shut inside the mountain Ålleberg, waiting for a new war to wake them so they can fight for the country; the mountain is also believed to house trolls. The Swedish article notes that legends put the riders at 'thousands upon thousands', and that the figure twelve comes from the 'twelve golden riders' said to have joined the Nordic army at Åsle.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knights-of-alleberg-c03",
        "knights-of-alleberg-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Versi paling umum menceritakan seorang petani yang membawa muatan gandum ke pasar Falköping. Seorang lelaki asing membeli semua muatannya dan membawanya ke sebuah aula di dalam gunung, tempat para ksatria tidur berbaju zirah lengkap. Petani itu tak sengaja membenturkan tali kekang hingga berdenting; para penunggang terbangun dan bertanya apakah sudah tiba waktunya, tetapi lelaki itu meyakinkan mereka untuk tidur lagi. Pada pertengahan abad ke-19 Horace Marryat juga mencatat salah satu versi legenda yang beredar di Falköping.",
      "en": "The commonest version tells of a farmer taking a load of grain to market in Falköping. A stranger bought the whole load and led him into a hall within the mountain, where the knights lay asleep in full armour. The farmer accidentally knocked a bridle so that it jingled; the riders woke and asked whether it was time, but the stranger assured them they could sleep on. In the mid-19th century Horace Marryat also wrote down one of the versions told in Falköping.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knights-of-alleberg-c04",

      ]

      "source_id": "knights-of-alleberg-s1",
      "quote": "According to Swedish legend, the Knights of Ålleberg are ghosts of twelve knights who died at the Battle of Åsle in a 1389. The legend is an example of the king asleep in mountain motif in folklore.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Menurut legenda Swedia (Swedish), Ksatria Ålleberg (Knights of Ålleberg) adalah arwah dua belas ksatria yang gugur dalam Pertempuran Åsle (Battle of Åsle) tahun 1389; legenda ini contoh motif raja yang tidur di dalam gunung.",
        "en": "According to Swedish legend, the Knights of Ålleberg are the ghosts of twelve knights killed at the Battle of Åsle in 1389; the legend exemplifies the king-asleep-in-the-mountain motif."

    },
    {
      "id": "knights-of-alleberg-c02",
      "source_id": "knights-of-alleberg-s1",
      "quote": "The legend concerns knights who died at the Battle of Åsle, sometimes known as the Battle of Falköping, that took place in or around Falköping on 24 February (or August) 1389 between the forces of Albert, King of Sweden and Margaret I of Denmark.",
      "locator": null,
      "context": "historical-record",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Pertempuran Åsle, juga disebut Pertempuran Falköping, terjadi di atau sekitar Falköping pada 24 Februari (atau Agustus) 1389 antara pasukan Albert, Raja Swedia (Sweden), dan Margaret I dari Denmark (Denmark).",
        "en": "The Battle of Åsle, also called the Battle of Falköping, took place in or near Falköping on 24 February (or August) 1389 between the forces of Albert, King of Sweden, and Margaret I of Denmark."

      "id": "knights-of-alleberg-c03",
      "source_id": "knights-of-alleberg-s1",

        "en": "The story has the ghosts trapped in Ålleberg mountain, awaiting a new war to wake them to fight for the country; the mountain is also thought to host trolls."

      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {
        "id": "Legenda paling umum menceritakan petani yang membawa muatan gandum ke pasar Falköping; seorang lelaki minta membeli semua muatannya, dan petani itu mengikutinya ke aula di dalam gunung, tempat para ksatria tidur berbaju zirah lengkap.",

      "quote": "Ållebergs ryttare är den mest kända av sägnerna kring Ålleberg nära Falköping på Falbygden i Västergötland.",

      "statement": {

      "locator": null,
      "context": "traditional-belief",
      "statement": {

        "en": "Legends put the riders at 'thousands upon thousands'; the number twelve relates to the 'twelve golden riders' who joined the Nordic (nordiska) army when Queen Margareta clashed with Albrekt of Mecklenburg at Åsle in 1389."

  "short_description": {
    "id": "Knochenfund von Seweckenberge adalah \"unicorn fosil\" yang direkonstruksi pada akhir abad ke-17 dan awal abad ke-18 dari tulang-tulang zaman Pleistosen yang ditemukan pada 1663 di dekat Quedlinburg, Jerman.",
    "en": "The Seweckenberge bone find is a 'fossil unicorn' reconstructed in the late 17th and early 18th centuries from Pleistocene bones found in 1663 near Quedlinburg, Germany.",
    "claim_ids": [
      "knochenfund-von-seweckenberge-c01",

  "long_description": [
    {
      "id": "Temuan tulang Seweckenberge merujuk pada dugaan rekonstruksi seekor unicorn fosil pada akhir abad ke-17 atau awal abad ke-18. Pada 1663 para pekerja tambang gipsum dan batu kapur di perbukitan Seweckenberge, tenggara Quedlinburg di Sachsen-Anhalt, menemukan tulang-tulang mamalia besar dari zaman Pleistosen yang belum memfosil, termasuk tengkorak bertanduk, rusuk, tulang belakang, tulang belikat, dan tulang kaki.",
      "en": "The Seweckenberge bone find refers to the supposed reconstruction of a fossil unicorn in the late 17th or early 18th century. In 1663 workers quarrying gypsum and limestone in the Seweckenberge hills south-east of Quedlinburg in Saxony-Anhalt uncovered unfossilised Pleistocene bones of large mammals, including a skull with a horn, ribs, spine, shoulder blades and leg bones.",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knochenfund-von-seweckenberge-c01",
        "knochenfund-von-seweckenberge-c02",
        "knochenfund-von-seweckenberge-c03"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Otto von Guericke kemudian menulis bahwa di gunung Seveckenberg ditemukan kerangka unicorn dengan kepala terangkat dan tanduk panjang setebal paha manusia di dahinya; kerangka itu dipecahkan karena ketidaktahuan, lalu kepala, tanduk, dan sebagian tulangnya diserahkan kepada kepala biara perempuan Quedlinburg. Gambar \"unicorn fosil\" dengan hanya dua kaki depan kemudian diterbitkan oleh Michael Bernhard Valentini (1704) dan dalam Protogaea karya Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz (1749).",
      "en": "Otto von Guericke later wrote that a unicorn skeleton had been found in the Seveckenberg hill, with a raised head and a long horn as thick as a human thigh on its forehead; it was broken up through ignorance, and the head, horn and some bones were given to the princess-abbess of Quedlinburg. Pictures of a 'fossil unicorn' with only two forelegs were later published by Michael Bernhard Valentini (1704) and in Gottfried Wilhelm Leibniz's Protogaea (1749).",
      "claim_ids": [
        "knochenfund-von-seweckenberge-c04",
        "knochenfund-von-seweckenberge-c05",
        "knochenfund-von-seweckenberge-c06"
      ]
    },
    {
      "id": "Berdasarkan gambar-gambar itu, tulangnya dapat dikenali sebagai milik beberapa hewan berbeda: badak berbulu atau hewan berkuku lain, mamut berbulu, dan narwal masa kini sebagai \"tanduk\"-nya. Rekonstruksi ini menjadi ikon kriptozoologi dan paleontologi awal; banyak penulis kemudian keliru menyebut Guericke sebagai penemu dan perakitnya, padahal sebenarnya ia tidak terlibat.",
      "en": "From those pictures the bones can be assigned to several different animals: a woolly rhinoceros or other hoofed animal, the woolly mammoth, and a modern narwhal as the 'horn'. The reconstruction became an icon of cryptozoology and early palaeontology; many later writers wrongly named Guericke as its finder and assembler, though he in fact took no part.",

      "locator": null,

      "statement": {

        "en": "In the Seweckenberge hills south-east of Quedlinburg in Saxony-Anhalt (Germany), limestone and gypsum were quarried from the Middle Ages; in 1663 workers found unfossilised Pleistocene (Pleistozän) bones of large mammals in a cavity."

        "en": "The bones included a skull with the horn, ribs, spine, shoulder blades and leg bones; from the published 'reconstructions' they can be assigned to woolly rhinoceros or other ungulates, the woolly mammoth, and a modern narwhal as donor of the 'horn'."
